# Project Overview

- **Purpose:** TranSync — Filipino public transportation platform (Jeepney / UV Express / Bus) covering ride boarding, driver dispatch queues, operator/association management, and admin-driven document verification with fare discounts.
- **Context:** Operates in Pampanga, Philippines. Route planner geocodes via Nominatim scoped to "Pampanga, Philippines".
- **Tech Stack:**
  - Backend: PHP 8 + MySQLi, no framework, stateless REST API
  - Mobile: React Native + Expo 54 + Expo Router 6 (TypeScript)
  - Admin: React 19 + Vite 7 + Tailwind 4 (JavaScript)
  - DB: MySQL, database `transync`, host `localhost`, user `root`, no password
- **Architecture:** Three independent apps sharing one MySQL backend. No shared package/lib. The Vite admin panel uses a proxy to avoid CORS (`/api` → XAMPP).

---

# Directory Map

```
TranSync-App/
├── transync-backend/
│   ├── config/db.php              ← Single DB connection (mysqli), included in every endpoint
│   ├── api/{domain}/{file}.php    ← All REST endpoints (70+ files, growing)
│   └── uploads/{commuters,operators}/  ← Uploaded document files
├── TranSync/TranSync-mobile/
│   └── app/
│       ├── _layout.tsx            ← Root layout: AsyncStorage auth redirect
│       ├── (auth)/                ← Login/register screens
│       ├── (main)/                ← Commuter tab screens
│       ├── (driver)/              ← Driver screens
│       ├── (operator)/            ← Operator tab screens
│       └── (association)/         ← Association tab screens
└── admin_panel/transync-admin/
    └── src/
        ├── App.jsx                ← Routes + PrivateRoute guard
        ├── config/admin_api.js    ← API_BASE = "/api"
        ├── pages/                 ← Login, Overview, UserManagement, VerifyUsers, TerminalManagement
        └── services/              ← authService, systemService, verifyService
```

---

# Roles & Access

| Role | Login table | Identifies by | Mobile route | Admin access |
|------|------------|---------------|--------------|-------------|
| Commuter | `commuters` | `email` | `/(main)/home` | None |
| Driver | `drivers` LEFT JOIN `operators` | `email` | `/(driver)/dashboard` | None |
| Operator | `operators` | `email` | `/(operator)/dashboard` | None |
| Association | `associations` | `contact_email` | `/(association)/dashboard` | None |
| Admin | `admins` | `email` | Web only | Full panel |

---

# Core Flows

## 1. Login (`login_mobile.php`)
Sequential table search — **first match wins**:
1. Query `commuters WHERE email = ?` → check `password_verify`
2. Query `drivers JOIN operators WHERE email = ?` → check `password_verify`
3. Query `operators WHERE email = ?` → check `password_verify`
4. Query `associations WHERE contact_email = ?` → check `password_verify`
5. If none match → 401

**Login gate rules:**
- Commuter: `status = inactive` AND `rejected_at IS NULL` → blocked. If `rejected_at` is set (rejected but can resubmit): category overridden to `default`, `is_verified` forced to `0` in response.
- Driver: must have `vehicle_id` assigned. Missing vehicle → 403. Driver accounts are always created by an operator with `operator_id` set immediately (no self-registration path), so this is the only driver login gate.
- Operator: `status = inactive` → blocked.
- Association: `status = pending` → blocked. `status = inactive` → blocked.

**Session storage:** Mobile stores `user` (JSON) + `role` (string) in AsyncStorage. Admin stores `admin` JSON in localStorage. `login_mobile.php` also issues a Bearer token via `createSession()` (`config/auth.php`, backed by a real `sessions` table with `requireAuth()`/`destroySession()` helpers) — but almost no endpoint calls `requireAuth()` yet, and the mobile client never stores or sends the token. Functionally the app still trusts `commuter_id`/`driver_id`/etc. straight from the request body (see Auth security below); the session-token plumbing exists but isn't wired into the client or enforced server-side outside a couple of endpoints.

## 2. Registration & Verification
1. User registers via mobile → PHP inserts into relevant table with `is_verified = 0`, `status = active`
2. File upload (id_photo/cpc_document) saved to `uploads/{commuters|operators}/` with `time() . uniqid()` filename
3. Admin sees pending users in VerifyUsers page (`GET /api/auth/verify.php?type=commuter|operator|association`)
4. Admin approves → `is_verified = 1, status = active, rejected_at = NULL`
5. Admin rejects → `is_verified = 0, status = inactive, rejected_at = NOW()`
6. After 48h cooldown, user can resubmit (`/api/auth/resubmit.php`) → resets to `status = active, is_verified = 0, rejected_at = NULL`
7. **The commuter side of steps 4–6 now reflects live, without a re-login** — `src/context/UserContext.tsx` (`UserProvider`, mounted in `(main)/_layout.tsx` alongside `BookingsProvider`) is the one shared source of the logged-in commuter's own `is_verified`/`status`/`category`/`rejected_at`, read by `home.tsx`, `profile.tsx`, and `book.tsx`'s discount preview instead of each independently reading the AsyncStorage `user` blob cached at login time (the original gap: an admin approving/rejecting a commuter mid-session was invisible everywhere in the app until the commuter logged out and back in). `refreshUser()` re-fetches from `commuter/get_profile.php` and both updates in-memory state **and** persists the merge back to AsyncStorage (so a screen that reads AsyncStorage directly on next mount also sees it) — called on tab focus (`home.tsx`, `profile.tsx`, `book.tsx`) plus an 18s poll while `home.tsx`/`book.tsx` stay open (same cadence as Core Flow 9a's booking-status polls), and from `home.tsx`'s pull-to-refresh. `home.tsx`'s post-resubmit UI update now calls `refreshUser()` (re-fetching the real server state) instead of client-side guessing the new fields.

**Pending query logic:**
- Commuters: `is_verified=0 AND rejected_at IS NULL AND (category != 'default' OR discount_id_url IS NOT NULL)`
- Operators: `is_verified=0 AND rejected_at IS NULL`
- Associations: `status = pending`

## 2a. Automated ID Verification — Student / PWD / Senior Citizen (OCR + face-match + liveness)
A shared, category-agnostic verification pipeline distinct from the manual admin-review path above — used by Student registration/renewal, PWD registration/renewal, and Senior Citizen registration + PWD/Senior post-expiry category-switching. All captures are **camera-only** (`expo-camera`'s `CameraView`, never gallery upload — deliberate anti-forgery policy) across every consumer.

- **OCR**: `config/student_verification.php`'s `runTesseractOcr()` — local Tesseract binary (`tesseract`), no API key, free, offline. Soft-skips (never blocks) if the binary isn't installed. Runs two passes (PSM 6 + PSM 11) and merges output, since ID cards are dense/scattered text a single page-segmentation mode handles poorly. Name/school matching is fuzzy (1-char Levenshtein tolerance for 4+-char words, via `fuzzyNameMatch()`/`fuzzySchoolMatch()`); ID-number matching (`ocrContainsIdNumber()`) is deliberately **exact-only** — it gates duplicate-account prevention, so tolerating a misread digit would defeat that check.
- **Face-match + liveness**: `config/rekognition.php` — AWS Rekognition (`compareFaces`/`detectFaces`), **demo-mode by default** (no AWS keys configured → synthetic-but-file-existence-checked results, matching this codebase's GCash/Maya "(Demo)" convention). `FACE_MATCH_MIN_SIMILARITY=80.0` (below this: not confident enough for auto-pass), `FACE_MATCH_HARD_REJECT_BELOW=50.0` (below this: confident impersonation, always a hard block, never softened to admin review). Liveness (`checkPoseChallenge()` in `config/student_verification.php`) is a **pose-challenge**, not presentation-attack detection — verifies three captured stills (center/left/right, one continuous camera session with a 3-2-1 countdown per pose) show one real face genuinely turning on command (`POSE_MIN_YAW_DEGREES=15.0`, opposite-direction check on the two turn photos). A failed liveness check is always an immediate, unambiguous retake — never an "uncertain" outcome.
- **Storage**: one table, `commuter_id_verifications` (migration 038, widened 039–042 — see DB Schema section) — `category` (`student`|`disabled`|`elderly`) determines which columns are populated; Student's `school_name`/`academic_year`/`student_id_number`/`id_type`, Senior's `birthdate`/`date_issued`, and PWD's `validity_date` are mutually exclusive on the same row, while `id_number`/`issuing_lgu` are shared between PWD and Senior.
- **Five consumer endpoints**, each its own outcome model:
  1. **Student** registration (`auth/commuter_register_student.php`) / renewal (`auth/commuter_renew_student.php`) — binary-ish: most check failures set a soft `needsReview` flag (account still created, `pending_review`); only liveness failure and an unconfirmable academic year with no Enrollment Form captured are immediate hard fails (nothing created). Academic-period rules (K-12 vs College validity windows, expiry) live in `config/academic_validation.php`, surfaced to the wizard via `auth/current_academic_period.php` and pre-checked via `auth/check_student_id_ocr.php` right after ID capture.
  2. **PWD** registration (`auth/commuter_register_pwd.php`) / renewal (`auth/commuter_renew_pwd.php`) — the 4-outcome classifier (`classifySwitchVerification('disabled', ...)`), same as category-switch/Senior registration below. Unlike Senior Citizen, **PWD is renewable**: the physical ID's own printed Validity Date becomes this row's `expires_at` (see "PWD renewability" below), so a lapsed PWD verification later downgrades the discount the same way Student's academic-period expiry already does — `auth/commuter_renew_pwd.php` is PWD's own equivalent of `commuter_renew_student.php`, just judged by the 4-outcome classifier instead of a binary pass/fail (PWD registration itself is 4-outcome, not binary).
  3. **Category switch** (`auth/commuter_switch_category.php`) — an already-logged-in commuter switching to `disabled`/`elderly` once their *current* category's verification has expired (server-side gated on a `commuter_id_verifications` row with `verification_status='verified' AND expires_at < NOW()` — Senior never writes `expires_at`, so this gate only ever passes for a lapsed Student or a lapsed PWD). Uses the 4-outcome classifier below. Switching to `default` skips this pipeline entirely (no verification needed to drop a discount).
  4. **Senior Citizen registration** (`auth/commuter_register_senior.php`) — the same 4-outcome classifier as category-switch (`classifySwitchVerification('elderly', ...)`), reused verbatim rather than duplicated, but **creates** a new account instead of updating an existing one, and has no "current category expired" gate to pass (there's no existing account yet). Senior Citizen verification never expires — there's no Senior renewal endpoint, unlike Student/PWD.
- **The 4-outcome classifier** (`classifySwitchVerification()` in `config/discount_switch_validation.php`) — `verified` (auto-approve immediately) / `uncertain` / `rejected` (both fall through to the admin's existing `VerifyUsers` queue — `uncertain`/`rejected` are treated identically by every calling endpoint, the distinction is audit-trail-only) / `blocked` (immediate hard reject, **no account created / no switch applied**). Three findings cause `blocked`: (a) a face-match similarity confidently below `FACE_MATCH_HARD_REJECT_BELOW` — someone else's ID plus the applicant's own face; (b) **Senior Citizen only**, an OCR-confirmed birthdate showing age < 60 — checked via `checkSeniorAge()`, which tries the typed birthdate against 6 common printed date formats on the OCR'd document; (c) **PWD only**, an OCR-confirmed Validity Date already in the past — checked via `checkPwdValidity()` (same 6-candidate-format approach as `checkSeniorAge()`). An *unconfirmable* birthdate/Validity Date (not found on the document at all) is `uncertain` instead for both, falling through to admin review like any other inconclusive check — the hard block only fires on a **confirmed** under-60 reading or a **confirmed**-expired Validity Date. This is a deliberate asymmetry from every other soft check in this pipeline: discount eligibility (age ≥ 60, or an unexpired PWD ID) is a strict requirement, not a judgment call worth an admin's time, the same way confident impersonation isn't.
- **PWD renewability**: unlike every other category in this pipeline, PWD's verification **expires** — `expires_at` is written as `"{validity_date} 23:59:59"` (same-day-inclusive: the validity date itself still counts as valid, only the day after is expired, matching the existing `expires_at < NOW()` comparison convention used everywhere else). `login_mobile.php`'s `pwdVerificationExpired()` and `commuter/get_profile.php`'s parallel `category === "disabled"` block (both deliberately separate functions/blocks from Student's own `studentVerificationExpired()`, not a merged generic one — that field name is read by `home.tsx`'s Student-specific expired banner) downgrade an expired PWD commuter to `category='default'`/`is_verified=0` on login/profile-fetch, exactly like Student's own academic-period expiry. `profile.tsx`'s "Renew Verification" button branches by `raw_category` to route to `pwd-verification.tsx` (PWD) or `student-verification.tsx` (Student/default) accordingly. Because `commuter_switch_category.php`'s "current category expired" gate is already fully generic (keyed off whatever category the commuter currently holds), an expired PWD commuter can also switch to `default`/`elderly` via that same existing flow with **no additional code** — it started working the moment PWD began writing a real `expires_at`.
- **Senior Citizen ID fields**: Last/First/Middle Name (middle optional), Senior Citizen ID Number, Issuing LGU (free text, not OCR-cross-checked — no reliable machine-normalizable format across differently-designed OSCA cards), Date Issued, Birthdate. Date Issued/Birthdate (migration 041) exist only for this category.
- **PWD ID fields**: Last/First/Middle Name (middle optional), PWD ID Number, Issuing LGU (free text, same as Senior, not OCR-cross-checked), Validity Date (`validity_date`, migration 042 — PWD-only, OCR-cross-checked via `checkPwdValidity()` and is the source of this row's `expires_at`). No birthdate is collected for PWD.
- **ID-number uniqueness**: `commuter_id_verifications.id_number` has **no DB-level UNIQUE constraint** (same rationale as Student's `student_id_number` — a legitimate renewal/resubmission needs to reinsert the same real number as a new row). Enforced at the application level, `WHERE id_number = ? [AND commuter_id != ?]`. **PWD and Senior Citizen ID numbers share this one column and uniqueness namespace** — a number already used as a PWD ID can't be reused as a Senior Citizen ID and vice versa. This is deliberate (matches the pre-existing category-switch behavior); don't scope it to `category='elderly'` only without the same conversation that decided this.
- **Admin review**: any outcome short of a clean pass lands in the exact same `VerifyUsers.jsx` queue every manually-reviewed disabled/elderly registration has always used — no separate UI needed. `auth/verify.php`'s commuter query is category-agnostic; it just `LEFT JOIN`s the commuter's most-recent `commuter_id_verifications` row and computes `claimed_name`/`claimed_id_number` server-side so the frontend doesn't need to know which underlying column (`student_id_number` vs the generic `id_number`) applies.

## 2b. Commuter Settings & Verification Status
Two previously-dead menu items on `profile.tsx` (`onPress={() => {}}` stubs) — reachable from Profile → "Verification Status" and Profile → "Settings". Profile itself is unchanged by any of this — same avatar/badge/info card/action list/Log Out it always had.

- **Verification Status** (`(main)/verification-status.tsx`) — read-only, no new fetch of its own: pure presentation over fields `UserContext` already loads live (Core Flow 2 point 7). Shows a status badge (Verified/Pending/Rejected/Expired), category, rejection date + 48h resubmission cooldown note when applicable, discount expiration date when set.
- **Settings** (`(main)/settings.tsx`), **rewritten into five sections** (superseding an earlier four-section version from the same day — Notifications and Delete Account are new/changed, Privacy & Consent was folded out since it wasn't part of the revised spec):
  1. **Account Information** — one "Edit Personal Info" modal covering Name (First/Middle/Last/Suffix — freely editable; the discount pipeline checks `category`/`is_verified` at fare time, never a live name re-match against `commuter_id_verifications`, so nothing breaks) + Email + Phone, all under one password confirmation → `commuter/update_contact.php` (extended to accept the four name fields alongside its original email/phone handling, recomputing `name` by the same concatenation `commuter_register.php` uses). Format + cross-table duplicate checks via `isValidEmailFormat()`/`emailAlreadyRegistered()`/`phoneAlreadyRegistered()` in `config/account_validation.php`, all excluding the commuter's own row.
  2. **Notifications** — four local-only toggles (`src/state/settingsPrefs.ts`, AsyncStorage — no backend, this app has no push infra, see Known Issues), each controlling a real piece of existing UI rather than a fake no-op switch: Booking Confirmation / Boarding & Trip Reminders / Cancellation & Refund Updates filter Home's Recent Activity feed by `BookingActivityKind` (`confirmed`/`completed`/`cancelled` respectively — `completed` is the closest existing kind to a trip-lifecycle reminder, since this app's activity feed has no separate boarding-reminder entry type). The fourth, Verification Status Updates, gates a genuinely new signal: `UserContext.refreshUser()` now diffs the previously-fetched `is_verified`/`rejected_at` against each new fetch and, when the preference is on and something actually changed, sets `verificationStatusAlert` (a short message) — `home.tsx` renders it as a dismissible banner above the greeting. The very first fetch after app open never triggers this (no prior value to diff against yet), only a genuine change detected on a later poll/focus-refetch does.
  3. **Privacy & Security** — Change Password (`commuter/change_password.php`, current-password-gated, 6+ char minimum, same rule as every other password-setting path in this app), plus two static informational rows: an "Account Security" reassurance blurb, and an honest "Active Sessions" note that per-device session management isn't available (`config/auth.php`'s `sessions` table/`requireAuth()` exist server-side but aren't wired into the mobile client at all — see Known Issues — so there's nothing real to list or revoke yet).
  4. **Terms & Policies** — three read-only viewers reusing existing content rather than a third copy: `COMMUTER_TERMS`/`PRIVACY_POLICY` (`src/constants/registrationTerms.ts`, already shown at registration) and a new `CANCELLATION_POLICY` constant in the same file, copied verbatim from `PassengerForm.tsx`'s own booking-time Cancellation Policy sheet (that file keeps its own separate inline copy, untouched — no shared source of truth was introduced there, just duplicated content copied once into an exported constant). All three render via `TermsGate.tsx`'s `PolicySheet`/`TcSection`, exported for this reuse (previously local/unexported) — `PassengerForm.tsx` has its own separate, still-local `PolicySheet` copy, deliberately left alone.
  5. **Delete Account** — **permanent soft-delete**, replacing the four-section version's reversible Deactivation in this UI. `commuter/delete_account.php` (password-gated) anonymizes `name`/`first_name`/`middle_name`/`last_name`/`suffix`/`date_of_birth`/`email`/`phone`/`discount_id_url` to placeholders and sets `commuters.deleted_at` (migration 046) — `email`/`phone` become per-id-unique placeholders (`deleted_{id}@transync.invalid`/`deleted{id}`), which as a side effect immediately frees the original email for a fresh registration (verified live). **Deliberately does not touch** `wallets`, `reservations` (which already store `passenger_name` independently at booking time — confirmed via `operator_rebook.php`'s own INSERT — so operator/driver manifests are unaffected), `wallet_transactions`, `commuter_id_verifications`, or `consent_acceptances` — a commuter row is referenced by too many other tables for a true `DELETE` to be safe, and this app never destroys historical financial/audit data. `deactivated_at`/`deactivate_account.php`/`login_mobile.php`'s reactivate-on-login branch (migration 045, same day) are left in place, dormant — no longer called by any current UI, per this codebase's deprecate-in-place convention.
  6. **Discount Eligibility** — a "Standard" (`category='default'`) commuter with no submission already in flight gets three menu rows (Apply for Student/PWD/Senior Citizen Discount) routing straight into the same wizards Core Flow 2a's registration-time flows use (`student-verification`/`pwd-verification?mode=renew`, `senior-verification?mode=apply` — reusing those screens' existing dual-purpose branching rather than a fourth "apply" mode). Gated on `commuter/get_profile.php`'s server-computed `can_apply_category` (`raw_category === 'default' && !has_pending_discount_verification`) — hidden the instant the commuter holds any category or has a review in flight, so it never overlaps with Profile's own "Renew Verification"/"Change Discount Type" actions (Core Flow 2, point 7's `can_switch_category` — a different, narrower feature for switching *away from* an already-expired category, which by construction can never be true at the same time as `can_apply_category` since `commuter_id_verifications.category` has no `'default'` value to match against). **Also surfaced directly on `profile.tsx` itself** (added 2026-09-20, same gate/routes) as a green "Eligible for a discount?" card with three buttons, sitting where the amber "EXPIRED DISCOUNT ACTIONS" card would be for an expired commuter — the two cards are mutually exclusive by the same `can_apply_category`/`can_switch_category` reasoning above, so a commuter only ever sees at most one. This closes a real discoverability gap: the feature already existed under Settings, but a first-time Standard commuter had no way to find it without first opening Settings, since nothing on Profile itself hinted a discount application was possible. **Student and PWD apply/renew (`?mode=renew` only, added 2026-09-20) now also require a Birthday** (shared `registration/DateField`, future dates blocked) — sent as `birthdate` to `commuter_renew_student.php`/`commuter_renew_pwd.php`, validated server-side (valid `Y-m-d`, not in the future, required), and stored on both `commuters.date_of_birth` and the new `commuter_id_verifications.birthdate` row (that column was previously Senior-only, no migration needed). Storage only — no OCR cross-check or pass/fail effect, unlike Senior's age check. Registration-time wizards are unchanged (their draft already carries `date_of_birth`); Senior's own Birthdate is unchanged. Blocked outcomes write nothing, so nothing is stored for them.

## 3. Dispatch Queue (Driver)
1. Driver taps "Join Queue" → `POST /api/queue/join_queue.php`
   - Clears any ghost `waiting` rows for this driver first
   - Counts current `waiting` rows for operator → `queue_position = count + 1`
   - Inserts new row: `status = waiting`
2. `GET /api/queue/queue_position.php` → counts `waiting` rows with `id < current_row_id` → position = ahead + 1
3. Driver taps "Start Trip" → `POST /api/trips/start_trip.php`
   - Updates `dispatch_queue SET status = dispatched WHERE driver_id = ? AND status = waiting`
   - Sets `drivers.is_online = 1, is_full = 0`
4. Driver taps "End Trip" → `POST /api/trips/end_trip.php`
   - Updates `dispatch_queue SET status = completed WHERE driver_id = ? AND status = dispatched`
   - Sets `drivers.is_full = 0`

**Queue position is id-based** (not timestamp): rows are inserted sequentially so `id < current` correctly identifies who's ahead.

## 4. Ride Flow (Commuter boards Jeepney) — **DEPRECATED/DORMANT as of 2026-08-02**
**Jeepney fare payment reverted to traditional/cash, off-app.** `(driver)/driver-map.tsx` no longer displays any boarding QR (removed entirely — it was the *only* way a `jeepney_rides` row could ever be created, since the driver's screen showed `DRIVER_{id}` for the commuter to scan). The commuter-side scanner (`(main)/scan.tsx`) already had zero navigation entry points before this change and remains orphaned. All backend/frontend pieces below are left in place dormant, per this codebase's deprecate-in-place convention — nothing was deleted, but none of it is reachable from the UI anymore:
- `system/verify_qr_board.php`, `trips/request_dropoff.php`, `trips/cancel_dropoff.php`, `trips/end_ride.php`, `trips/active_ride.php`, `trips/latest_ride.php` — still functional for any historical/direct caller, never invoked by the app now.
- The Jeepney fare formula (Core Flow 5) and the commuter/driver wallet debit-credit mechanics it drives are likewise dormant for Jeepney specifically — Bus/UV Express escrow/remittance (Core Flows 9–12) are entirely unaffected, this only concerns Jeepney's old per-ride digital fare.
- `driver-map.tsx`'s other live-hailing remnants (nearby-commuter markers, the "Para Po" drop-off confirmation modal, the ride-notification modal) were deliberately left in place — only the QR was explicitly requested for removal — but are now functionally unreachable too, since no `jeepney_rides` row can ever be created to drive them.
- The Jeepney dispatch queue (Core Flow 3 — join/leave/start/end trip) is **unaffected** and still fully live; only the QR-boarding/fare-payment step (this section) is dormant.

Original flow, preserved for reference:
1. Commuter approaches jeepney → driver scans QR → `POST /api/system/verify_qr_board.php`
   - Creates row in `jeepney_rides` with `status = active`
   - Records `board_lat, board_lng, board_time, discount_type` (from commuter's category + is_verified at boarding time)
2. Commuter taps "Para Po" → `POST /api/trips/request_dropoff.php`
   - Updates `jeepney_rides.status = dropoff_requested`
   - Records `dropoff_lat, dropoff_lng`
3. Driver confirms drop-off → `POST /api/trips/end_ride.php`
   - Accepts `ride_id`, `dropoff_lat`, `dropoff_lng`, `triggered_by`
   - Works on status `active` OR `dropoff_requested`
   - Calculates fare (see Fare Calculation below)
   - Atomic transaction: updates ride → deducts commuter wallet → logs commuter debit → credits driver wallet → logs driver credit
4. `GET /api/trips/active_ride.php?commuter_id=X` → commuter polls for their active ride
5. `GET /api/trips/active_ride.php?driver_id=X` → driver polls for passengers + dropoff requests

## 5. Fare Calculation (`end_ride.php`) — dormant alongside Core Flow 4 (Jeepney fare payment reverted to traditional/cash, off-app)
```
base_fare = ₱13.00 (covers first 4 km)
if distance_km > 4.0:
    fare += (distance_km - 4.0) × ₱1.80/km
if discount_type IN (student, disabled, elderly):
    fare × 0.80
fare = round(fare × 4) / 4   ← rounds to nearest ₱0.25
if wallet_balance < fare:
    fare = round(wallet_balance × 4) / 4  ← never blocks ride, charges max available
```

**discount_type** is captured at boarding (in `verify_qr_board.php`) and stored on the ride record. `end_ride.php` reads it from there — not from the commuter's current category — to prevent mid-ride category changes affecting the fare.

## 6. Route Planner (`route_planner.php`)
- Input: `destination`, `origin` (text or lat/lng coords)
- Geocodes text via Nominatim OSM API (scoped to Pampanga, Philippines)
- Loads all `status = active` routes with JSON waypoints from DB
- Haversine + perpendicular segment distance to match routes to destination
- Thresholds: dest 1500m, origin 2500m, transfer connection 800m
- Output types: `direct` (0 transfers), `transfer` (1 transfer), `nearest` (destination off all routes, fallback)
- Transfer: checks if route A's endpoint is within 800m of route B's start
- Returns top 3 options sorted: direct → nearest → transfer → fastest → cheapest
- Annotates each option with `has_active_drivers` (driver online + location updated in last 2 min)
- Speed estimates: Jeepney 20 km/h, UV Express 35 km/h, Bus 30 km/h

## 7. Nearby Drivers (`nearby_drivers.php`)
- Haversine in SQL, radius 5km, limit configurable (default 5)
- **Only returns Jeepney Op type** — UV Express and Bus are excluded
- Requires `is_online = 1` and non-null lat/lng

## 8. Org Hierarchy (Association ← Operator ← Driver) — per-vehicle-type membership
Driver accounts are **operator-managed, not self-service** — there is no public "Driver" option on the registration role picker. Only the operator↔association hop is a self-service application, and it is now **per vehicle type**: one operator account can simultaneously run a Jeepney fleet under one association and a Bus fleet under a completely different one. `operators.operator_type` (single fixed type) and `operators.association_id` (single FK) and `associations.association_type` are **deprecated in place** — still present in the schema (never dropped, matching this codebase's convention — see `drivers.association_id`), but no code reads or writes them for gating anymore.

1. **Registration is generic but still requires one document** — `auth/operator_register.php` collects name/email/phone/password plus a single required upload (`registration_document`, a valid ID or business permit, stored in `operators.registration_document_url`). No vehicle type, no association, and no *vehicle-type-specific* documents at this step — those are entirely separate from this one generic document and only happen later (point 2). Admin verification of the account (`operators.is_verified`) reviews this document but is otherwise a judgment call independent of any vehicle-type membership — admin approves/rejects the account itself, not a specific fleet type.
2. **Applying to an association happens afterward, per vehicle type** — `(operator)/apply-association.tsx` (reachable from Profile → "My Associations" → "Apply"): pick a vehicle type (Jeepney/UV Express/Bus) → pick any active association (no longer filtered by a matching type, since associations aren't typed) → upload that type's own LTO/LTFRB document checklist (one file per named document — see Core Flow 8a) → submit. `operators/apply_to_association.php` blocks a duplicate `pending`/`approved` application for the *same* vehicle type, but a Jeepney application and a Bus application (even to different associations) coexist freely.
3. **The operator's association for a given vehicle type is derived, not stored** — every endpoint that needs "this operator's association" resolves it live:
   ```sql
   SELECT association_id FROM operator_association_applications
   WHERE operator_id = ? AND vehicle_type = ? AND status = 'approved'
   ```
   scoped to whichever vehicle type is contextually relevant (e.g. `bus_trips/create_trip.php` and the Bus branch of `vehicles.php` always use `vehicle_type='Bus'`). This replaces every old read of `operators.association_id`.
4. Association reviews via `associations/pending_operator_applications.php` (returns `vehicle_type` + the per-type document URLs, **plus the operator's registration-time `registration_document_url`** — the association sees everything the operator has ever submitted, not just the vehicle-type-specific checklist) + `associations/review_operator_application.php`. Approving no longer touches any column on `operators` — the `operator_association_applications` row itself (already carrying `vehicle_type` + `association_id`) is authoritative. Auto-rejecting other pending applications on approval is now scoped to the *same vehicle_type* only (a pending Bus application elsewhere is untouched by approving a Jeepney one). Mobile: `(association)/dashboard.tsx`'s review modal renders a "View Registration Document" button alongside the vehicle-type checklist buttons.
5. **A driver's association is transitive through their own vehicle's type**, not just their operator — derived via `drivers.operator_id → operators (approved application WHERE vehicle_type = driver's vehicle's type) → association`. A driver assigned a Bus vehicle resolves to the operator's *Bus* association, even if that same operator also has an approved Jeepney association elsewhere. The legacy `drivers.association_id` column still exists but is never written. `auth/login_mobile.php`'s `findDriver()` computes this same join at login time and reports it back to the mobile client under the (historically-named, unchanged for compatibility) `operator_type`/`association_id`/`association_name` fields on the driver's `user` object — so `(driver)/dashboard.tsx`'s existing `isBusDriver`/`isUvDriver`/`isJeepney` checks keep working unchanged, now fed a value derived from the driver's own vehicle instead of the operator's deprecated fixed type. The **operator's own** login response still returns a raw (now usually-null) `operator_type` straight from the deprecated column — harmless, since every operator-side mobile screen was migrated off that field (see points 8/10 below).
6. **Association visibility**: `associations/association_members.php` derives members from `operator_association_applications WHERE association_id = ? AND status = 'approved'` (one row per operator, listing every vehicle type approved *at this specific association* as a `vehicle_types` array) — each operator's nested drivers are additionally filtered to only those whose own vehicle type is in that approved set, so an association only ever sees the slice of an operator's fleet it actually approved.
7. **Removal is per vehicle type**: `associations/kick_operator.php` and `operators/leave_association.php` both now require `vehicle_type` and set that one `operator_association_applications` row's `status = 'removed'` (a new terminal state, added migration 022, replacing the old silent `operators.association_id = NULL` reset) — sweeping only that operator's drivers *of that vehicle type* out of their `route_assignments`. Removing a Bus membership never touches the same operator's separate Jeepney membership.
8. **Operator account "on hold" is now vehicle-type-scoped**: `(operator)/vehicles.tsx`'s "Add Vehicle" form is an open 3-way type picker, but each type is only selectable if that specific type has an approved association membership (checked against `operators/association_status.php`'s per-type list) — an operator approved for Jeepney but not yet for Bus can add Jeepney vehicles immediately while Bus stays locked. `(operator)/dashboard.tsx`/`drivers.tsx` keep a coarser "operational if approved for *any* type" gate for their shared (not vehicle-type-specific) actions. Login itself is **never** gated either way — only in-app feature access is.
9. **Dual gate is still order-enforced server-side, per vehicle-type application**: `associations/review_operator_application.php`'s `approve` branch still checks `operators.is_verified` before allowing any vehicle-type approval — an unverified operator's application (whichever vehicle type) can't be approved yet, rejected with `"This operator hasn't been verified by the admin yet — approval isn't available until then."`
10. **Screens that assumed one vehicle type per operator now show a switcher when more than one applies**: `(operator)/trips.tsx`, `fare.tsx` derive their available vehicle kinds live from `operators/association_status.php`'s approved list (not a fixed `operator_type`), defaulting to one and offering a chip-switcher when the operator holds multiple approved types. The association-side equivalents (`(association)/fare.tsx`, `schedules.tsx`) similarly no longer gate on `association_type` — an association's Bus-only sub-features (classes, schedules, seat maps) just show their existing empty states when that association has no Bus routes/operators yet, instead of being hidden behind a type check.
11. **Route creation belongs to the association for every vehicle type, not just Bus — the operator can only assign an already-published route to one of their own drivers.** `routes/save_route.php` requires `association_id` for Jeepney/UV Express/Bus alike (the operator-owned INSERT/UPDATE path is dead — `(operator)` has no route-creation screen at all; `(association)/route-builder.tsx` is the only caller). The operator-side action is `routes/assign_route.php`: validates the driver belongs to the operator and has a vehicle assigned, then that the route's `association_id` has an **approved** `operator_association_applications` row for the operator matching the *driver's own vehicle's type* (and that the route itself is that same type) before writing to `route_assignments`; `action=unassign` clears it. Mobile: `(operator)/drivers.tsx` — each driver card shows its currently-assigned route as a chip (with an inline unassign ✕) or an "Assign Route" button (only once a vehicle is assigned, since the route offered is scoped to that vehicle's type); the picker fetches the operator's approved association for that vehicle type (`operators/association_status.php`) then that association's routes (`routes/get_association_routes.php`), filtered client-side to the matching type and `status==='active'`. A driver's assigned route (if any) is included directly in `drivers/drivers.php`'s GET response (`route_id`/`route_name`, via a `route_assignments`/`routes` LEFT JOIN) so the list doesn't need a separate per-driver fetch.
12. **The live dispatch queue (`dispatch_queue`/`join_queue.php`/`start_trip.php`/`leave_queue.php`) is entirely independent of route *ownership*** — it only ever reads/writes `route_id` (nullable for Jeepney, required for UV Express's FIFO scoping, see the "UV Express dispatch queue" note under Critical Insights), never who created or owns that route. A driver joins the queue, waits, and gets dispatched identically whether their route was just created by the association yesterday or has been running for a year, and regardless of whether that route currently has any scheduled trip/slot at all — the "station wait-and-go" Jeepney/UV flow needed zero changes when route creation moved fully to the association.

### 8a. Vehicle-type document checklist (per Core Flow 8.2)
Each vehicle type has its own LTO/LTFRB paperwork, one upload field per named document (stored as flat nullable columns on `operator_association_applications`, migration 022 — no separate documents table, matching `operators.cpc_document_url`'s existing convention):
- **Jeepney** (4): `doc_lto_cr_url` (must state PUJ or Modern PUV Class 1/2/3), `doc_capacity_spec_url` (certified seating blueprint, 16–30 pax), `doc_engine_cert_url` (Euro-4/5 compliance), `doc_lto_stencil_url` (physical stencil of engine/chassis numbers).
- **UV Express** (4): `doc_lto_cr_url` (must state UV Express Service), `doc_model_proof_url` (approved van/AUV model), `doc_terminal_route_map_url` (approved P2P origin/destination map), `doc_garage_proof_url` (off-road commercial garage slot).
- **Bus** (5): `doc_lto_cr_url` (must state Public Utility Bus/PUB), `doc_speed_limiter_url` (RA 10916 calibration cert), `doc_gps_cert_url` (LTFRB-compliant tracking device), `doc_pami_policy_url` (Passenger Accident Insurance), `doc_fare_matrix_url` (LTFRB distance-based fare table).

Only the subset of columns relevant to the application's own `vehicle_type` gets populated; the rest stay `NULL`. `associations/pending_operator_applications.php` returns all 11 columns per row (prefixed with the admin/association document base URL) so the association reviewer only sees "View X" buttons for the ones that are actually present.

The older `association_applications`/`association_memberships` tables (driver/vehicle applying **directly** to an association, bypassing the operator) are superseded by this model and are no longer written by current code — left in place, unused, rather than dropped, so no historical data was destroyed. `applications/submit_application.php`, `review_application.php`, `pending_applications.php`, `associations/kick_member.php`, and `associations/leave_association.php` (the old driver-level ones) are dormant for the same reason.

### 8b. UV Express Scheduling — Association Template → Operator Activation → Pooled Slot (rewritten)
UV Express scheduling now mirrors Bus's association-template model (Core Flow 12a) at the *ownership* level, but stays queue-based at the *execution* level: the association still publishes one recurring schedule per route (now with the same daily/specific-days recurrence Bus schedules have), but the operator no longer manually creates individual `uv_slots` rows one at a time — they simply **activate** the association's schedule for their own fleet, and a commuter's own chosen booking time is what actually creates (or joins) the slot, pooled across every operator currently active on that route. This replaced an earlier, simpler version of this section (no recurrence, operator manually created each slot) — that model is fully superseded, not layered on top of.

1. **The association's schedule now carries a recurrence, same shape as `bus_schedules`** — `uv_operating_hours` (`association_id, route_id, start_time, end_time, recurrence_type ENUM('daily','weekly') DEFAULT 'daily', weekdays VARCHAR(20) NULL, status`, `UNIQUE route_id`, migration 027, recurrence added migration 028). CRUD unchanged in shape at `associations/uv_operating_hours.php`: POST creates (rejects if the route already has a row — edit via PUT instead), PUT does a full edit (now including `recurrence_type`/`weekdays`, validated exactly like `bus_schedules.php`'s own POST/PUT) or a quick status-only toggle, DELETE removes. A route with no row, or an `inactive` one, has no book-ahead schedule at all. (The live "go online" walk-up flow, `uv_sessions`, used to be a separate, unaffected channel here — it has since been removed from the app entirely, see point 8 below.) **The operating window may wrap past midnight** (`start_time > end_time`, e.g. 23:00 → 02:00 the next day) — UV Express runs 24 hours, so an overnight window is valid, not an error; only a zero-duration window (`start_time === end_time`) is rejected. See Do Not Break rule #54a for every place this wrap must be accounted for.
2. **The operator's job is a single activate/deactivate toggle, not slot creation** — `uv_schedule_activations` (`operator_id, uv_operating_hours_id, status`, `UNIQUE (operator_id, uv_operating_hours_id)`, migration 028) records which operators are currently running a given association schedule; **multiple operators can independently activate the same route's schedule**. `operators/uv_schedule_activation.php`: GET returns the operator's UV-approved association's schedules merged with this operator's own `activation_status` (`active`|`inactive`|`null` if never toggled); POST validates the operator has an approved UV Express membership at the schedule's own association, then upserts the toggle. No vehicle or driver is committed here — UV Express stays entirely queue-based (`dispatch_queue`), unlike Bus's `bus_trips/create_trip.php` which does commit a specific vehicle+driver. Mobile: `(operator)/trips.tsx`'s old "Schedule UV Slot" modal (route+date+time+capacity picker, calling `uv_slots/create_slot.php`) is gone entirely — the existing vehicle-kind chip switcher (Jeepney/UV Express/Bus, already shown whenever the operator holds more than one approved type) is the only switcher; no separate "Schedules" tab was added. Selecting **UV Express** shows a "Your UV Schedules" section (one card per association schedule: route, operating hours, recurrence, Activate/Deactivate button) directly above the existing "Route Queue" panel and upcoming-bookings list, all in one view. Selecting **Bus** is completely unchanged — the trips list + "+ Add" → "Schedule Bus Trip" flow, no read-only schedule display was added there (an earlier version of this feature briefly added a second "Trips vs Schedules" tab layer on top of the vehicle-kind switcher — this was confusing/redundant and was reverted; don't reintroduce it).
3. **A commuter's own chosen time is what creates the actual bookable slot — pooled across every operator active on it, not owned by any one of them.** `uv_slots/resolve_slot.php` (`{route_id, departure_time}`, commuter-triggered) validates the advance-booking window (24h+), that the route has an `active` schedule, that the chosen date's weekday matches the schedule's recurrence (`DAYOFWEEK()` on the commuter's own literal date string — timezone-independent, no DB-clock dance needed since there's no "now" comparison), that the time-of-day falls within the operating window (via `SELECT TIME(...)`, same clock-mismatch-safe pattern as everywhere else), and that **at least one operator has actively activated this schedule** — then finds an existing `uv_slots` row for that exact `(route_id, departure_time)` or creates one with `operator_id = NULL, capacity = NULL` (both columns widened nullable, migration 028). A second commuter booking the same route+time reuses the same row (`create_reservation.php`'s claim treats `capacity IS NULL` as unbounded — never blocks on `seats_booked`). The mobile commuter flow (`(main)/book.tsx`'s UV "Book Ahead" section) still lists already-created upcoming slots (so commuters naturally converge on shared times) via the unchanged `uv_slots/get_available_slots.php`, but now also offers a "Pick Your Own Time" date+time stepper (mirroring the Bus date-picker pattern) that calls `resolve_slot.php` directly — there is no requirement to join a time someone else already picked.
4. **Boarding authorization pools across operators too, since a slot no longer belongs to one** — `reservations/board_reservation.php`'s UV-slot authorization check dropped its `dq.operator_id = s.operator_id` join condition: any driver currently `dispatched` for that `route_id` (regardless of which operator they belong to) can scan and board a pooled slot's passengers. The remittance split still correctly credits *that scanning driver's own operator* (derived fresh from `drivers.operator_id` at scan time, never from the slot), so money always goes to whoever actually did the work. `uv_slots/update_slot_status.php` (operator/driver boarding-lifecycle actions — start_boarding/depart/complete/cancel) got the matching fix: an operator is authorized either by exact legacy ownership (`slot.operator_id` still set and matching) **or** by having an active `uv_schedule_activations` row for that route; a driver is authorized by being currently dispatched for that route, full stop (no operator match required). A legacy operator-created slot (`operator_id` still set, from before this migration) keeps working exactly as before — none of this is a breaking change for old rows, only an *additional* path for new pooled ones. **`reservations/preview_ticket.php`'s own UV-slot authorization query had drifted out of sync with this fix** — it still joined on `dq.operator_id = s.operator_id` alongside `route_id`, found and fixed 2026-08-10. Since a pooled slot's `operator_id` is `NULL`, that join could never match (`NULL` never equals anything in SQL, including another `NULL`), so `preview_ticket.php` silently reported "not for your current trip" for *every* pooled slot scan — and because `(driver)/scan-ticket.tsx` always calls `previewTicket()` before `boardReservation()` and returns immediately on a failed preview (never reaching the boarding call at all), this had been silently blocking **all** pooled UV Express boarding end-to-end, even though `board_reservation.php` itself was correct. Verified fixed via a full live round-trip: a throwaway pooled slot + dispatched driver + reservation now previews successfully and boards successfully, with the correct remittance split. `preview_ticket.php`'s query must be kept in sync with `board_reservation.php`'s any time one of them changes — they're documented as mirroring each other exactly but are two separate copies of the same logic, not a shared function, so this kind of drift can recur.
5. **No-show forfeiture on a pooled slot has no operator to pay** — since capacity is uncapped, nobody held a genuinely scarce seat that couldn't be resold, so `config/sweep.php`'s no-show sweep (see Core Flow 11.4) credits the *system* ledger only for a pooled slot's (`operator_id IS NULL`) forfeited fare, instead of crediting an arbitrary or nonexistent operator. A legacy operator-owned slot's no-show still pays that operator/driver via the normal `creditRemittanceSplit()` split, unchanged.
6. Endpoints touched by nullable `capacity`: `uv_slots/get_available_slots.php` and `get_operator_slots.php` both changed their capacity-comparison `WHERE` clauses to `(capacity IS NULL OR seats_booked < capacity)` and now return `capacity`/`seats_left` as `null` (never coerced to `0`) so the client can tell "uncapped" apart from a real legacy zero-seat row; `get_operator_slots.php` additionally widened its own `WHERE` from a strict `operator_id = ?` match to also include any route the operator has an active schedule activation on (`EXISTS` subquery through `uv_schedule_activations`/`uv_operating_hours`), or pooled slots would never show up in that operator's own list at all.
7. **A pooled slot's commuter-facing visibility tracks activation live, not just at creation** — `get_available_slots.php`'s `WHERE` also requires `s.operator_id IS NOT NULL OR EXISTS (... uv_schedule_activations ... status='active' ...)` (reusing the same `h` LEFT JOIN as the operating-hours columns, so a deactivated/missing `uv_operating_hours` row — `h.id IS NULL` — also falls through to hidden). This was a real gap: a pooled slot created while a schedule was bookable stayed listed/joinable forever afterward, even once every operator deactivated it or the association itself deactivated the schedule — commuters could still see and book a slot the operator had turned off, with no new row or manual step needed to reproduce it. A legacy operator-owned slot (`operator_id` still set, predates the activation model) is exempt from this check — it was never gated by activation, so an unrelated pooled schedule's deactivation must never hide it.
7. `uv_slots/create_slot.php` (the old operator-manual slot-creation endpoint) is now **dormant** — left in place (still requires a real `capacity`, still Do Not Break rule #48-safe for any historical direct caller) but nothing in the mobile UI calls it anymore, matching this codebase's established "deprecate in place" convention (e.g. `vehicles/seed_seats.php`, `operators/service_types.php`).
8. **The live, driver-initiated "go online" walk-up session flow (`uv_sessions`) has been removed from the app.** UV Express drivers never controlled booking availability the way a Bus's fixed schedule does even before this — availability was always meant to come from the operator's Active/Not Active toggle (point 2 above) plus the association's published operating hours (point 1) — so the separate live-session channel was redundant with, and confusing alongside, the pooled-slot model above. Removed: `(driver)/dashboard.tsx`'s "UV Express Booking" card (Go Online/Go Offline buttons, live passenger queue) and its state/handlers; `(main)/book.tsx`'s "Available Now" section on the UV wizard's second stage (now just "Book Ahead" — already-created slots plus "Pick Your Own Time"), the `UvSessionCard`/`UvDetailCard` components, and the 5s live-session poll. The driver's UV Express role now begins purely at boarding — they join the shared route-scoped `dispatch_queue` (unchanged, see the UV Express dispatch queue Critical Insight) and, once dispatched, board whichever commuters already booked a pooled slot for that route via the (renamed) "UV Express Passengers" card's "Scan to Board" button. **Backend endpoints are left dormant, not deleted**, matching this codebase's established convention (same as `uv_slots/create_slot.php` above): `uv_sessions/go_online.php`, `go_offline.php`, `get_active_sessions.php`, `get_session_passengers.php`, and `reservations/create_reservation.php`'s/`preview_ticket.php`'s/`board_reservation.php`'s `uv_session_id` branches all still exist and still work for any historical direct caller — nothing in the mobile UI calls any of them anymore, and no existing `uv_sessions` row or past reservation's `uv_session_id` is affected. The `bookingService.ts` client wrappers (`fetchUvSessions`, `goOnlineUv`, `goOfflineUv`, `fetchSessionPassengers`) were deleted outright (dead frontend code, not a DB/API surface subject to the deprecate-in-place convention).

## 8c. Association Settings
`(association)/profile.tsx` was previously minimal — a hardcoded "Active" status pill (not reading real `status`), Name-only inline edit, Log Out. `associations/update_association.php` had **zero authentication** — any request carrying a valid `association_id` could rename any association, no password/session check of any kind. Both fixed alongside adding a real Settings surface for this role.

- **`associations/get_profile.php`** (new, GET) — mirrors `commuter/get_profile.php`'s role: returns `id/name/acronym/email/phone/address/status/created_at` live, since `login_mobile.php`'s association branch never returns `acronym` at all and every other field only ever reflects login-time state. No Association-equivalent of `UserContext` exists — `profile.tsx` and `settings.tsx` each call this directly on focus (paint-from-AsyncStorage-then-reconcile, same pattern, just not lifted into a shared context) rather than introducing a new provider for what's currently a two-screen need.
- **`associations/update_association.php` extended, not replaced** — now accepts optional `acronym`/`contact_email`/`contact_phone`/`address` alongside the still-required `name`, plus a new `password` field. **Password is required only when `contact_email` or `contact_phone` changes** — a name-only save (Profile's existing inline edit, unchanged, still sends just `{association_id, name}`) keeps working with zero added friction, while the new Settings-driven full-field edit is gated exactly when it touches the two login-credential-adjacent fields. This is a deliberate asymmetry from the commuter pattern (`commuter/update_contact.php` gates uniformly) — required here specifically to satisfy "keep the existing Profile edit behavior unchanged." Email/phone changes get the same format + cross-table duplicate checks (`isValidEmailFormat()`/`emailAlreadyRegistered()`/`phoneAlreadyRegistered()`) every other contact-update endpoint in this codebase uses.
- **`associations/change_password.php`** (new) — mirrors `commuter/change_password.php` exactly (current-password-gated, 6+ char minimum, rehash). No association password-change capability existed anywhere before this.
- **Association Verification is intentionally a single view, not two** — the `associations` table has only ever had one status concept (`status`: pending/active/inactive, admin-approved via `auth/verify.php`'s `type==='association'` branch), never a separate `is_verified` column the way `commuters`/`operators` have both. An association's login credential *is* the account; there's no separate representative sub-identity with its own verification state to distinguish from the org's own status. `(association)/association-verification.tsx` shows only what's real — building a second concept would mean designing a whole new verification pipeline from scratch, out of scope here.
- **Settings' 8 Notification toggles are deliberately inert** — unlike commuter's equivalent section (which filters a real, already-existing Recent Activity feed derived from booking data), this app has **no activity-log surface anywhere** for driver/vehicle/route/fare/schedule changes on the association side. The toggles persist a preference (`src/state/settingsPrefs.ts`, 8 new `pref_assoc_notify_*` keys) and say so honestly in-app, rather than silently gating nothing or inventing a new activity-log system disproportionate to a Settings addition.
- **No Delete/Deactivate action exists anywhere in this Settings screen** — an association is referenced by operators, drivers, vehicles, routes, fares, schedules, trips, bookings, and payments; removing that self-service capability was an explicit requirement, not an oversight. If a closure process is ever needed, it belongs to the admin panel, not this screen — no such workflow was invented here.
- **Terms & Policies shows 2 of the 4 originally-requested rows** — Terms & Conditions (`ASSOCIATION_TERMS`) and Privacy Policy (`PRIVACY_POLICY`, both in `src/constants/registrationTerms.ts`, reused verbatim from registration). A separate "Association Policies" row was skipped — `ASSOCIATION_TERMS` already covers operator-approval responsibilities, route/schedule/fare management, and remittance, so a second row would just duplicate it under a different label. Cancellation & Refund Policy was skipped too — that content is written entirely in a commuter's voice ("your TranSync wallet") and nothing in it is association-configurable.
- Applications/Members/Routes/Fares/Schedules (`dashboard.tsx`/`members.tsx`/`route-builder.tsx`/`fare.tsx`/`schedules.tsx`) and the tab bar (`_layout.tsx`) are entirely untouched by this feature — confirmed via file-modification timestamps showing zero edits to any of them.

## 8d. Operator Settings
`(operator)/profile.tsx` already had real, working multi-association logic (per-association cards, vehicle-type badges, Leave/Switch, "+ Apply") and a 3-state verification badge — both left completely alone. No operator password-change or self-service profile-update endpoint existed anywhere before this.

- **`operators/apply_to_association.php` and `operators/leave_association.php` were read, not touched** — confirmed by direct reading (not assumption) that the duplicate-application check is already scoped to `(operator_id, vehicle_type, association_id)` (a comment in the file itself states a different association is always allowed for the same vehicle type), and that leaving an association already scopes to exactly one `(vehicle_type, association_id)` row, never touching the `operators` table. Both were already correct; this pass only added new files alongside them.
- **`operators/get_profile.php`** (new, GET) — same role as the commuter/association equivalents: returns `id/name/email/phone/operator_type/is_verified/status/rejected_at` live, since `login_mobile.php`'s operator branch (same 8 fields) only ever reflects login-time state. `profile.tsx`, `settings.tsx`, and `operator-verification.tsx` each call it directly on focus — no Operator-equivalent of `UserContext` exists.
- **`operators/update_profile.php`** (new, POST) — `operator_id`/`password` (always required — unlike associations' endpoint, there was no existing frictionless inline-edit UX on Operator Profile to preserve, so this is uniformly gated, matching the simpler commuter pattern) plus optional `name`/`email`/`phone`, same format/duplicate checks as every other contact-update endpoint. Edits `operators.name` (the account-holder's own person name, already shown in Profile's header) — **not** the separate `business_name` column (added migration 043), which `login_mobile.php` has never returned and no mobile screen has ever shown; introducing it now would be new surface beyond what was asked.
- **`operators/change_password.php`** (new) — mirrors the commuter/association versions exactly.
- **Operator Verification is a real, pre-existing, single concept** — unlike associations, `operators` already has both `is_verified`/`status`/`rejected_at` (admin reviews `registration_document_url`) *and* per-vehicle-type Association Approval (`operator_association_applications`), and Core Flow 8 point 9 already keeps them server-side independent (an association can't approve an operator who isn't yet admin-verified, but admin verification never auto-approves any association). `(operator)/operator-verification.tsx` is new (none existed before) — the existing verification badge on Profile becomes tappable into it; the page's own copy explicitly states the two concepts are separate, on top of them already being architecturally separate.
- **Settings' 6 Notification toggles are deliberately inert** — same reasoning as Association's 8 (Core Flow 8c): no activity-log surface exists for association-application/vehicle/driver/trip changes on the operator side either. `src/state/settingsPrefs.ts` gained 6 new `pref_operator_notify_*` keys.
- **No account-closure action anywhere** — confirmed no operator account-closure/deactivation/deletion endpoint exists anywhere in the backend before or after this pass. Per the spec's own instruction not to invent one, Settings has none.
- **Terms & Policies shows 2 rows**, same reasoning as Association's: `OPERATOR_TERMS` (13 sections, already covers fare/remittance/vehicle/driver/document-authenticity policy) plus `PRIVACY_POLICY`; a separate "Operator Policies" row and Cancellation & Refund Policy were both skipped as redundant/mismatched-audience, matching Core Flow 8c's identical resolution.
- `apply-association.tsx`/`dashboard.tsx`/`drivers.tsx`/`vehicles.tsx`/`trips.tsx`/`quota.tsx`/`fare.tsx`/`_layout.tsx` and every other `api/operators/*.php` file are untouched — confirmed via file-modification timestamps.

## 8e. Driver Profile & Settings
Unlike the other three roles, the Driver app has **no Profile tab and no route-group `_layout.tsx` at all** — `(driver)/dashboard.tsx` (1600+ lines) handles its own internal 4-tab UI (`Dispatch`/`Map`/`Wallet`/`Settings`) entirely in one file. The existing "Settings" tab (`SettingsScreen()`) already showed Profile Info (name/email/phone/license), Earnings (UV/Bus only), Operator Info, and Log Out — the closest existing analog to a Driver Profile, just under a different tab label. Deliberately **not** renamed to "Profile" and no 5th tab added — either would change existing navigation.

- **Vehicle type was already 100% server-derived, confirmed by reading `login_mobile.php`'s `findDriver()` before touching anything** — `operator_type` (`"Jeepney Op"|"UV Op"|"Bus Op"`) comes from a `CASE` on the driver's own currently-assigned `vehicles.type`, never a fixed/editable column; `dashboard.tsx`'s existing `isBusDriver`/`isUvDriver`-style checks (re-derived locally per component, no shared helper — the established pattern here, left as-is) already correctly gate all vehicle-type-specific operational UI (QR scanner access, Bus trip panel, UV Passengers card). None of Dispatch/Map/Wallet, `scan-ticket.tsx`, or `driver-map.tsx` were touched by this pass.
- **`drivers/get_profile.php`** (new, GET) — same live-derivation SQL as `findDriver()`, **plus** a join on `vehicles` for `vehicle_number`/`plate_number` (never returned by login) — gives the Settings tab a real "Assigned Vehicle: Bus #23" style display using genuinely available data. Also surfaces `association_id`/`association_name`, which login already computes but no driver screen had ever displayed before this.
- **`drivers/update_profile.php`** (new, POST) — `driver_id`/`password` (always required, same as operator's pattern — no existing frictionless inline-edit UX here) plus optional `name`/`email`/`phone`. Distinct from `drivers/drivers.php`'s `action=update_details`, which is the *operator* editing a driver they manage, not driver self-service.
- **`drivers/change_password.php`** (new) — mirrors the other three roles exactly. No driver password-change capability existed anywhere before this.
- **Driver Verification is a single, real concept** — `drivers` has no `is_verified` column at all (confirmed, no migration ever adds one), only `status` (active|inactive, operator-controlled via `drivers/drivers.php`). Same resolution as Association (Core Flow 8c): one status, not two, shown via `(driver)/driver-verification.tsx` (new) with explicit copy distinguishing it from association membership and vehicle assignment — both already structurally separate fields, shown alongside it on the enriched Settings tab, never combined.
- **Settings' 6 Notification toggles are deliberately inert** — same reasoning as the other two roles' equivalents (Core Flow 8c/8d): no activity-log surface exists for trip-assignment/schedule/vehicle-assignment/association-announcement/QR-boarding events. `src/state/settingsPrefs.ts` gained 6 new `pref_driver_notify_*` keys.
- **No account-closure action anywhere** — confirmed no driver self-service closure/deletion endpoint exists (only `drivers/drivers.php`'s operator-side DELETE, which is the operator removing a driver they manage). Per the spec's own instruction, none was invented.
- **Bus trip details (assigned bus/trip/route/departure/status) deliberately stay in the Dispatch tab** — already live-displayed there via the existing Bus trip panel; Settings/Profile scope is identity only (who, which association, which vehicle type/assigned vehicle, verification status), not live operational trip state, avoiding the duplicate vehicle-type sections the spec explicitly warned against.
- **Terms & Policies shows 2 rows** — a new `DRIVER_TERMS` constant (`src/constants/registrationTerms.ts`, written in the same voice/structure as `OPERATOR_TERMS`/`ASSOCIATION_TERMS` — covers QR/boarding conduct, fare/discount honesty, vehicle-assignment-is-operator-controlled, wallet/quota/remittance obligations) plus `PRIVACY_POLICY`. "Driver Policies" and Cancellation/Refund Policy skipped as separate rows, same reasoning applied to the other two roles.
- `scan-ticket.tsx`, `driver-map.tsx`, and every existing `api/drivers/*.php` file are untouched — confirmed via file-modification timestamps; the diff inside `dashboard.tsx` is limited to `SettingsScreen()`, the `User` type, and one new live-refresh effect.
- **Profile Picture (added 2026-08-11)** — the Settings tab's avatar circle is now a tappable `AvatarPicker` (shared component, see Core Flow 8f) instead of initials-only; uploads via `drivers/update_avatar.php`, merged into the tab's own `user` state and persisted to AsyncStorage.
- **Earnings History (added 2026-08-10)** — the Settings tab's Earnings card (Bus/UV Express only, `isUVorBus`-gated, unchanged) gained a "View Earnings History →" button pushing to new `(driver)/earnings-history.tsx`, backed by new `drivers/earnings_history.php` (see Drivers API table). Shows a per-day breakdown (date, trip count, total earnings) of the same `wallet_transactions` booking-credit ledger `driver_summary.php`'s "Today" stat already reads from, last 30 days newest-first, plus a 30-day grand total card at the top. Jeepney drivers never see the entry point at all (same `isUVorBus` gate as the Earnings card itself) since they have no per-ride app earnings to show.

## 8f. Profile Picture / Avatar Upload (all four self-managed roles, added 2026-08-11)
Lets a Commuter, Driver, Operator, or Association attach a real photo to their account, shown wherever that role's own Profile/Settings screen previously rendered an initials-only (or, for Association, icon-only) colored circle — no role had any image-upload capability for this before. Purely cosmetic: never read by any verification, fare, discount, or matching logic — the existing `id_photo`/`discount_id_url`/`registration_document_url`/`document_url` columns remain the only documents anything actually checks (see the "Automated ID Verification" and Org Hierarchy document flows, which are entirely untouched by this).

- **Storage**: `avatar_url` (nullable, migration 049) added to `commuters`, `drivers`, `operators`, `associations`. Stored as a relative path under a new, dedicated `uploads/avatars/` folder — deliberately kept separate from `uploads/{commuters,operators,associations}/`'s existing verification-document uploads, so avatar replace/remove logic can never risk touching a document file. Filename is `{role}_{id}_` . `time()` . `_` . `uniqid()` . `.{ext}`, same convention as every other upload endpoint in this codebase.
- **Endpoints**: `commuter/update_avatar.php`, `drivers/update_avatar.php`, `operators/update_avatar.php`, `associations/update_avatar.php` — four near-identical POST (multipart) endpoints, one per role (matching this codebase's existing per-role duplication convention for `get_profile.php`/`change_password.php`, not a shared polymorphic endpoint). Each accepts `{role}_id` plus either an `avatar` file (jpg/jpeg/png/webp only, 5MB max) or `remove=1`. **Replacing or removing a photo deletes the previous file from disk** (best-effort `@unlink`, looked up from the row's own prior `avatar_url` before the UPDATE) so `uploads/avatars/` doesn't accumulate an orphan every time someone changes their picture — verified live end-to-end (upload → replace → remove, each step confirmed the previous file was actually gone from disk, not just dereferenced in the DB).
- **`avatar_url` is always a bare relative path from the backend (`uploads/avatars/xxx.jpg`), never prefixed server-side** — this is a deliberate departure from `verify.php`/`ltfrb_fare_guides.php`'s existing convention of prepending a hardcoded `http://localhost/TranSync-App/transync-backend/` base. That hardcoded-host pattern only ever resolves on Expo web; `src/config/api.ts`'s `API_BASE` already branches to the LAN IP (`192.168.1.122`) for every native build, so a `localhost`-prefixed image URL silently fails to load on a real device or emulator (the avatar circle showed no photo — a real bug found and fixed the same day this feature shipped, root-caused live and confirmed by testing the endpoint directly). The mobile client now prefixes with `FILE_BASE` (new export in `src/config/api.ts`, `API_BASE` with the trailing `/api` stripped) instead — the same host the client already successfully reached to make the API call in the first place, so it resolves on web, physical device, and emulator alike. `verify.php`/`ltfrb_fare_guides.php`/`pending_operator_applications.php` were **not** touched or migrated to this pattern — they're consumed by the admin panel (always same-machine `localhost`, this bug never manifests there) or weren't in scope for this fix; if either is ever reported broken on a real mobile device, this is the same root cause.
- **Surfaced everywhere that role's own live-profile-resync already runs** — `avatar_url` was added to `commuter/get_profile.php`, `drivers/get_profile.php`, `operators/get_profile.php`, `associations/get_profile.php`, and to all four branches of `auth/login_mobile.php` — no new fetch/poll machinery needed, since every role's Profile screen already re-fetches its own profile on focus (`UserContext.refreshUser()` for commuter, a lightweight per-screen fetch-and-merge for the other three, see Core Flow 2/8c/8d/8e).
- **Mobile — one shared `AvatarPicker` component** (`src/components/common/AvatarPicker.tsx`, new), used by all four Profile screens instead of four independently-drifting copies of the same tap-to-change-photo UI. Renders the image (or the existing initials/icon fallback when `uri` is null) plus a small camera badge; tapping either opens an action sheet — Take Photo / Choose from Library / Remove Photo (only shown once a photo exists) / Cancel — via `expo-image-picker` (`~17.0.10`, already an installed dependency, no new native module added), square-cropped (`aspect:[1,1]`) and compressed (`quality:0.7`) client-side before upload. Camera **and** gallery are both offered (not camera-only) — deliberately different from this app's ID-verification pipeline's camera-only anti-forgery policy (Core Flow 2a), since a profile photo isn't a fraud-sensitive document. **`AvatarPicker` itself takes an already-`FILE_BASE`-prefixed `uri`, not the raw backend value** — each of the four call sites does `user.avatar_url ? FILE_BASE + user.avatar_url : null` inline; the component has no knowledge of the backend's relative-path convention. A co-exported `buildAvatarFormData(idField, idValue, localUri)` helper builds the identical multipart body shape all four screens send, so only the endpoint URL and the merge-into-local-state step differ per screen (commuter merges via `UserContext.refreshUser()`; Driver/Operator/Association merge the returned relative `avatar_url` into their own local `user`/`assoc` state and persist it to AsyncStorage, same pattern each screen's existing profile-fetch already uses — still unprefixed at rest, prefixed only at render time).
- **No account-closure/other-role scope**: Admin panel has no avatar UI and wasn't touched (confirmed no existing avatar/initials pattern exists there at all). Beyond each role's own Profile screen, the only other consumer is the Forgot Password flow's "Is this you?" step (below) — it doesn't surface anywhere else a *different* user would see (e.g. a commuter's photo isn't shown to a driver scanning their ticket, an operator's photo isn't shown on an association's member list) — no such display surface was asked for or added.
- **Read-only consumer: Forgot Password's "Is this you?" step** (`(auth)/forgot-password.tsx`, added 2026-08-11) — `config/account_lookup.php`'s `findAccountByPhone()` (Commuter/Operator/Association only, Driver excluded per Do Not Break rule #15) now also selects `avatar_url`, and `auth/lookup_account_by_phone.php` returns it alongside the existing masked name/account type, still never the account `id`. The confirm screen shows the real photo if set, or falls back to initials derived from the masked name (`initialsOf()` — this helper already existed in the file, unused, evidently scaffolded for exactly this) — previously a static generic person icon regardless of who the account belonged to. This is deliberately **not** the shared `AvatarPicker` component — that bundles tap-to-edit affordances (camera/gallery/remove) that must never be reachable here, since the caller hasn't proven their identity yet at this step (no OTP verified) — just a plain read-only `Image`/`Text` swap inside the screen's own existing avatar circle style.

## 8g. Jeepney Operating Hours (informational only, added 2026-08-12)
An association can publish a daily operating-hours window per Jeepney route — structurally the simplest possible sibling of UV Express's `uv_operating_hours` (Core Flow 8b) with everything booking-related stripped out, since Jeepney has no scheduled-slot booking flow to gate and fares stay cash/off-app (Core Flow 4 is dormant). This is **pure display data**: nothing in dispatch (`join_queue.php`/`start_trip.php`), the route planner, or any fare path reads `jeepney_operating_hours` — it exists solely so a commuter and a driver can see "when does this jeepney route typically run."

- **Storage**: `jeepney_operating_hours` (migration 051) — `id, association_id, route_id (UNIQUE), start_time, end_time, status (active|inactive), created_at`. One row per route, same shape as `uv_operating_hours` minus `recurrence_type`/`weekdays` (flat daily hours only — no per-weekday variation was asked for). A route with no row, or an `inactive` one, has no published hours.
- **Association CRUD** — `associations/jeepney_operating_hours.php` (GET/POST/PUT/DELETE), a direct copy of `uv_operating_hours.php`'s validation (HH:MM regex, minutes-since-midnight comparison per Do Not Break rule #54, only a zero-duration window is rejected — an overnight wrap like 22:00→05:00 is valid and treated as ending the next day) with the recurrence fields removed and the route-ownership check swapped to `vehicle_type = 'Jeepney'`. Mobile: `(association)/schedules.tsx` gained a third tab, "Jeepney Hours" (green `#16a34a` accent, matching `vehicleColor()`'s Jeepney default in `map.tsx`), structurally identical to the existing UV Operating Hours tab — a card per route with published hours (status toggle/edit/delete) plus a "Set Hours" prompt card per route that doesn't have one yet, and a matching add/edit modal with just Operations Start/End fields (no recurrence toggle/weekday chips).
- **Commuter-facing read**: `routes/get_jeepney_operating_hours.php` (GET `?route_id=`), a stripped copy of `routes/get_uv_schedule.php` (no `is_bookable` — there's no activation concept to check) returning `{"success": true, "hours": {"start_time","end_time"} | null}`. `commuter/nearby_drivers.php` was extended with a `LEFT JOIN route_assignments`/`routes` so its response now also carries `route_id`/`route_name` per driver (previously absent — this endpoint only ever returned driver/operator identity + position). `(main)/map.tsx`'s driver markers (previously non-interactive — only terminal markers had an `onPress`) gained one: tapping a jeepney marker opens a new "Driver Detail Sheet" (styled after the existing Terminal Detail Sheet) showing the driver's name/operator, assigned route, and that route's operating hours (or "no route assigned yet" / "no operating hours published yet" as appropriate) — fetched fresh per tap via `route_id`.
- **Driver-facing read**: `(driver)/dashboard.tsx`'s Dispatch tab — a new "Route Operating Hours" card renders directly below the existing "Assigned Route" card, gated on `isJeepneyDriver && assignedRoute` (UV/Bus drivers don't see it; a Jeepney driver with no assigned route yet sees neither card populated). Fetches `get_jeepney_operating_hours.php` keyed on `assignedRoute.id` whenever it changes — `assignedRoute` itself was already fetched once at login-restore via `get_driver_route.php` (Core Flow 8e's existing wiring), no changes needed there.
- **Not connected to anything else in this codebase** — no dispatch-queue gate (unlike Jeepney's platform-quota suspension, Core Flow 15), no booking-window enforcement (unlike UV Express's `uv_operating_hours`), no fare impact. Purely "here's when this route typically runs," shown at exactly two read points (commuter map tap, driver Dispatch tab) plus the association's own management tab.
- **Route Suggestions no longer show driver-status info for Jeepney, added 2026-08-12** — `(main)/map.tsx`'s Route Suggestions panel used to show a "⚠ No drivers" badge on every option card (`route_planner.php`'s `has_active_drivers`, computed from online/recently-located drivers on the option's route(s)) regardless of vehicle type. For an option whose every leg is Jeepney (`isAllJeepneyOption()`), each card's content is now split into two columns: the existing badge/fare/segments/notices on the left (unchanged), and a dedicated `jeepneyHoursCol` on the right — a small light-green circular clock icon + green "Operating Hours" label, with the actual schedule (`describeJeepneyHours()`, e.g. "5:00 AM – 10:00 PM", "24 Hours", or "Not set") in bold dark text directly underneath, right-aligned. Sourced from `routes/get_jeepney_operating_hours.php` keyed on the option's first segment's `route_id`, fetched fresh per search into a `jeepneyHoursCache` map. If the device's current local time falls outside that window (same overnight-wrap math as everywhere else, Do Not Break rule #54a), a small amber "Currently not operating" pill is shown above the icon/label — purely a client-side clock comparison against the published window, never derived from `has_active_drivers`/driver presence, which remains an unrelated signal. Mixed-vehicle-type transfer options and pure Bus/UV Express options are unaffected — they keep the original single-column layout with the "No drivers" badge in the header row.
