- **12-hour time picker, added 2026-08-12** — every schedule-related time field on `(association)/schedules.tsx` (Bus Boarding Time/Departure Time, UV Operations Start/End, Jeepney Operations Start/End) went from a raw `HH:MM` text input to a shared `src/components/common/TimeField.tsx` component: a tappable field showing 12-hour `formatTime12h()` text (e.g. "5:30 PM") that opens the native `@react-native-community/datetimepicker` (`mode="time"`, `is24Hour={false}`) — same iOS-spinner-with-Done-button / Android-auto-dismiss pattern `registration/DateField.tsx` already established for date fields. The value exchanged with the backend is still 24-hour `HH:MM` (no API/DB change) — only the picker and the closed-field display are 12-hour; 24-hour text is never shown to the user anywhere in this UI. UV Operating Hours and Jeepney Hours both additionally gained an **"24 Hours" / "Set Specific Hours"** toggle above the time fields — selecting "24 Hours" hides the pickers entirely and silently submits `00:00`–`23:59` (inferred back into the toggle's "24 Hours" state on re-edit whenever a row's stored `start_time`/`end_time` happen to equal exactly that pair); Bus Schedules has no such toggle, since boarding/departure are two distinct instants for one trip template, not a window. This was a pure UI/UX change — no validation rule, endpoint, or stored value's meaning changed (Do Not Break rule #54's minutes-since-midnight comparison and the overnight-wrap allowance are both untouched, just no longer user-facing text).

## 9. Booking Policy (advance window, refund, late cancellation, discount dedup)
1. **Advance window**, enforced in `create_reservation.php` (constants in `config/policy.php`): Bus **and** UV Express both require 24h+ before `departure_time` (UV Express used to require 48h+ — reduced to match Bus). Listing endpoints (`get_upcoming_trips.php`, `get_available_slots.php`) filter to the same window so commuters never browse a trip they can't book.
2. **Refund is a two-tier percentage schedule, not a binary cutoff — rewritten 2026-08-11, simplified again 2026-08-12 (dropped the 0% floor and the 30-minute post-booking grace period entirely).** `REFUND_TIERS` in `config/policy.php` is a per-vehicle-type list of `{hours, pct}` thresholds (descending), evaluated by `config/refund.php`'s `computeRefundOutcome()` — the single implementation of this logic, called only from `reservations/request_refund.php` (now the sole commuter-facing cancellation endpoint; see point 3). **Bus**: ≥72h (3 days) → 100%, <72h → 50%. **UV Express**: ≥24h (1 day) → 100%, <24h → 50%. There is no cancellation timing that ever yields 0% — 50% is the floor all the way up to departure, and there is no override tied to how soon after booking the cancellation happens (the earlier `POST_BOOKING_REFUND_GRACE_MINUTES` override was removed on request, along with the 0% tier). `reservations.refund_amount`/`refund_pct` (migration 050) persist exactly what was actually credited back (never recomputed later from a policy value that could change, matching `system_fee_amount`/`rebooking_fee_amount`'s existing convention) — `cancellation_type` stays `'refunded'` for both a 100% and a 50% outcome (a `'refunded'` cancellation is no longer assumed to mean "in full"; check `refund_pct`).
3. **Whatever isn't refunded (the other 50%) is forfeited to driver/operator/system.** `request_refund.php` runs the exact same 3-way remittance split a real boarding uses (`config/remittance.php`, see Core Flow 12) on the forfeited half so the driver/operator/system are paid as if that portion of the ride had happened. Releases the seat either way (another commuter can still book it if there's time left). **`reservations/cancel_late.php` is deprecated in place** (frozen at the old three-tier/binary policy history — hardcoded 72h/24h cutoff, a 0% tier, and at one point a grace period — for any historical/direct caller; nothing in the mobile client calls it anymore, matching this codebase's established deprecate-in-place convention). The old design rationale for keeping refund and forfeiture as *separate* endpoints doesn't apply under a tiered/continuous policy — a single cancellation is always partly "money comes back" and partly "money is gone" now, so `request_refund.php` alone determines and settles both sides of a given cancellation.
3a. **Operator-initiated trip/slot cancellation is unaffected — still always 100%, unconditionally** — `bus_trips/update_trip_status.php`'s and `uv_slots/update_slot_status.php`'s `cancel` actions refund every still-`reserved` passenger in full regardless of how close to departure the cancellation happens (not the commuter's fault), now also stamping `refund_amount`/`refund_pct=100.00` on each row for display consistency with the tiered paths above.
4. **Rebooking is a 2-step, operator-approved exception — rewritten 2026-08-10 from an earlier operator-executes-everything model.** A commuter can request a rebook either **proactively** (while still `boarding_status='reserved'`, any time before departure, no cutoff) or **reactively** within a `NO_SHOW_REBOOK_REQUEST_GRACE_HOURS` (24h, `config/policy.php`) window after the booking becomes `no_show` — see `config/rebook_eligibility.php`'s `isRebookEligible()`, the single shared source of this rule (also used by an opportunistic `expireStaleRebookRequests()` sweep, same no-cron pattern as `sweepDepartedReservations()`). The operator's role is now **approve/reject only** (`reservations/operator_review_rebook.php`, `{operator_id, reservation_id, action:'approve'|'reject', rejection_reason?}` — mirrors `associations/review_operator_application.php`'s single-endpoint-action-param convention) — approving never creates a reservation or moves money, it only flips the request to `'approved'`. Only then does the **commuter** pick their own new trip/seat and pay the fee themselves, via `reservations/complete_rebook.php` (`{commuter_id, reservation_id, new_trip_id?, new_uv_slot_id?, new_seat_label?}`) — reachable from `BookingDetailsModal`'s "Select New Trip" button, which pushes to `(main)/select-rebook-trip.tsx`. This is a genuine behavior change from the old model, where the operator picked the new trip *for* the commuter in one atomic action.
   - **The fee is always 10% of the ORIGINAL fare** (`REBOOKING_SURCHARGE_PCT`, unchanged), never the new trip's own fare. **Split via the normal 3-way remittance (`creditRemittanceSplit()`, Core Flow 12) as of 2026-08-12** — system's flat cut, the new trip's operator's own configured cut, and the new trip's driver (fixed for Bus; folds into the operator's credit for a driver-less pooled UV Express slot) keeps the remainder — rather than the earlier design where the whole fee went to the operator outright. Recorded against the **new** reservation's own `system_fee_amount`/`operator_fee_amount`/`driver_net_amount` audit columns, kept separate from the old reservation's own forfeiture-split amounts (see the next bullet) so neither overwrites the other.
   - **Same-operator-only** — the new trip/slot must belong to the exact same operator as the original booking (derived server-side from the original reservation's own trip/slot, never client-supplied); any route, not just the same one. Not an open cross-operator marketplace. A route-level pooled UV slot (`operator_id IS NULL`) has no single operator to rebook within and is rejected with a clear message — a pre-existing limitation of the old model too, just now surfaced as a real error instead of silently unreachable.
   - **Settling the OLD reservation depends on its state at completion time**: if still `'reserved'` (a proactive rebooking, completed before the original departure), it's force-settled as a forfeiture — the exact same remittance split `cancel_late.php` already uses (driver/operator/system paid the original fare, as if the ride had happened), with `cancellation_type='rebooked'` instead of `'late_cancelled'`. If already `'no_show'` (the automatic sweep already paid it out), nothing further is settled — just linked (`cancellation_type='rebooked'`, `rebooked_to_reservation_id`) — this is the same "no double payment" behavior the old model already had for its only reachable case.
   - **The new reservation is pre-settled** (`payment_status='released'` directly, `fare_amount`/`base_fare_amount` = the original fare carried forward, `rebooking_fee_amount` = the fee actually charged, `rebooked_from_reservation_id` set, fresh QR) — preserves the existing double-payment guard in `board_reservation.php` (never re-runs `creditRemittanceSplit()` for an already-`released` reservation). `board_reservation.php`/`preview_ticket.php` both additively return `is_rebooked`/`original_fare_amount`/`rebooking_fee_amount`/`additional_fare_due:0` when scanning/previewing one, surfaced by `(driver)/scan-ticket.tsx`'s result modal as a "REBOOKED PASSENGER" info block.
   - **`rebook_requests.status`** widened (migration 047) from `('pending','fulfilled','dismissed')` to `('pending','approved','rejected','fulfilled','expired')` — `'dismissed'` renamed to `'rejected'` (with a `reviewed_by_operator_id`/`reviewed_at`/`rejection_reason` audit trail added), `'expired'` is new (a `'pending'` or `'approved'` request whose underlying booking's eligibility lapses before it's acted on).
   - Old `reservations/operator_rebook.php` and `reservations/reject_rebook.php` are deprecated in place (left functional for any historical caller, no longer called by any current screen) — see the API table.
   - Mobile: `(operator)/trips.tsx`'s manifest row shows Approve/Reject buttons (Reject requires a typed reason) whenever `rebook_requested` is true, regardless of the booking's `boarding_status` (previously gated to `no_show` rows only, since that was the only eligible state).
5. **Student/disabled/elderly discount dedup**: `create_reservation.php` checks for a prior non-default-discount reservation by the same commuter on the same `trip_id`/`uv_session_id`/`uv_slot_id` before applying the 20% off; a repeat booking is silently forced to `discount_type='default'` (`discount_capped: true` in the response) rather than rejected.
6. **Passenger Info & Booking Confirmation screens** (`(main)/book.tsx`'s "passenger"/"confirm" steps, `PassengerForm.tsx`/`BookingConfirm.tsx`) — expanded 2026-08-03, twice the same day. First pass added a fuller Booking Summary, optional passenger fields (email, emergency contact name/phone — `Passenger` type widened accordingly), a Fare Breakdown preview, a Payment Method Preview, a Booking Reminder card, a required Terms & Conditions checkbox, and a pre-submit confirmation dialog — plus an *informational* Passenger Type selector. **Second pass removed that selector entirely** (no chip picker, nothing tappable resembling a manual type choice — see Do Not Break rule #70) — the Fare Breakdown's discount is now purely automatic from `commuterProfile.isVerified`/`category`, silent when not eligible. The same pass added a real Estimated Arrival/Travel Time to the Booking Summary, computed server-side (`get_upcoming_trips.php`/`get_available_slots.php` now return `estimated_duration_minutes`, see rule #71 and `routes/geo_helpers.php`) rather than omitted as in the first pass, and replaced the checkbox's bare label with a full, viewable (but not mandatory-to-open) Terms & Conditions/Cancellation Policy sheet whose copy mirrors this app's actual booking/refund/cancellation/rebooking/QR-validity rules (Core Flow 9/11/12/16) — checking the box never requires opening it. **Split into two separate policy modals** (2026-08-05, on request) — `PolicySheet` (a shared sheet-chrome wrapper) now backs two independent modals, Terms & Conditions (commuter responsibilities, booking validity, QR code usage, refund-eligibility pointer, verified discounts, compliance) and Cancellation Policy (full/no-refund cutoffs, where a refund goes, booking again after cancelling, when cancellation is no longer possible) — reached via two blue (`#2563eb`) hyperlink-styled `Text` spans, "Terms & Conditions" and "Cancellation Policy", laid out side by side in one row (`f.policyLinksRow`) below the still-unchanged required checkbox (the checkbox itself is the actual submit-gate, per rule #70 — opening either link is optional either way, same as before the split). The Cancellation Policy copy is written to match this app's real, already-implemented two-tier behavior (full refund only before the 72h Bus/24h UV Express cutoff; still-cancellable-but-forfeited after that, right up to departure — Core Flow 9.2/9.3) rather than a stricter "blocked entirely under 24h" reading, since the latter would contradict the working `cancel_late.php` flow. `BookingConfirm.tsx` (the success screen, unchanged by the second pass) has a prominent, enlarged total-paid display, a Seat/Vehicle No./Booking ID detail row, and a real "Download Ticket" action (`expo-print` + `expo-sharing`, already-installed deps — generates a PDF embedding the QR via `react-native-qrcode-svg`'s `getRef().toDataURL()`, then opens the native share sheet; no new native dependency was added). The Fare Breakdown's permanent "Booking Fee: ₱0" line and the Payment Method Preview's wallet-only (no fake GCash) scope are both still load-bearing — see rule #70.

## 9a. Commuter Booking Sync (My Upcoming Bookings / Recent Activity / Booking History)
Keeps a commuter's own bookings synchronized across three surfaces without a manual refresh — no websocket/push infra exists in this app, so "live" here means an instant local update the moment an action succeeds, plus a refetch whenever a screen using this data regains focus (the same `useFocusEffect` pattern `wallet.tsx` already used), not a background push.

1. **Single shared source, not three independent fetches**: `src/context/BookingsContext.tsx` (`BookingsProvider`, mounted once in `(main)/_layout.tsx` around `<Tabs>`, so it persists across tab switches) is the one place a commuter's reservations are fetched and held — `book.tsx`'s "My Upcoming Bookings" strip, `home.tsx`'s "Recent Activity", and `(main)/booking-history.tsx` all read from it via `useBookings()` instead of each independently calling `get_commuter_reservations.php` and holding its own stale copy (the original gap: returning to the Book tab never refreshed its own booking list, and Home's "Recent Activity" section was a permanent hardcoded placeholder that was never wired to real data at all).
2. **`reservations.resolved_at`** (migration 033, nullable `DATETIME`) is set the moment a reservation leaves the active/reserved state — refund (`request_refund.php`), late cancellation (`cancel_late.php`), no-show forfeiture (`config/sweep.php`, both branches), and trip/slot completion or operator-cancellation (`bus_trips/update_trip_status.php` and `uv_slots/update_slot_status.php`, all three relevant branches in each). Nothing previously captured *when* a booking resolved — only `created_at` (booking time) and `boarded_at` (migration 029, boarding time) existed — so there was no timestamp to order or display a "Booking Cancelled"/"Trip Completed" event by.
3. **`get_commuter_reservations.php` gained a `scope` param** — `scope=upcoming` (default) is the original query, byte-for-byte unchanged (active bookings only, soonest departure first, so no existing caller breaks); `scope=all` drops the active-only filter and returns every booking regardless of status, newest-created first, capped at 100 (this app has no pagination anywhere yet — matches that existing convention rather than introducing a one-off exception). Both scopes now also return `seat_labels` (previously omitted — Upcoming Bookings couldn't show a Bus seat number before this), `payment_status`, `cancellation_type`, `resolved_at`, and (added for the Booking Details screen, see point 8) `base_fare_amount`, `passenger_phone`, `passenger_age`, `vehicle_plate`/`vehicle_type`/`driver_name` (Bus only — `LEFT JOIN trips`/`vehicles`/`drivers`; always `null` for UV Express, which is pooled/queue-based with no fixed vehicle until a driver is actually dispatched, Core Flow 8b/12), `estimated_duration_minutes` (same Haversine-over-waypoints estimate as the trip-listing endpoints, Do Not Break rule #71), `rebook_request_status`, and (added for the unified commuter-facing status vocabulary, see point 9) `vehicle_status` — the underlying `trips`/`uv_slots` row's own lifecycle status (`scheduled|boarding|departed|completed|cancelled`), via a `LEFT JOIN uv_slots` alongside the pre-existing `trips` join; `null` for a legacy reservation with no `trip_id`/`uv_slot_id` at all.

9. **A single, consumer-facing status vocabulary — Upcoming / Boarding / Completed / Cancelled / Refunded / Rebooked / Expired — is derived client-side, never stored**: `getDisplayStatus()` (`src/types/booking.ts`) computes it purely from `boarding_status` + `cancellation_type` + the new `vehicle_status` field (point 3), so there's no second status column that could drift from the reservation's real state. Mapping: `completed`→Completed; `boarded`→Boarding (already scanned, trip under way); `cancelled`→Rebooked if `cancellation_type='rebooked'` (a proactive rebooking completed while still `reserved` — see Core Flow 9.4 — force-settles to `boarding_status='cancelled'`, so this check must come before the refunded/plain-cancelled fallback) else Refunded if `cancellation_type='refunded'` else Cancelled (covers `late_cancelled`/`operator_cancelled`); `no_show`→Rebooked if `cancellation_type='rebooked'` (the no_show-path branch of Core Flow 9.4's rebooking) else Expired; `reserved`→Boarding if the trip/slot's own `vehicle_status` is `boarding`/`departed` (ahead of this passenger's own QR being scanned) else Upcoming. Used by "My Upcoming Bookings" (`book.tsx`), Booking History (`booking-history.tsx`), and the Booking Details header (`BookingDetailsModal.tsx`) — all three now always agree on what a booking is called. **Operator/driver-facing manifests are deliberately untouched** — `(driver)/dashboard.tsx`, `(operator)/trips.tsx`'s manifest, still read the raw `BOARDING_STATUS_LABEL` (Waiting/Boarded/Completed/No Show/Cancelled), since an operator needs the literal state, not the commuter-friendly rollup.
10. **"My Upcoming Bookings" and Booking History both now poll while the screen stays open**, not just on focus — `book.tsx` gained an 18s `refreshUpcoming()` interval (`UPCOMING_POLL_MS`, mirrors `home.tsx`'s pre-existing `ACTIVITY_POLL_MS` Recent Activity poll) and `booking-history.tsx` gained the equivalent for `refreshHistory()`. This closes a real gap: previously a booking sitting on the Book tab (or Booking History) with no tab-switch would keep showing a stale status — e.g. still "Waiting" — for as long as the commuter stayed put, even after the grace-period sweep (opportunistically triggered by this same endpoint, Core Flow 11.4) had already flipped it to `no_show`/expired server-side. Combined with `addOptimistic()` (point 5) for the create path, every corner of the commuter booking lifecycle — creation, in-flight status changes, and terminal resolution — now reaches "My Upcoming Bookings"/Booking History without a manual refresh, tab switch, logout, or app restart.
11. **"My Upcoming Bookings" cards show the seat label(s) inline** (`Seat 12A`, appended next to the departure time) when `seat_labels` is set — previously only visible after opening Booking Details; Booking History's own cards already did this.
4. **Recent Activity has no persisted event log of its own** — `deriveBookingActivity()` (`src/services/bookingService.ts`) synthesizes it client-side from `scope=all` rows: one "Booking Confirmed" entry per reservation at `created_at` (this system confirms a booking atomically at creation, no separate pending-confirmation step, so "Booking Created" and "Booking Confirmed" are deliberately collapsed into one entry rather than two identical-timestamp rows), plus one more at `resolved_at` if set — "Trip Completed" when `status='completed'`, otherwise "Booking Cancelled" (covers refund/late-cancel/operator-cancel/no-show alike — there's no separate "No Show" activity type in the requested vocabulary). Sorted newest-first.
5. **Instant local updates, reconciled by refetch**: `BookingsContext`'s `addOptimistic()` is called right after `create_reservation.php` succeeds (`book.tsx` reconstructs a full booking record from `draft.selected`/`draft.passenger` client-side, the same pattern `BookingConfirm.tsx` already used, since the create endpoint doesn't echo back route/seat/vehicle-type info) — the new booking appears in "My Upcoming Bookings" immediately, no "Book Another Trip" tap needed. `applyLocalStatusChange()` is called right after a cancel/refund succeeds, instantly dropping it out of Upcoming; both call sites also trigger a background `refreshAll()`/`refreshHistory()` to reconcile with the server's actual `resolved_at`/`cancellation_type` once it lands.
6. **Refresh cadence**: `book.tsx` and `booking-history.tsx` refetch on focus only (`useFocusEffect`, no interval). `home.tsx` additionally polls every 18s while focused (`ACTIVITY_POLL_MS`) on top of its own focus refetch, so a status change triggered elsewhere (driver boards, trip completes, no-show sweep) shows up on Home without switching tabs — slower than this app's usual 5s poll cadence (book.tsx's live-status/UV-availability polls) since these events aren't second-sensitive.
7. Mobile: `(main)/booking-history.tsx` (new screen, pushed from Profile → "My Bookings", previously a dead `onPress={() => {}}` stub) lists every booking via `scope=all`, empty state "No booking history yet." — nothing is auto-deleted, matching the requirement that history only clears if the commuter does it manually (no such delete action exists yet). `home.tsx`'s "Recent Activity" empty state is "No recent activity." (previously hardcoded "No rides yet" regardless of actual data). `book.tsx`'s "My Upcoming Bookings" section now always renders on each mode's first stage (previously hidden entirely when empty) showing "You don't have any upcoming trips." in place of the card strip when `myBookings` is empty.
8. **Every booking card (both "My Upcoming Bookings" and Booking History) is tappable, opening `src/components/booking/BookingDetailsModal.tsx`** — one shared component (not duplicated per screen) showing Booking ID, Route, Vehicle Number/Type, Seat(s) (highlighted the same blue as the seat map), Departure, Est. Arrival, Terminal, Passenger Info, Fare Breakdown, Payment Details, Booking Status, and the commuter's actual boarding QR (the exact same `qr_code` value `board_reservation.php` scans — not a separately generated one). It owns its own live-status polling, Cancel (identical eligibility rules to the old inline modal it replaced — Core Flow 9.2/9.3), and **Request Rebook** (new) internally, via `useBookings()`'s `applyLocalStatusChange`/`refreshAll`. This replaced two separate, narrower things: `book.tsx`'s old inline `viewBooking` modal (QR + Cancel only, no other detail), and Booking History's cards, which weren't tappable at all before.
   - **Request Rebook is commuter-initiated but never rebooks anything itself** — shown whenever `isRebookEligible()` (Core Flow 9.4) says so, client-mirrored in `BookingDetailsModal.tsx` (server stays authoritative). This app has no push/in-app notification system, so a request can't be "delivered" to the operator any other way than a visible flag on the operator's own trip/slot manifest. `reservations/request_rebook.php` inserts a `rebook_requests` row (`reservation_id, commuter_id, status='pending'`, migration 035; `UNIQUE (reservation_id, status)` blocks duplicate pending requests for the same booking); `bus_trips/get_trip_bookings.php`/`uv_slots/get_slot_bookings.php` (the operator's manifest fetch) `LEFT JOIN rebook_requests` and return `rebook_requested: bool` (true only while `status='pending'`), rendered as an amber "Commuter requested a rebook" badge plus Approve/Reject buttons in `(operator)/trips.tsx`'s manifest. Once approved, the commuter sees a "Select New Trip" button on the same Booking Details screen instead of the request badge — see Core Flow 9.4 for the full 2-step flow.
   - **The student/senior/PWD discount dedup rule is unchanged** — still scoped to the exact same `trip_id`/`uv_slot_id`/`uv_session_id` a commuter already used a discount on (Core Flow 9.5, Do Not Break rule #13), not a broader "once per vehicle type" cap. The Booking Details screen's Fare Breakdown just accurately *displays* whatever `create_reservation.php` actually applied (`base_fare_amount` vs `fare_amount`) — it doesn't change the underlying eligibility logic, which was confirmed correct by direct code read and was already covered by existing tests/behavior before this pass.

## 10. Payment Escrow (`reservations.payment_status`)
Money is debited from the commuter at booking time same as before, but now explicitly tracked as `held` until resolved:
- `held → released` — `board_reservation.php` on a real QR scan (full remittance split), **or** the pre-settled reservation created by `complete_rebook.php` (no split — see Core Flow 9.4).
- `held → refunded` — `request_refund.php`, or an operator-initiated `cancel` (see Core Flow 11) which auto-refunds every still-`reserved` passenger.
- `held → forfeited` — the grace-period sweep or `reservations/cancel_late.php` (see Core Flow 9.3/11). **Despite the name, `forfeited` is not "nobody gets it"** — from the commuter's side the fare is gone (forfeited), but the driver/operator/system are actually paid via the normal remittance split at the moment of forfeiture. It's named from the commuter's perspective, not a statement that the money vanishes.
It never moves any other direction. This is orthogonal to (and doesn't change) the jeepney live-ride flow's "wallet never blocks" rule — bookings still hard-block insufficient balance at `create_reservation.php`, same as before escrow existed.

## 11. Boarding, Departure & QR Grace Period
1. Driver taps **Start Boarding** / **Depart** (mobile: driver dashboard's Bus-trip panel and UV-slot panel) → `bus_trips/update_trip_status.php` or `uv_slots/update_slot_status.php`. UV slot actions are authorized for the owning operator **or** whichever driver is currently `dispatched` for that route (dispatch-queue check, same pattern `board_reservation.php` already used for scanning). Both buttons are always tappable — never hard-disabled by time — but `(driver)/dashboard.tsx`'s Bus panel shows a time-aware hint line above them computed from the trip's `boarding_time`/`departure_time` vs. `Date.now()`: "Boarding opens at {time}" (before boarding time), "Departs at {time}" (during the boarding window), or "Departure time has passed — tap Depart when ready" (after departure time) — informational only, the driver retains a manual override to act at any time regardless of what the clock says.
2. **`depart` no longer immediately expires unboarded reservations** — that was the pre-Phase-3 behavior. Passengers can still board for `QR_GRACE_MINUTES` after `departure_time` — a per-vehicle-type map in `config/policy.php` (`["Bus" => 20, "UV Express" => 30]`, keyed by `reservations.vehicle_category`), not a single flat number: Bus's fixed schedule/vehicle needs less slack than UV Express's queue-based rollover.
3. **QR validity window**: for Bus and UV-slot bookings, `board_reservation.php` now accepts the QR from booking time through `departure_time + 30min`. UV slots additionally keep their existing "inactive until 30 min before departure" anti-line-skip lower bound (both constraints apply together). Live UV sessions (walk-up) have no time gate at all — no fixed schedule to expire against.
4. **Forfeiture sweep** — the actual sweep logic lives in one shared function, `sweepDepartedReservations()` in `config/sweep.php` (extracted from a bug: `get_reservation_status.php` used to run its own separate, simpler inline `UPDATE` that flipped `boarding_status`/`payment_status` **without** calling `creditRemittanceSplit()` — silently leaving the driver/operator/system unpaid, and since the row left `boarding_status='reserved'` it also became invisible to the "real" sweep in `sweep_departed_trips.php` afterward, so it could never get paid later either. Both callers now go through the same function). It first `SELECT`s every reservation still `reserved` past its grace period (Bus via `trips`+`vehicles` join for the fixed `driver_id`/`operator_id`; UV Express via `uv_slots.operator_id`, no fixed driver — and for a route-level pooled slot, `operator_id` is `NULL`, so the forfeited fare goes to the system ledger only instead of an arbitrary/nonexistent operator, see Core Flow 8b), then per-row: flips it to `boarding_status='no_show'`, `cancellation_type='no_show'`, `payment_status='forfeited'`, **and runs the same remittance split as a real boarding** (`creditRemittanceSplit()`, see Core Flow 12) so the driver/operator/system are compensated for a seat that couldn't be resold. **Purely time-based — never gated on the trip/slot's own status being `'departed'`.** This was also a real bug: the original `WHERE` clauses on both the sweep and `get_reservation_status.php`'s own check required `t.status = 'departed'`/`s.status = 'departed'` first — meaning a driver who simply never tapped "Depart" left the booking (and its QR) looking permanently "still valid" in the app, even though `board_reservation.php`'s own date check already silently refused to let it be scanned. Both call sites now key purely off `NOW() > departure_time + grace`, regardless of trip/slot status (a `'cancelled'`/`'completed'` trip never has a lingering `'reserved'` row to match anyway, since both resolve every reservation synchronously elsewhere). Still idempotent — once a row leaves `boarding_status='reserved'` it no longer matches the candidate `SELECT` on a later call. Called opportunistically (no cron exists in this project) from `get_reservation_status.php` (which also now computes its `grace_seconds_remaining` countdown the same way — time-based, not `vehicle_status==='departed'`-based), `sweep_departed_trips.php`, and `get_commuter_reservations.php` (both scopes — so a booking that's past its grace period but never got flipped by a status poll still moves out of "Upcoming" and into "Booking History" the next time the commuter opens either screen), callable from anywhere that reads booking state. A `no_show` booking can still be rebooked afterward by the operator (Core Flow 9.4) — that's a separate, additional transaction, not a reversal of this one.
5. **Operator `cancel`** (trip or slot) is different from a no-show: it refunds every still-`reserved` passenger in full (`cancellation_type='operator_cancelled'`) since the trip not happening isn't the passenger's fault. **For a Bus trip specifically, cancelling before boarding starts (`status='scheduled'`) doesn't terminally cancel the trip — it returns the trip to `pending_assignment`** instead (`bus_trips/update_trip_status.php`, added 2026-08-03): after refunding every reserved passenger, `operator_id`/`vehicle_id`/`driver_id`/`conductor_name`/`conductor_phone`/`capacity` all reset to `NULL` and `seats_booked` resets to `0`, but `route_id`/`schedule_id`/`departure_time`/`boarding_time`/`fare_override` are untouched — the trip reappears in the Pending Assignment pool (any operator approved at its schedule's association can claim it, same as a freshly-generated trip) instead of the operator losing it permanently. Cancelling a trip that's already `boarding` (or later) keeps the old terminal `cancelled` outcome unchanged — see Do Not Break rule #41.
6. **Operator/driver `complete`** also sweeps any lingering `reserved` bookings to forfeited (with the same split-credit) immediately, since the sweep's own `WHERE status='departed'` condition can never match a trip that's already `completed`.
7. Commuter side: `book.tsx`'s booking re-view modal polls `get_reservation_status.php` every 5s while open, showing a live status pill (Scheduled/Boarding Now/Departed) and a grace-period countdown banner once departed.
8. **Operator-facing Bus lifecycle controls**: `update_trip_status.php` already authorized `operator_id` for every transition — `(operator)/trips.tsx`'s Bus trip cards now expose Start Boarding / Depart / "Mark Arrived" buttons directly (mirroring the driver dashboard's buttons, same `updateTripStatus()` call), so the operator doesn't have to rely on the driver to progress a trip. "Mark Arrived" is a relabeled call to the existing `complete` action — it already force-resolves any lingering `reserved` passengers via the untouched grace sweep and frees the vehicle/driver for a new trip, so no schema/status change was needed to support it (the per-vehicle-type grace period, Do Not Break rule #17, is unaffected). `bus_trips/create_trip.php` additionally rejects creating a trip if the chosen vehicle or driver already has another `trips` row with `status IN ('scheduled','boarding','departed')` on the same `date` — prevents double-booking a vehicle/driver across two trips at once.

## 12. Driver/Operator/System Remittance
- **System's cut is a flat 5% of the fare, on every Bus/UV Express booking, no operator involvement** — `config/remittance.php`'s `creditRemittanceSplit()` always looks up the system fee from `remittance_rates WHERE scope='default'` (seeded at `system_fee_pct=5.00`; no more per-operator `operator_override` row — the system's cut is not operator-negotiable). This is deliberately separate from whatever the operator charges (below); the system always takes its 5% regardless of the operator's own mode.
- **The operator's own cut is a per-operator choice between two modes**, stored on `operators.remittance_mode` (`'quota'` default | `'percentage'`) and `operators.operator_fee_pct` (nullable, only meaningful in percentage mode): `operators/set_remittance_mode.php` (GET returns current mode + `operator_fee_pct` + the system's flat `system_fee_pct` for display; POST validates mode is `quota`|`percentage`, requires a `0–100` `operator_fee_pct` only when switching to `percentage` — nulls it out when switching to `quota`). Mobile: `(operator)/quota.tsx`'s "Your Cut Per Booking" card (Bus/UV operators only, not shown for Jeepney which has no per-ride system cut to begin with) toggles between the two and explicitly notes "the system already takes 5% of the fare" so the operator understands their percentage stacks on top of, not instead of, that 5%.
  - `remittance_mode='quota'` (default): the operator takes **0%** automatically per booking — `creditRemittanceSplit()` computes `operator_fee_pct=0`. The operator instead settles with drivers via the existing manual `driver_quotas`/`submit_quota.php`/`review_submission.php` pipeline (see below), unrelated to the automatic Bus/UV split.
  - `remittance_mode='percentage'`: the operator sets their own `operator_fee_pct` (any operator-chosen rate, 0–100), auto-deducted every booking alongside the system's flat 5% — no manual settlement needed for that operator.
  - The driver keeps whatever remains: `100% - system_fee_pct - operator_fee_pct`.
- **Fully automatic, instant, at the moment of the money event** — `creditRemittanceSplit()` is called from **three** places, all crediting system/operator/driver in the same instant transaction — there is no submission, approval, or other manual step anywhere in this flow: (1) `board_reservation.php` on a real QR scan, (2) `reservations/cancel_late.php` on a post-cutoff commuter cancellation, (3) `bus_trips/sweep_departed_trips.php` per no-show row. Amounts are stored on the reservation (`system_fee_amount`/`operator_fee_amount`/`driver_net_amount`) so later rate/mode changes don't distort historical reporting. The system's cut is a `wallet_transactions` ledger entry only (`owner_type='system', owner_id=0`) — there's no mutable "system wallet balance" column anywhere; `system/system_wallet.php` computes it live as `SUM(credits) - SUM(debits)` over that ledger (see Core Flow 15 for the admin-facing page).
- **UV Express has no fixed driver to pay** (queue-based rollover — a slot booking isn't tied to a specific driver the way a Bus trip's fixed `vehicle_id`/`driver_id` are). `creditRemittanceSplit()` handles this by folding the driver's would-be share into the operator's credit instead of leaving it unpaid — pass `driver_id = null` and the function does this automatically.
- **Double-payment is prevented for operator-rebooked tickets**: a reservation created by `operator_rebook.php` is inserted with `payment_status='released'` directly (its fare's value was already fully distributed by the original no-show's forfeiture — see Core Flow 9.4). `board_reservation.php` checks this on scan: if `payment_status` is already `'released'`, it marks the ticket boarded for tracking purposes but **skips** `creditRemittanceSplit()` entirely — otherwise the same fare's value would be paid out twice.
- **Jeepney**: no percentage cut on rides at all (fares are cash, off-app, and there's no per-ride system fee for Jeepney the way there is for Bus/UV) — instead a **flat remittance target tiered by vehicle capacity**, seeded into `driver_quotas` the first time `trips/end_trip.php` fires for a driver with no existing target row (never overwrites an operator's own customization): 12–15 pax ₱700, 16–19 ₱900, 20–23 ₱1,100, 24–27 ₱1,300, 28–32 ₱1,500, 33–36 ₱1,700 (`jeepney_quota_tiers`, retunable via `quota/tier_defaults.php`). The driver still actively settles via `submit_quota.php` (wallet debit + `quota_submissions` row for operator review), confirmed via `quota/review_submission.php` (credits the operator immediately). **This manual remittance-target flow is optional and available for every vehicle type now, not Jeepney-only** — an operator can set a driver's target regardless of Bus/UV/Jeepney, it's just most commonly used for Jeepney since that's the only type with no automatic per-ride system cut, and for Bus/UV operators who've chosen `remittance_mode='quota'` over `'percentage'`. See Core Flow 15 for the *separate* mechanism (subscription) the system uses to earn from Jeepney specifically, and Core Flow 12b for the full "Driver Quotas" mobile UI (browsed by Vehicle Type → Route → Driver, rewritten 2026-09-20 to be explicitly a remittance target, never a booking cap) and its separate, unrelated `driver_route_quotas` sibling feature.
- **Operator withdrawal**: `wallet/operator_withdraw.php` debits `operators.wallet_balance`/`operator_association_wallets` and creates a `withdrawal_requests` row **completed immediately** (`status='paid'`, `resolved_at=NOW()` — rewritten 2026-09-20, no association/admin approval step at all, since the balance is already debited synchronously in the same transaction and there's nothing left pending to approve). The mobile Wallet tab's "Withdrawals" list is purely a completed-withdrawal history now, not a request-status tracker — see Do Not Break rule #76. This is the *only* money-out action available to an operator regardless of vehicle type; it draws from the same `wallet_balance` whether that balance came from Bus/UV remittance (percentage mode), confirmed Jeepney/quota-mode quota, or Jeepney-driver subscription revenue folded through (it isn't — subscription revenue goes straight to the system, not the operator, see Core Flow 15).

## 12a. Bus Trip Scheduling — Three-Tier (Association Template → System-Generated Instance → Operator Assignment)
**Rewritten 2026-08-02** from the earlier two-tier model (association template → operator commits vehicle+driver, one trip at a time, date computed on demand) to a three-tier one: **Association** defines recurring schedules → **System** auto-generates date-specific `pending_assignment` trip instances for a rolling window → **Operator** assigns a vehicle/driver/optional conductor to an already-existing trip, which flips it to `scheduled` ("Ready/Open for Booking" — the existing status value and meaning, just no longer the trip's very first status). Commuters only ever see fully-assigned, schedule-active, non-cancelled trips. This app has **no cron/scheduler infrastructure anywhere** (confirmed, still true) — generation is lazy/opportunistic, the same pattern `sweepDepartedReservations()` already established: called synchronously from a normal request, not a background job.

1. **Association owns the recurring template** — `bus_schedules` (`association_id, route_id, service_type_id, boarding_time, departure_time, recurrence_type ENUM('daily','weekly'), weekdays` CSV nullable, `status`) — schema itself is unchanged by this rewrite. CRUD at `associations/bus_schedules.php`: GET lists an association's schedules (and opportunistically top-ups every active schedule's generated window, see point 2); POST validates the route and class both belong to the association, `boarding_time`/`departure_time` are `HH:MM`, and (for `recurrence_type='weekly'`) `weekdays` is a non-empty array of `0=Sun..6=Sat` (matching PHP's `date("w")` convention, stored as a sorted comma-separated string), then immediately generates that new schedule's initial window; DELETE first purges any of its still-`pending_assignment` trips (nothing else worth preserving — no operator, no booking) before removing the schedule itself (the FK's `ON DELETE SET NULL` still protects any already-assigned/boarded/completed/cancelled trip, unchanged). **PUT does full field edit or a quick status toggle**, distinguished by payload shape: if the body includes any of `route_id`/`service_type_id`/`boarding_time`/`departure_time`/`recurrence_type`, it re-validates, updates all schedule fields (same rules as POST), deletes any of *this* schedule's still-`pending_assignment` trips (stale under the old pattern — safe, nothing was ever assigned or booked against them), then regenerates fresh under the new pattern if the schedule ends up `active`; the quick `{id, association_id, status}`-only toggle also regenerates/tops-up on reactivation, and does nothing extra on deactivation (see point 4). Mobile: `(association)/schedules.tsx`'s "Recurrence" field is now four **UI-only preset buttons** — "Every Day" / "Weekdays" / "Weekends" / "Custom" — layered directly over the same `recurrence_type`/`weekdays` fields (`daily`; `weekly`+`[1,2,3,4,5]`; `weekly`+`[0,6]`; `weekly`+manual weekday chips respectively) — no schema or backend change for this, purely a friendlier picker over what already existed.
2. **System auto-generates trip instances from every Active schedule for a rolling window** (default 30 days, `BUS_TRIP_GENERATION_WINDOW_DAYS` in `config/policy.php`) — `config/trip_generation.php`'s `generateTripsForSchedule($conn, $schedule)` / `generateTripsForAllActiveSchedules($conn)`. For each day in the window matching the schedule's own recurrence (skipping any day/time already past the DB clock), it inserts a `trips` row with `route_id`/`schedule_id`/`boarding_time`/`departure_time` set and **`operator_id`/`vehicle_id`/`driver_id`/`capacity` left `NULL`, `status` defaulting to `'pending_assignment'`** — idempotent via a `SELECT ... WHERE schedule_id = ? AND departure_time = ?` existence check before each `INSERT` (**not** a DB-level `UNIQUE` constraint — this database already had real duplicate `(schedule_id, departure_time)` rows from before this rewrite, from the old flow having no per-schedule-per-day uniqueness check at all; adding a hard constraint would have required deleting that pre-existing data, which this app never does silently — see migration 034's own comment). Called opportunistically wherever it's cheap to top up: `associations/bus_schedules.php` GET/POST/PUT(reactivate), and `bus_trips/get_operator_trips.php` on every load (system-wide, not scoped to one operator — the pending pool is shared until claimed).
3. **Operator assigns, doesn't create** — `bus_trips/assign_trip.php` (`{operator_id, trip_id, vehicle_id, driver_id, conductor_name?, conductor_phone?}`) replaces the operator-facing half of the old `create_trip.php` flow. Validates the trip is still `status='pending_assignment'` (rejects with a clear "already assigned" message otherwise — this doubles as the race-safety net: the `UPDATE ... WHERE id = ? AND status = 'pending_assignment'` only succeeds for whichever request gets there first), the operator's approved Bus membership at the trip's own schedule's association (via `trips→bus_schedules.association_id`, never assumed to be whichever association happens to be the operator's currently-*active* one — an operator can be Bus-approved at several at once), then the same vehicle/driver checks `create_trip.php` always had (belongs to operator, active, matches class + association, has a built seat layout). **No vehicle/driver scheduling-conflict check at all** — deliberately removed, see Do Not Break rule #41; the operator has full control over assigning even with overlapping schedules or back-to-back trips. On success: `UPDATE trips SET operator_id=?, vehicle_id=?, driver_id=?, conductor_name=?, conductor_phone=?, capacity=?, status='scheduled' WHERE id=?` — capacity is still always read live from `vehicles.capacity` at this point, never accepted from the request (Do Not Break rule #43 unchanged, just now enforced at assign-time instead of create-time). **`bus_trips/create_trip.php` is left in place, fully functional, unused by any current mobile screen** — deprecated in place per this codebase's standard convention, not deleted, so any historical/direct caller keeps working; its own next-occurrence-computation logic and `preview` flag are irrelevant to the new flow (the date is already fixed by which generated trip the operator picked) but harmless left as-is. Mobile: `(operator)/trips.tsx`'s header "+ Add" button is gone — each `pending_assignment` trip card shows an "Assign Trip" button instead, opening a modal scoped to *that* trip (route/date/class shown read-only, vehicle picker filtered to the trip's own class+association, optional conductor name/phone fields) — no schedule picker, no date preview, since both are already fixed by which card was tapped.
3a. **Reassigning an already-`scheduled` trip's vehicle/driver/conductor doesn't create a duplicate pending assignment** — `bus_trips/reassign_trip.php` (`{operator_id, trip_id, vehicle_id, driver_id, conductor_name?, conductor_phone?}`) runs the same vehicle/driver validation as `assign_trip.php` (no scheduling-conflict check either, Do Not Break rule #41), but targets a trip that's already `status='scheduled'` (rejects otherwise) and never touches `departure_time` (rule #58 stays intact). **Blocks the vehicle change specifically if the trip already has real bookings** (`reservations.boarding_status IN ('reserved','boarded')`) — those commuters' `seat_labels` are tied to the current vehicle's own seat map, so swapping vehicles would orphan them; the driver/conductor can still be swapped freely regardless, since neither has a seat-map dependency (Do Not Break rule #66). Mobile: `(operator)/trips.tsx`'s `TripCard` gained a "Reassign" button (Ready/Open status only, alongside Manifest/Start Boarding/Cancel) that reopens the same Assign Trip modal pre-filled with the trip's current vehicle/conductor, retitled "Reassign Trip"/"Save Changes" for that mode.
4. **Commuter visibility requires all of**: schedule active (or `schedule_id IS NULL`, for pre-this-rewrite trips that never had one), trip exists, vehicle assigned, driver assigned, `status = 'scheduled'`, not cancelled — `bus_trips/get_upcoming_trips.php` already enforced most of this implicitly (`INNER JOIN vehicles`/`drivers` excludes any `NULL` FK once trips gained nullable vehicle/driver; `status = 'scheduled'` already excludes `pending_assignment`/`cancelled`); the one addition is a `LEFT JOIN bus_schedules` + `(t.schedule_id IS NULL OR bs.status = 'active')` check. **Deactivating a schedule hides, never cancels or deletes** — no trips are mutated when `bus_schedules.status` flips to `inactive`; the same commuter-visibility join simply stops matching, for *every* trip under that schedule regardless of whether it's still `pending_assignment` or already fully `scheduled` (this is deliberate — "the schedule is Active" is a hard, universal visibility condition, not conditional on booking state; an already-booked commuter's own ticket/QR is entirely unaffected, since boarding validation reads the reservation's own state, never `get_upcoming_trips.php`). Reactivating instantly restores visibility with zero data touched either way. Verified live: toggling a schedule off/on immediately hid/restored its trips from `get_upcoming_trips.php`.
5. **`trips` gains `schedule_id` (FK, nullable — legacy rows created before this model have `NULL`) and `boarding_time`** (`DATETIME`, alongside the pre-existing `departure_time`) — both surfaced by `get_operator_trips.php`/`get_driver_trips.php` so operator/driver UIs can show boarding time distinctly from departure time (see Core Flow 11's time-aware boarding/departure buttons). Migration 034 (this rewrite) further relaxed `operator_id`/`vehicle_id`/`driver_id`/`capacity` to nullable and added `conductor_name`/`conductor_phone` (simple text fields, nullable — no dedicated conductor table/role exists elsewhere in this codebase, so this was kept minimal on purpose) and the `'pending_assignment'` status value — see the DB Schema section's migration-history note for the full list.
6. `(operator)/trips.tsx`'s non-pending Bus trip list (Ready/Open → Boarding → Departed → Completed → Cancelled) **groups by date (soonest first) then orders each date's trips by status** via `groupTripsByDate()` — client-side over `get_operator_trips.php`'s response. The "Your Classes" amenities-overlay editor (previously living inside the old "Add Trip" modal) now loads and renders directly on the main Bus tab instead, since it's no longer tied to any single trip-creation flow.
7. **Pending Assignment is date-scoped, not loaded in bulk** (rewritten 2026-08-02, same day as point 1–6's own rewrite) — with generation running 30 days out across every active schedule, the pending pool could reach 60–90+ rows; loading all of them on every Bus Trips page visit was exactly the "overwhelm the screen" problem this fixed. `bus_trips/get_operator_trips.php` no longer returns `pending_assignment` trips **at all** (`WHERE t.operator_id = ? AND t.status != 'pending_assignment'` — pending trips have no `operator_id` yet by definition, so this is also just correctness, not only a performance trim). A new endpoint, `bus_trips/get_pending_trips.php` (`{operator_id, date}`, `date` defaults to today), returns only that one calendar date's still-unclaimed pending trips (same operator-pooling rule as before — approved Bus membership at the trip's own schedule's association), sorted chronologically by `departure_time` within the day. **Past-date pending trips are auto-expired, never just hidden** — `config/trip_generation.php`'s `expirePastPendingTrips($conn)` (`UPDATE trips SET status='cancelled' WHERE status='pending_assignment' AND DATE(departure_time) < CURDATE()`, entirely inside MySQL via `CURDATE()` — no PHP clock involved at all) reuses the existing `'cancelled'` terminal status rather than adding a new `'expired'` ENUM value, since nothing else in this codebase needs to distinguish "expired unassigned" from "cancelled" as a stored state. Keyed on the whole calendar DATE, not the exact `departure_time` — a trip departing at 11pm today is still manageable at 9am the same day; only trips left over from a *previous* day get swept. Called opportunistically (no cron) from `get_pending_trips.php`, `get_operator_trips.php`, and `associations/bus_schedules.php`'s GET — same multi-trigger-point convention as `sweepDepartedReservations()`. Mobile: `(operator)/trips.tsx`'s "Pending Assignment" status chip switches the trip-list area into a distinct date-nav mode (prev/next arrows + full date label, plus a horizontal scrollable strip of every date in the window for jumping straight to any of them — see the mobile screens section below) instead of the normal grouped-by-date list; forward navigation is capped at `PENDING_WINDOW_DAYS` (must stay equal to `BUS_TRIP_GENERATION_WINDOW_DAYS` — **60**, raised from an initial 30 on request) since nothing is ever generated past that horizon, and backward navigation is capped at today, matching "operators only manage today's and future generated trips." Verified live across the full date range including the window boundary.
   - **`addDays()` timezone bug (found and fixed same day)**: the original version computed the next date via local-time `setDate()` then extracted the result with `.toISOString().slice(0,10)` — `toISOString()` converts to UTC, which in any timezone ahead of UTC (Philippines is UTC+8) silently lands on the *previous* calendar day. The two shifts canceled out almost exactly for `delta=1`, so `addDays(date, 1)` returned the *same* date it was given — the "next" button looked like it did nothing at all. Root-caused and reproduced directly in this project's own dev environment (also UTC+8): `addDays("2026-08-02", 1)` returned `"2026-08-02"`. Fixed by formatting the result from the `Date` object's own local getters (`formatLocalDate()`) and never converting through UTC at all — same fix applied to how `TODAY` itself is computed. **Any new date-arithmetic helper in this codebase must avoid `toISOString()` for local-calendar-date extraction** — it's only safe for timestamps, never for "what calendar date is this" in a non-UTC timezone.
8. **UI/UX/layout pass (same day) — presentational only, no workflow or business-logic change.** Root-caused the reported "action buttons overlap into the next card" bug to two independent style gaps, both now fixed on the shared `tripCard`/`tripActions` styles (so the fix also applies to UV Express's slot cards and everywhere else that reuses them, for free): (a) `tripCard` had no `marginBottom` — a card nested inside a date/status group `View` (no gap of its own) rendered flush against the next one; (b) `tripActions` had no `flexWrap`, so 3-4 buttons on a narrow screen overflowed the card's right edge instead of dropping to a second line. Neither `position: absolute`, negative margins, nor a fixed card height was ever involved (verified by inspection — this app doesn't use them here); the card has always sized to its content, so once both gaps closed, wrapped buttons already grow the card's height correctly with no extra code needed. A new shared `TripCard` component (module-level, below `TripsScreen`) replaces three previously-drifting inline copies of the same card JSX (pending list, date-grouped list, and the new status-grouped "All" dashboard below) — one place to keep the lifecycle-button rules correct: **Pending Assignment → Assign Trip only. Ready/Open (`scheduled`) → Manifest, Start Boarding, Cancel** (Depart no longer shows here — it now only appears once boarding has actually started). **Boarding → Manifest, Depart** (Cancel no longer shows here — cancelling before boarding starts is the Ready/Open action). **Departed → Manifest, Mark Arrived. Completed/Cancelled → Manifest only** (already the de facto read-only detail view — no separate "View Details" button was added, since Manifest already serves that purpose). The status filter row gained a **Departed** tab, split out from what used to be a combined "Boarding" filter that silently matched both `boarding` and `departed` — every tab now maps to exactly one real `status` value 1:1, so a trip is mechanically never visible in more than one tab, and moves tabs automatically the moment its status changes (nothing new needed here — every mutating action already refetches). **The "All" tab is now a status-grouped operational dashboard**, not a flat chronological list — `groupTripsByStatus()` sections trips into Ready/Open → Boarding → Departed → Completed → Cancelled (each section internally sorted by `departure_time`), replacing the old single date-grouped list for that one tab only (every other tab keeps `groupTripsByDate()`, unaffected, since narrowing to one status makes date the only useful remaining axis). **Pending Assignment is deliberately represented as a tappable count banner** ("`N` trips awaiting vehicle & driver today → view") at the top of "All", not as inline cards — including the actual pending trips there would reintroduce the exact bulk-loading problem point 7 fixed; tapping it jumps to the real, date-scoped Pending Assignment tab. Typography/spacing: route name is now the largest text in the card (16px); a new `tripInfoRow`/`tripInfoText` pattern (small icon + 12px text, one row per topic — time, then vehicle/driver, then seats/fare) replaces the old two dense "·"-joined lines, without adding net vertical height since it was already effectively 2-3 lines of text either way, just organized more legibly; the status badge got `flexShrink: 0` so it can never be compressed by a long route name (which still truncates via `numberOfLines={1}` on its own side, unchanged). Header/tab-row bottom padding was unified to a consistent 16px across the title row, the Bus/UV switcher, and the status filter row (previously an inconsistent mix of 20/12); status chips (both the Bus/UV switcher and the status filter row) got a uniform `minHeight: 34` so every chip is the same height regardless of label length.
4. A schedule being `inactive` (or deleted) doesn't affect already-created `trips` rows — those are independent instances once committed; it just removes the schedule from the picker for *new* trips.

## 12b. Driver Quotas — the Peso Remittance Target (and a Dormant Booking-Cap Sibling)
`(operator)/quota.tsx` (the "Driver Quotas" screen, per its own header)'s **"Targets"** tab is the peso remittance target (`driver_quotas`/`quota_submissions`, unchanged schema/endpoints from Core Flow 12 — `quota/set_quota.php`, `quota/get_driver_quota.php`, `submit_quota.php`, `review_submission.php`). Rewritten 2026-09-20 from a flat, ungrouped "every driver, one target" list into Vehicle Type → Route → Driver, per an explicit requirement that this is **never a booking cap** — a driver who has fully met (or exceeded) their remittance target keeps accepting bookings exactly as before; nothing here gates `create_reservation.php`/`board_reservation.php`.

1. Vehicle-type chips are **always shown, locked (not hidden)** when the operator has no approved membership for that type yet (`VEHICLE_TYPES.map` with a `disabled`+lock-icon state, unlike every other vehicle-type chip row in this app which just filters unavailable types out) — the one deliberate UI exception in this codebase to the usual "hide what isn't approved yet" pattern, because the feature's own spec called for the tab always being visible.
2. A driver still only ever has one active target at a time (`driver_quotas` keeps its pre-existing `UNIQUE(driver_id)` — **no schema change was needed for this rewrite**); the vehicle type/route shown in the hierarchy and in the Set/Edit Target modal are purely live display context (resolved fresh via `route_assignments`/`vehicles`), never stored on the target row itself — so a driver's one target simply follows them if their route assignment later changes.
3. An **"Unassigned Drivers"** bucket is offered under every vehicle type alongside its real routes (new `quota/get_remittance_targets.php`, `?operator_id=&vehicle_type=&association_id=`, omitting `route_id`) so a driver with a vehicle but no route assignment yet still has somewhere to get a target set — without this, such a driver would be unreachable now that the old flat "every driver" list is gone.
4. **Removing a target** (new `quota/remove_quota.php`, `{operator_id, driver_id}`) hard-deletes the `driver_quotas` row only — `quota_submissions` has no FK to it and stores its own `amount`/`period_type`/`status` directly, so past remittance history is never affected; `get_submissions.php`'s `LEFT JOIN driver_quotas` just shows `quota_target: null` for that submission afterward, same as it already does for a submission whose driver never had a target.
5. Progress display (Target / Remitted-this-period / Remaining / Completed-or-Pending) reuses `get_driver_quota.php`'s exact calendar-aligned period math (daily = today, weekly = Mon–Sun, both computed in MySQL per the PHP/MySQL clock-mismatch landmine) inline inside the new endpoint, so both stay in sync — if one changes its period logic, the other must too. Jeepney's capacity-tier auto-seeding (`trips/end_trip.php`, see Core Flow 12) is unaffected by any of this — it still upserts a plain `driver_quotas` row with no route/vehicle-type field to set.
6. **A separate, unrelated booking-capacity cap (`driver_route_quotas`, migration 057) is now dormant in the UI, per this codebase's deprecate-in-place convention** — it briefly shared this same screen under a "Limits" tab (itself relocated here from an ambiguous, originally-unlabeled "Driver Quotas" tab of its own) before being removed from the mobile app entirely on 2026-09-20, the same day as the rewrite above, since it wasn't wanted as part of this feature. `daily_quota` is a plain integer cap on how many bookings a specific driver can accept **on one specific route**, per calendar day — the **backend enforcement is untouched and still fully live**: `create_reservation.php` still enforces it for Bus (fixed driver at booking time), `board_reservation.php`/`preview_ticket.php` still enforce it for UV Express at boarding/scan time (a UV slot has no fixed driver until dispatch, so it can't be checked at booking time the way Bus can — both still return `quota_exceeded: true` on rejection). `quota/get_route_quotas.php`/`set_route_quota.php` (GET/POST) still exist and work for any historical/direct caller; only the mobile UI that called them (`(operator)/quota.tsx`'s old "Limits" tab, its category/route/driver state, and its Set Daily Quota modal) was deleted outright as dead frontend code, not deprecated-in-place, matching this codebase's existing convention for unused *frontend* code (e.g. `bookingService.ts`'s deleted UV-session wrappers) as opposed to backend/DB surfaces. **Any pre-existing `driver_route_quotas` row set before this removal keeps enforcing its cap** — there is currently no in-app way to view, change, or clear one; that would require either restoring the UI or a direct DB/API action.
7. **Never conflate the remittance target with the booking cap**: a driver can independently have a remittance target on one route and a (now UI-orphaned but still-enforced) booking limit on a different (or the same) route — setting/removing one never reads or writes the other's table. `quota/get_route_quotas.php` and `quota/get_remittance_targets.php` both resolve "drivers on this route" via the identical `route_assignments ra JOIN vehicles v ON v.id = ra.vehicle_id JOIN drivers d ON d.vehicle_id = v.id` query shape (the vehicle-keyed convention, migration 056 — never `ra.driver_id`, which stays nullable/legacy) but are otherwise fully separate code paths against fully separate tables.

## 13. Bus Service Types, Fare Tiers & Seat Selection — Association-Owned
Classes and their point-to-point fares belong entirely to the **association**, not the operator — an operator can only assign one of its association's classes to a vehicle and build that vehicle's seat layout; it cannot create classes, create/edit a Bus route, or set a Bus fare.

- **Service/class catalog** (`bus_service_types`, e.g. Ordinary/Deluxe/Royal Class): **association-scoped** via `association_id` (nullable legacy `operator_id` column still exists but is no longer written by any current code path — same pattern as `drivers.association_id`). CRUD lives at `associations/service_types.php` (association-owned, create/list/delete). Operators get a **read-only** view scoped to their own *Bus-approved* association via `operators/get_association_service_types.php` (joins through `operator_association_applications WHERE vehicle_type='Bus' AND status='approved'`, not the deprecated `operators.association_id` — see Core Flow 8) — this is what populates the class picker in `(operator)/vehicles.tsx`'s Bus registration form. The old `operators/service_types.php` (operator-owned catalog) is legacy/dormant.
- **Bus routes are association-only**: `routes/save_route.php` rejects creating a route with `vehicle_type='Bus'` under `operator_id` ownership (must go through `association_id`); the legacy `routes/routes.php` POST rejects `vehicle_type='Bus'` outright (that endpoint only ever creates operator-owned routes). `bus_trips/create_trip.php`'s route-ownership check now accepts a route if it's owned by the scheduling operator directly (legacy rows) **or** if the route's `association_id` matches the operator's *Bus-approved* association (derived, see Core Flow 8) — an operator schedules Bus trips against any active Bus route their association has published, but can never create or edit that route.
- **Per-class fares**: revives the previously-dead `fare_matrix` table, now **association-only**. `routes/set_service_fare.php` (GET reads by `route_id`, no ownership check; POST/DELETE require `association_id` and validate both the route and the class belong to that association) upserts/removes a `(route_id, service_type_id)` row. `create_reservation.php`'s Bus branch prefers this over the route's flat `routes.fare` when the trip's vehicle has a `service_type_id`. Mobile: `(association)/fare.tsx` always shows all three vehicle-kind tabs (Jeepney/UV Express/Bus, chip-switchable) regardless of the association's (now-deprecated) `association_type` — a kind with no routes yet just shows its own empty state; the "Classes" button (Bus catalog + per-route "Manage Class Fares" modal) is always reachable from the header, independent of which kind's routes are currently shown. `(operator)/fare.tsx` mirrors this with its own kind switcher (only shown when the operator holds more than one approved vehicle type) and shows the same Bus class-fare data **read-only** (Jeepney/UV Express keep the original editable flat-fare screen on both apps, unaffected by any of this).
- **Vehicle capacity now differs by type** (`vehicles.php` POST/`update_details`): **Jeepney** unchanged — even number, 12–36. **UV Express** — any whole number **9–18** (raised from an earlier 9–12 cap), **including the driver's own seat** (so bookable/passenger seats = capacity − 1; nothing currently subtracts this automatically, it's a documented convention for anything that displays UV passenger counts). **Bus** — no capacity requested at vehicle registration at all; `capacity` stays `NULL` until the operator generates a seat map (below). A Bus vehicle POST instead requires `service_type_id`, validated to belong to the operator's own association.
- **Seat selection is still Bus-only** (UV stays headcount-only — no fixed vehicle to seat-map, queue-based rollover). Required (not optional): `create_reservation.php` rejects a Bus booking with no `seat_label`.
- **Seat Layout Builder + Library (supersedes the old silent-reuse model — see Do Not Break rule #26)**: `vehicles/seed_seats.php` still exists as a legacy/dormant endpoint (kept, not called by the UI anymore — see below) but the operator's actual seat-building path is now the Seat Layout Builder: a **generator algorithm** (pure client-side `generateLayout()` in `(operator)/vehicles.tsx`, no backend endpoint needed — it just produces a seat array that feeds straight into the existing `save_seat_layout.php`) creates a realistic starting layout from a vehicle type + a target passenger-seat count. Driver and Conductor seats (row 0, `seat_type` `driver`/`conductor`, always non-bookable) are always included. **Standard**: 2×2 with a center aisle, numbered front-to-back (`1A,1B,1C,1D`, `2A...`, seat columns `1,2,4,5` — **column 3 is always a real `blank`-type placeholder seat marking the aisle path itself**, not just an unnumbered gap, so the builder/commuter grids render an actual visible spacer there instead of the seats simply looking closer together); if the requested count doesn't divide evenly by 4, an even remainder (2) becomes a shorter final 2-seat row (window seats only, still with the col-3 blank between them), and an **odd** remainder (1 or 3) drops the last full row back out and replaces it with a **solid 5-seat bench row** (columns `1,2,3,4,5`, no aisle blank in this row) instead of forcing an uneven split — the back bench spans the full width with no gap, since a real bus's aisle has nowhere left to continue to past the last row (e.g. 55 requested → 12 full rows + one 5-seat bench = 53 generated — the realized count is surfaced back to the operator plainly since it won't always match the typed number exactly, same as a real bus's row layout). **Sleeper**: 2 berth positions per row (one each side of the aisle, columns `1,2` and `4,5`), each emitting a `Lower` then `Upper` bed (`1L-Lower`, `1L-Upper`, `1R-Lower`, `1R-Upper`, `2L-Lower`...), individually labeled and bookable, `seat_type: "sleeper"` for both — column 3 gets the same real `blank` aisle-path placeholder as the standard layout, added once per row regardless of whether that row's right side ended up partially or fully filled. `seat_type` was widened (migration 026) to add `blank` alongside the existing `window|aisle|sleeper|driver|conductor` — a `blank` seat is never counted toward capacity anywhere (`save_seat_layout.php`, `apply_seat_template.php`, `save_seat_template.php` all exclude `driver`,`conductor`,`blank` from their capacity math) and is never selectable by a commuter (see below). A **"5-Seat End Bench"** toolbar action in the builder applies this same solid-5-across back-row pattern (no aisle blank, replacing any that row previously had) on demand to whichever row is currently the highest-numbered one in the layout — for a hand-built or hand-edited layout where the generator's automatic version wasn't used or no longer matches; blocked (same as any row edit) if that row has an active booking. The generated layout populates the builder purely as a **draft** — nothing is saved until the operator reviews it and taps "Save Layout"; a persistent amber "unsaved changes" banner is shown the whole time a generated/hand-edited draft hasn't been saved yet (a real earlier point of confusion — generating a layout and closing the builder without saving left the vehicle's capacity unset despite looking configured). They can freely add/move/remove/rename any seat first, including a `blank` one (mobile: `(operator)/vehicles.tsx`'s Seat Layout Builder modal, backed by `vehicles/get_seat_layout.php` GET and `vehicles/save_seat_layout.php` POST, which diffs the submitted full seat list against what's stored and upserts/deletes accordingly) — including retyping to `driver`/`conductor`/`blank` or toggling the per-seat `is_blocked` flag (operator-blocked "Reserved/Saved" seats, hidden from commuter booking regardless of actual booking state). `save_seat_layout.php` also recomputes `vehicles.capacity` from the saved seats (excluding `driver`/`conductor`/`blank`) — an **empty** save (the "Clear Layout" action) resets capacity to `NULL`, not `0`, matching a freshly registered vehicle's not-yet-configured state. A completed layout can be saved as a **named** entry in the operator's **Seat Layout Library** (`vehicles/save_seat_template.php`, snapshotting current seats into `bus_layout_templates`/`bus_layout_template_seats`) and later applied to a different vehicle (`vehicles/apply_seat_template.php`, `vehicles/list_seat_templates.php`, `vehicles/delete_seat_template.php`) — applying a template **copies** its seats into the target vehicle's own `seats` rows (no FK back to the template), so editing one vehicle's layout, or deleting the template afterward, never affects any other vehicle that applied the same template. Both `save_seat_layout.php` and `apply_seat_template.php` block the operation (with a `locked_seats` list) if it would remove a seat label that has an active reservation (`boarding_status IN ('reserved','boarded')`) anywhere on that vehicle — repositioning/retyping a booked seat's label is still allowed, only removing/renaming it is blocked (see also Do Not Break rule #40). `reservations/get_available_seats.php` computes availability live, scoped per-trip — a seat is "taken" only if an active reservation on *that trip* claimed it; cancellation/refund/rebooking releases it automatically since availability is derived, not a stored flag — and now also returns a derived `state` (`available|booked|blocked|driver|conductor|blank`) driving the commuter UI directly: the 5-color scheme (Green=Available, Red=Booked, Blue=Selected (client-side), Yellow=Reserved/Saved i.e. operator-blocked, Gray=Driver/Conductor) covers real seats, and `blank` renders as a plain invisible spacer — same footprint as a seat box so row alignment holds, but no color, no border fill, and never tappable, since (per the user's own framing) "the commuter can't choose this, it's just a path" — `(main)/book.tsx`'s seat picker, positioned by each seat's actual `seat_row`/`seat_col` rather than a flat wrap grid.
- **Registration chains straight into the builder** — a Bus vehicle is still created bare via `vehicles.php` POST (capacity `NULL` until a layout exists — no structural change there), but on success `(operator)/vehicles.tsx` immediately opens the Seat Layout Builder for the newly created vehicle instead of leaving the operator to find it later from the vehicle card; the builder's empty state offers "Generate Standard Layout" / "Generate Sleeper Layout" / pick from the Library right there, so registration reads as one continuous flow (Register → build/pick a layout → Save Layout → done).
- **Changing a Bus vehicle's class is blocked once it has a seat layout** — `vehicles.php`'s `update_details` action checks `seats` for that vehicle before accepting a *different* `service_type_id` (same-value resubmission is never blocked) and rejects with `409` if any exist, since a layout is built for a specific class and letting the class label drift out of sync with it silently was the actual root cause of an earlier "vehicle edits not reflected" report. Mobile: the Edit modal greys out the class picker with a hint + a shortcut into the builder when `capacity` is set; the builder's new "Clear Layout" action (confirms, then saves an empty `seats: []`) is the only path back to "no layout" to unblock the change (see Do Not Break rule #42).
- **Operator-added class details, overlaid on the association's class (never a write to it)**: a new `operator_class_details` table (`operators/set_class_details.php` POST upsert, joined into `operators/get_association_service_types.php` and `bus_trips/get_upcoming_trips.php`) lets an operator attach their own free-text amenities/details blurb to a class they're approved to use (e.g. "Royal Class — 180-degree fully reclining bunk seats..."), surfaced under `(operator)/trips.tsx`'s Bus tab in a **"Your Classes"** section covering every class the operator is approved for — deliberately independent of whether any schedule exists for that class yet (an association can define a class with no schedule published for it at all, e.g. right after adding a new class; the details editor must still be reachable for it) — and shown to the commuter on `(main)/book.tsx`'s trip cards/detail card. This never touches `bus_service_types` (association-owned, rule #24 intact) — it's a separate `(operator_id, service_type_id)`-keyed row, so different operators sharing the same association class can each word their own details independently.
- **Atomicity**: `reservations` has a `UNIQUE (trip_id, seat_labels)` index (MySQL treats NULL as distinct, so UV/no-seat rows are unaffected) — two commuters racing for the same seat get a clean "That seat was just taken" instead of a silent double-book.
- **LTFRB Approved Fare Guide removed**: the static reference-rate card that used to appear on both `(operator)/fare.tsx` and `(association)/fare.tsx` (all three vehicle kinds) has been deleted — those screens now show only the actual routes/fares/classes described above.

## 13a. LTFRB Fare Reference Documents (read-only, added 2026-08-06)
Not a revival of the removed "LTFRB Approved Fare Guide" card above — that was a hardcoded rate table baked into the UI (a static reference-rate card with no real source document, functionally the same kind of stale-placeholder-data problem later cleaned up app-wide). This is the opposite: real official LTFRB PDF fare guides, provided directly by the project owner, shown as-is and never re-typed into UI text.

1. **`ltfrb_fare_guides`** (migration 036) — `id, vehicle_type (Jeepney|UV Express|Bus), label, file_path, sort_order, created_at`. No owner column (association/operator) — this is global system reference data, not scoped to any account. Seeded once directly via the migration's own `INSERT` statements, one row per official document: 1 for Jeepney (Traditional PUJ), 1 for UV Express, and 7 for Bus (LTFRB publishes City vs Provincial separately, and Provincial further splits into Ordinary/Aircon/Deluxe/Super Deluxe/Luxury — there is no single canonical "the" Bus fare guide, unlike Jeepney/UV Express which each only have one). **Deliberately no POST/PUT/DELETE endpoint exists anywhere for this table** — satisfies "read-only, cannot be edited/replaced/deleted through the application by any role" by construction, not by a permission check. Updating the fare rates in the future (LTFRB revises them periodically) means a developer replacing the physical file + editing the DB row directly, the same way this table was seeded, not an in-app action.
2. **Files live at `transync-backend/uploads/ltfrb_fare_guides/`**, served as static files the same way every other upload in this app already is (`uploads/{commuters|operators}/...`) — `file_path` stores the relative path, `reference/ltfrb_fare_guides.php` prefixes it with the same `http://localhost/TranSync-App/transync-backend/` base URL every other document-serving endpoint uses.
3. **`reference/ltfrb_fare_guides.php`** (GET, optional `?vehicle_type=` filter) — the only endpoint touching this table, read-only. Called unfiltered by the commuter screen (fetches all 9, groups client-side) and unfiltered once-on-mount by the association's `fare.tsx` (filtered client-side to whichever `kind` tab is currently active, same fetch-all-then-filter pattern that screen already uses for its own routes list).
4. **Viewer**: opened via `expo-web-browser`'s `WebBrowser.openBrowserAsync()` — already an installed, already-proven-working dependency in this app (`wallet.tsx` already uses the same package for PayMongo checkout, via `openAuthSessionAsync`), not a new native dependency. Opens the PDF in the device's native in-app browser sheet (SFSafariViewController on iOS, Chrome Custom Tabs on Android), which renders PDFs using the OS's own viewer — real pinch-zoom, scroll, and page navigation with no custom viewer code needed. Chosen over adding `react-native-webview` for a fully custom-embedded viewer, which would have required a native rebuild that couldn't be tested in this environment.
5. **Mobile — commuter**: `(main)/ltfrb-fare-guide.tsx` (new screen, pushed from Profile → "LTFRB Fare Guide", a new menu item). Lists every guide grouped by vehicle type in a fixed order (Jeepney, UV Express, Bus); a vehicle type with zero rows shows "No LTFRB Fare Guide available for this vehicle type." verbatim, per spec. Loading spinner while fetching, a retry-able error state on network failure, pull-to-refresh.
