6. **Mobile — association**: `(association)/fare.tsx` gained an "LTFRB Fare Reference" section, positioned above the existing "Point-to-Point Routes" list so it's visible immediately under whichever vehicle-kind chip is active — Jeepney/UV Express show their one document as a single tappable row, Bus shows all 7 (labeled "City - Ordinary", "Provincial - Deluxe", etc.). Fetched once on mount (independent of `associationId`, since this data isn't association-owned) and filtered client-side by the existing `kind` state — no separate loading/refresh cycle of its own.
7. **Viewing never touches fare computation** — no code path reads from `ltfrb_fare_guides` when creating/editing a route's fare or a class's `fare_matrix` row; this table is purely informational, exactly as specified. Labels use a plain hyphen, not an en dash — this project's DB connection (`config/db.php`) never calls `set_charset()`, so a multi-byte UTF-8 character round-tripped through it came back mangled (`ÔÇô`) when first seeded; worked around in this feature's own data rather than changing the shared connection charset, which would be a larger, unrelated fix affecting every endpoint in the app.

## 14. Operator Driver Management
`drivers/drivers.php` PUT now supports two actions: the original `status` toggle (default, unchanged) and `action='update_details'` — lets an operator edit an existing driver's `name`/`email`/`phone`/`license_number`, **and now also `password` and `vehicle_id`** (same format validation + uniqueness-excluding-self checks as registration for the identity fields). Password (if provided, non-empty) must be 6+ chars and is rehashed via `password_hash`. Vehicle reassignment (if the given `vehicle_id` differs from the driver's current one) is transactional: validates the new vehicle belongs to the same operator, is active, and unassigned (404/409 otherwise), unassigns the old vehicle (`vehicles.driver_id = NULL`), then assigns the new one bidirectionally (`vehicles.driver_id` and `drivers.vehicle_id` both updated) — all inside `begin_transaction`/`commit`/`rollback` alongside the identity-field update. Mobile: `(operator)/drivers.tsx`'s Edit modal now has a "New Password (optional)" field and an "Assigned Vehicle" picker (merging the operator's unassigned vehicles with the driver's *current* vehicle as a synthetic always-visible entry, so re-selecting the same vehicle is a no-op rather than an error). This is separate from the vehicle-assignment endpoint used elsewhere (`vehicles.php` PUT `action=assign/unassign`) and from the status toggle — both code paths can reassign a driver's vehicle, they just serve different screens.

## 15. Jeepney Driver Platform Quota & System Wallet
The system has no per-ride cut from Jeepney (fares are cash-off-app, see Core Flow 12) — this is a **separate** platform-revenue mechanism, a recurring fee paid by the driver, distinct from and unrelated to the operator's driver-quota cut (Core Flow 12's `driver_quotas`/`quota_submissions`, a different feature that happens to also use the word "quota" — watch for this naming collision when reading code/UI).

**Display wording is "Quota" everywhere a user sees it; the underlying tables/columns/endpoints keep their original "subscription" names** (`subscription_rates`, `subscription_payments`, `drivers/subscription_status.php`, `drivers/pay_subscription.php`, `reference_type='subscription'`, `SubscriptionStatus` type, `handlePaySubscription()`, etc.) — only the strings actually rendered to a user were reworded, not the schema/API surface, to avoid a much larger and riskier rename.

- **Single global admin-tunable rate** (`subscription_rates` — one row, `frequency` **daily|weekly|monthly** + `amount`; POST overwrites it rather than versioning). Admin sets/edits it from the admin panel's System Wallet page (`system/subscription_rate.php` GET/POST) — the frequency `<select>` in `SystemWallet.jsx` lists all three (daily was missing from the dropdown until this phase, even though the backend enum already supported it since migration 020). Seeded default: ₱199/month.
- **Calendar-aligned periods, not rolling windows** — `config/subscription.php`'s `currentSubscriptionPeriod($conn, $frequency)` computes `{period_start, period_end}` entirely in MySQL (no PHP `time()` involved, sidestepping the timezone landmine): daily = `CURDATE()` to `CURDATE() + 1 day` (i.e. resets at midnight, matching the "reset every 11:59pm / starts at 12:00" requirement); weekly = Monday-anchored via `WEEKDAY(NOW())` to next Monday; monthly = the 1st of the month to the 1st of the next month.
- **No scheduled job generates dues or auto-debits** (no cron infra in this project, matching the rest of the codebase's lazy-sweep pattern) — `drivers/subscription_status.php` (GET `driver_id`) computes the current period via `currentSubscriptionPeriod()` and checks `subscription_payments` for a row matching that exact `period_start`; `is_overdue = true` if none exists. The driver pays manually like a bill via `drivers/pay_subscription.php` (POST `driver_id`) — rejects with `"Already paid for the current period."` if a payment row already exists for the current `period_start` (prevents double-charging within the same period); otherwise debits the driver's wallet, credits the system ledger (`reference_type='subscription'`), and inserts a `subscription_payments` row with the computed `period_start`/`period_end`. User-facing failure/success messages read "quota" (e.g. `"No quota rate has been set yet."`, `"Insufficient wallet balance for the quota fee."`, `"Quota paid!"`).
- **Non-payment now suspends queue access, not the account/login** — `queue/join_queue.php` checks (for drivers whose own assigned vehicle is a Jeepney only — via `vehicles.type`, not the operator's deprecated `operator_type`, since one operator can now run multiple vehicle types) whether a `subscription_payments` row exists for the current period; if not, it rejects with `{"success": false, "message": "Your platform quota is overdue — pay it from your Wallet tab to rejoin the queue.", "subscription_overdue": true}` **before** the ghost-row-cleanup/insert logic runs. Login itself is deliberately **not** gated — an overdue driver can still log in and reach their Wallet tab to pay; only joining the dispatch queue (the actual revenue-generating action) is blocked. This was a deliberate design choice: gating login instead would create a dead-end (the driver couldn't reach the "Pay Now" button to resolve it).
- Mobile: `(driver)/dashboard.tsx`'s Wallet tab shows a "Platform Quota" card (amount, next-due-date or "Due now", Pay Now button) — **Jeepney drivers only** (`user.operator_type === 'Jeepney Op'`, a driver-response field that `login_mobile.php`'s `findDriver()` now derives from the driver's own assigned vehicle type, not the operator's deprecated fixed field — see Core Flow 8), since that's the vehicle type this mechanism targets; Bus/UV Express drivers don't see it (their system contribution is the automatic per-ride remittance instead, see Core Flow 12).
- **System Wallet** (admin panel, `/system-wallet`, `SystemWallet.jsx`): reads `system/system_wallet.php`, which computes the system's balance live as `SUM(credit) - SUM(debit)` over `wallet_transactions WHERE owner_type='system'` (no mutable balance column — see Core Flow 12) — shows total balance, a by-source breakdown (`remittance_fee` labeled "Bus/UV remittance", `subscription` labeled "Jeepney quota"), and the last 50 transactions. This replaced an earlier "Remittance Review" admin page from a reverted design (see Known Issues history) — there is no approval action anywhere on this page, it's read-only reporting plus the "Jeepney Driver Quota Rate" editor.

## 15a. Wallet Top-Up via PayMongo — GCash (Demo) / Maya (Demo) (rewritten 2026-08-06)
One centralized PayMongo integration backs every real top-up entry point in the app — there is no separate implementation per payment method, and no separate implementation per owner type (commuter vs driver) beyond which id is supplied. **Operator and Association apps have no top-up feature of any kind** (confirmed by audit — nothing to touch there); Admin has no wallet at all.

1. **Both "GCash (Demo)" and "Maya (Demo)" place the identical PayMongo API call** — `wallet/create_source.php` always sends `type: "gcash"` to PayMongo's Sources API regardless of which demo button was tapped. This is deliberate, not a bug: PayMongo's live Sources API only actually accepts `gcash`/`grab_pay` as a source type (confirmed against the real sandbox API and PayMongo's own current docs — `paymaya` is rejected outright, `"source_type passed paymaya is invalid"`). Real Maya support requires a completely different, separate PayMongo integration (Payment Intents + Payment Methods), which was explicitly ruled out in favor of reusing the one working flow. The commuter/driver's own chosen method (`gcash`/`paymaya`) is still recorded accurately in `paymongo_sources.method` and everywhere it's displayed (`wallet_transactions.note`, success toasts) — only the literal outbound PayMongo API call is shared. Practical effect: tapping "Maya (Demo)" opens a real, working PayMongo checkout page that happens to be GCash-branded (since that's the only page PayMongo actually serves) — expected and acceptable for a stated Demo, not a bug to fix later.
2. **`verify_payment.php` must read the method from `paymongo_sources.method` (`$tracked['method']`), never from PayMongo's own source response** (`source_data['attributes']['type']`) — since every source is really typed `gcash` on PayMongo's side per point 1, trusting PayMongo's own field would silently relabel every "Maya (Demo)" top-up as "GCash (Demo)" the moment it's verified.
3. **Owner is polymorphic — exactly one of `commuter_id`/`driver_id` in every request**, mirroring this schema's existing convention for "exactly one of several nullable FK columns" (e.g. `reservations.trip_id`/`uv_session_id`/`uv_slot_id`) rather than a `wallet_transactions`-style `owner_type`/`owner_id` pair — added via migration 037 (`paymongo_sources.driver_id`, nullable, FK→drivers; `commuter_id` relaxed to nullable). `create_source.php` validates the owner exists in the corresponding table; `verify_payment.php` looks up `paymongo_sources` scoped to `(source_id, owner_id)` and credits `wallets.balance` (commuter) or `drivers.wallet_balance` (driver, matching `drivers/driver_wallet.php`'s own existing pattern) accordingly, inserting into `wallet_transactions` with the matching `owner_type`.
4. **The owner lookup is also a security gate, not just a dedup check** — `verify_payment.php` now rejects with 404 immediately if no `paymongo_sources` row matches `(source_id, owner_id)`, rather than falling through to query PayMongo directly. This closes a real gap found while adding driver support (verified live): previously, calling verify with a source_id that belonged to a *different* owner still returned that source's live PayMongo status instead of being rejected — meaning a chargeable-but-unclaimed source could in principle be credited to whichever owner_id the caller supplied, not necessarily who actually paid. Low real-world severity (PayMongo source IDs are long random strings, not guessable) but a real logic gap in code this task was already touching, so fixed alongside rather than left as pre-existing.
5. **Driver top-up was previously a mock, no-gateway instant credit** (`drivers/driver_wallet.php`'s POST handler, still fully functional and left in place per this codebase's deprecate-in-place convention, just no longer called by any current screen) — `(driver)/dashboard.tsx`'s Wallet tab "Top Up" now runs the exact same amount-select → GCash (Demo)/Maya (Demo) → `create_source.php` → `WebBrowser.openAuthSessionAsync` checkout → `verify_payment.php` flow as the commuter wallet (`(main)/wallet.tsx`), just passing `driver_id` instead of `commuter_id`.
6. **"Demo" labeling is UI-only, not a schema/API rename** — `method` values, column names, and function names throughout the backend stay `gcash`/`paymaya` (matching this codebase's existing "Quota"-display-vs-"subscription"-schema split precedent, Core Flow 15's own note) — only user-visible text (button labels, toasts, transaction notes) says "(Demo)". A "Demo payment methods — sandbox transactions for testing, not real charges" note is shown directly above the method buttons on both wallets.
7. **Everything else about the flow is genuinely unchanged, not reimplemented**: `payment_return.php`'s redirect page needed zero changes (already owner/method-agnostic — it only deep-links to `transyncmobile://...`, which `openAuthSessionAsync` intercepts by scheme prefix, not exact path); escrow/de-duplication via `paymongo_sources.status` (`pending→credited`), atomic wallet-credit + `wallet_transactions` insert, and the retry-verification UI for a browser closed without confirming all work identically for both owners and both demo methods.
8. **Two other direct/no-gateway top-up code paths exist, both dormant** — `wallet/wallet.php`'s POST handler (a plain instant commuter credit, predates the PayMongo integration) and `drivers/driver_wallet.php`'s POST handler (the driver's own pre-PayMongo instant credit) are both left in place, fully functional, per this codebase's deprecate-in-place convention — no current screen calls either. The one genuinely dead piece was removed outright rather than deprecated-in-place: `src/services/walletService.ts`'s `topUpWallet()` wrapped `wallet.php`'s POST but had zero callers anywhere in the app (confirmed by grep) — an unused function is a foot-gun for a future accidental reintroduction of the no-gateway bypass, not a real historical/direct-caller API surface worth preserving the way an actual endpoint is.

## 16. Driver Discount-Override Scanner (Bus/UV Express boarding only)
Scoped to reservation boarding only — Jeepney live boarding (`verify_qr_board.php`/`end_ride.php`) is untouched, since no escrow exists yet at that scan point (fare isn't computed until `end_ride.php`), so there's nothing to financially correct there.

1. **`reservations.base_fare_amount`** (added migration 021) stores the pre-discount, pre-rounding fare at booking time (`create_reservation.php`) alongside the already-existing `fare_amount`. This lets the scanner reconstruct *both* the full and discounted fare exactly later — reconstructing from the already-rounded `fare_amount` alone would be lossy. Reservations created before this migration have `base_fare_amount = NULL` and simply don't support override (an acceptable gap — they're already old/settled).
2. **Two-step scan flow**: `reservations/preview_ticket.php` (GET, new) mirrors `board_reservation.php`'s exact validation (ticket exists, `reserved`, QR window, driver authorization) without mutating anything, returning `discount_type`/`commuter_category`/`commuter_is_verified`/`full_fare`/`discounted_fare`/`can_decline`/`can_grant` so the mobile scanner can decide what to show *before* committing. `board_reservation.php` then commits with an explicit `discount_decision` (`"keep"` default | `"decline"` | `"grant"`).
3. **Mobile: `(driver)/scan-ticket.tsx`** has a persistent "Discount Mode" toggle (stays set across scans — a driver may scan several ID-holding passengers in a row) plus an always-shown Accept/Decline prompt whenever a scanned ticket was already booked with a system-verified discount (`discount_type != 'default'`), independent of the toggle:
   - **Booked with discount, no ID shown → decline**: `board_reservation.php` recomputes `full_fare = round(base_fare_amount * 4) / 4`, charges the difference (`full_fare - fare_amount`) as a **surcharge** — debited from the commuter's wallet (capped at balance, same "never hard-block" convention as the rest of the app), logged as `wallet_transactions.reference_type='discount_surcharge'`. Reservation's `discount_type` resets to `'default'`, `fare_amount` becomes the full fare.
   - **Booked without discount, ID shown physically → grant** (only when the toggle is ON and the commuter's own `category` isn't `'default'`): recomputes `discounted_fare = round(base_fare_amount * 0.80 * 4) / 4`, **credits** the difference (`fare_amount - discounted_fare`) back to the commuter as a **cashback**, logged as `reference_type='discount_cashback'`. Reservation's `discount_type` becomes the commuter's own category (student/disabled/elderly), `fare_amount` becomes the discounted fare.
   - Toggle ON with no valid decision available (commuter's category is `'default'`) → boards normally (`"keep"`) with no change.
4. **Remittance runs on the corrected fare**: `creditRemittanceSplit()` is called with the *final* (possibly surcharge/cashback-adjusted) `fare_amount`, not the original booking-time one — this is "the money event," consistent with the existing rule that remittance reflects the actual settled amount (Do Not Break rule 19).
5. **Double-payment guard extends to discount decisions**: any `discount_decision` other than `"keep"` is rejected on a `payment_status === 'released'` ticket (an operator-rebooked, pre-settled reservation) — protects the existing no-show/rebook double-payment guard (rule 29) from being reopened by a discount adjustment.

## 17. Button Labeling Audit — Commuter/Driver/Operator/Association/Admin (2026-08-06)
A full pass across every app converting icon-only action buttons to icon+visible-text, superseding Do Not Break rule #67's original accessibility-only baseline.

1. **Inventory method**: the prior accessibility pass (rule #67) had already catalogued nearly every icon-only control in the mobile app via `accessibilityLabel` — grepping for it gave a near-complete, pre-labeled inventory (72 controls across 25 files) to work from directly, rather than re-discovering everything from scratch. The admin panel (plain JS, no `accessibilityLabel` convention) was separately audited by hand: 13 in-scope controls across 6 pages, plus an existing icon+text style already established there (sidebar "Log out", "Add Route", `VerifyUsers.jsx`'s `ActionButton`/`DocButton`) that the new labels were made to match.
2. **A deliberately exempted category of embedded input micro-affordances was kept icon-only** (confirmed with the user before starting, since this materially changed scope): password show/hide eye toggles, search-field clear-X buttons, date/hour/minute/row/column stepper arrows (‹/›), the admin panel's sidebar hamburger toggle (present on every page), the login page's remember-me checkbox (already has an adjacent visible "Remember me" text sibling — not actually unlabeled), and `DocumentViewerModal`'s prev/next document arrows (edge-positioned circular overlay buttons, matching the universal media-gallery convention). These already carry `accessibilityLabel` for screen readers and were judged to be well-established, universally-understood conventions where adding permanent visible text would look broken (e.g. text next to every arrow of an hour/minute stepper) rather than clearer. Every other icon-only control — Edit, Delete, Close, Add, View, Back, Save/Remove, Center-on-my-location FABs, etc. — got a real visible label.
3. **Layout risk was handled by widening/restructuring, never by hiding text**, per the explicit requirement: fixed-size square icon buttons (e.g. 34×34 Edit/Delete pairs) became auto-width pills with `flexDirection:"row"` + icon + `Text`; rows with a `flex:1` sibling (a name, a status pill, a title) absorbed the width difference automatically; the one row with no slack at all — `(association)/route-builder.tsx`'s route-library list item (View/Edit/Delete all three needed text) — was restructured from a single horizontal row into two stacked rows (name+meta on top, right-aligned actions below) rather than cramming three labeled buttons into one line. Same treatment for `(association)/fare.tsx`'s per-class fare row (input + Save + Remove) — split into a name row and an actions row.
4. **One real icon/behavior mismatch was found and fixed, not just labeled**: `(main)/home.tsx` and `(operator)/dashboard.tsx` both had a bell ("notifications") icon that actually navigated to Profile — this app has no notification inbox at all. Labeling it "Notifications" next to a bell would have been accurately-labeled-but-still-misleading (a technically-true label describing the icon, not the destination), so the icon itself was swapped to a profile/person icon alongside the "Profile" label, matching what the button actually does — consistent with the task's own instruction that "the label must accurately reflect the button's behavior."
5. **A few `title=` attributes in the admin panel (hover-only tooltips) were upgraded to always-visible text**, since a tooltip that only appears on mouse hover doesn't satisfy "visible label" on touch devices or at a glance — e.g. `RouteLibrary.jsx`'s Edit/Delete/Fullscreen buttons. `RouteTerminalManagement.jsx`'s fullscreen toggle additionally gained the same dynamic "Fullscreen"/"Exit Fullscreen" text its `RouteLibrary.jsx` twin already had via icon-swapping, closing a small pre-existing inconsistency between the two nearly-identical screens.
6. **Verification**: mobile app type-checked clean (`tsc --noEmit`) after every batch of edits, not just once at the end; admin panel verified via a real `vite build` (clean, only a pre-existing unrelated chunk-size warning). Neither app was run on a device/simulator — this environment can't do that — so the *visual* result (exact wrapping/spacing on a real screen) wasn't eyeballed, only reasoned about from each container's actual flex layout (`flex:1` siblings, existing `gap`/gap-x paddings) before deciding a given row had room.

---

# API Structure

All endpoints: `POST /TranSync-App/transync-backend/api/{domain}/{file}.php`
Mobile base URL: `http://192.168.1.122/TranSync-App/transync-backend/api`
Admin base URL via Vite proxy: `/api` → `http://localhost/TranSync-App/transync-backend/api`

## Auth
| File | Method | Purpose |
|------|--------|---------|
| `auth/login_mobile.php` | POST | Multi-role login |
| `auth/login.php` | POST | Admin login |
| `auth/commuter_register.php` | POST (multipart) | Commuter signup + wallet creation |
| `auth/operator_register.php` | POST (multipart) | Operator signup — name/email/phone/password + one required `registration_document` upload (valid ID/business permit); no vehicle type, no association, no vehicle-type-specific documents (those move to `operators/apply_to_association.php` later, see Core Flow 8) |
| `auth/association_register.php` | POST (multipart) | Association signup |
| `auth/verify.php` | GET/POST | Fetch pending / approve / reject |
| `auth/resubmit.php` | POST (multipart) | Resubmit docs after 48h cooldown |
| `auth/commuter_register_student.php` | POST (multipart) | Student registration — OCR/face-match/liveness auto-verification, see Core Flow 2a |
| `auth/commuter_renew_student.php` | POST (multipart) | Existing Student commuter renews a lapsed verification — same checks, hard binary pass/fail (no soft-review path) |
| `auth/commuter_register_senior.php` | POST (multipart) | Senior Citizen registration — OCR/face-match/liveness auto-verification via the same 4-outcome classifier the category-switch flow uses; see Core Flow 2a |
| `auth/commuter_register_pwd.php` | POST (multipart) | PWD registration — OCR/face-match/liveness auto-verification via the same 4-outcome classifier; PWD's own Validity Date becomes this row's `expires_at` (renewable, unlike Senior), see Core Flow 2a |
| `auth/commuter_renew_pwd.php` | POST (multipart) | Existing PWD commuter renews a lapsed verification — same 4-outcome classifier as registration (not Student's binary renewal), see Core Flow 2a |
| `auth/commuter_switch_category.php` | POST | Already-logged-in commuter switches to `default`/`disabled`/`elderly` once their current category has expired — see Core Flow 2a |
| `auth/check_student_id_ocr.php` | POST (multipart) | Pre-check right after Student ID capture — probes whether an Enrollment Form will be needed, writes nothing to DB |
| `auth/current_academic_period.php` | GET | Server-computed current academic year/semester, auto-fills the Student wizard's read-only field |

## Org Hierarchy (Operator → Association per vehicle type; driver accounts are operator-created, not self-service)
| File | Method | Purpose |
|------|--------|---------|
| `operators/apply_to_association.php` | POST (multipart) | Operator applies for ONE vehicle type at an association — `{operator_id, association_id, vehicle_type, ...doc_* files}`; blocks a duplicate pending/approved application for the same vehicle_type only (see Core Flow 8.2/8a) |
| `operators/association_status.php` | GET | List — one entry per vehicle type the operator has ever applied for (`{applications: [{vehicle_type, association_id, association_name, status}, ...]}`), not a single association |
| `operators/leave_association.php` | POST | Operator-initiated leave — now requires `vehicle_type`, sets that one application's `status='removed'` |
| `operators/get_profile.php` | GET | Operator Profile/Settings/Verification — live `id/name/email/phone/operator_type/is_verified/status/rejected_at` (Core Flow 8d) |
| `operators/update_profile.php` | POST | Operator Settings → Account Information — `password` always required, optional `name`/`email`/`phone` (Core Flow 8d) |
| `operators/change_password.php` | POST | Operator Settings → Privacy & Security — current-password-gated password change (Core Flow 8d) |
| `operators/update_avatar.php` | POST (multipart) | Profile Picture upload/replace/remove — `{operator_id, avatar file}` or `{operator_id, remove:1}` (Core Flow 8f) |
| `associations/pending_operator_applications.php` | GET | Association's incoming operator applications — returns `vehicle_type` + the per-type document URLs (see Core Flow 8a) plus the operator's registration-time `registration_document_url` |
| `associations/review_operator_application.php` | POST | Association approves/rejects an operator application — approval is rejected if the admin hasn't verified the operator yet (see Core Flow 8); no longer touches any column on `operators`, only auto-rejects other pending applications for the *same* vehicle_type |
| `associations/kick_operator.php` | POST | Association removes a member operator for ONE vehicle type (`vehicle_type` required) — clears that type's fleet route assignments only, `status='removed'` |
| `associations/association_members.php` | GET | Member operators (one row per operator, `vehicle_types` array of what's approved *at this association*), each with drivers nested and filtered to those vehicle types |
| `associations/service_types.php` | GET/POST/DELETE | Association's Bus class catalog (Ordinary/Deluxe/Royal Class) — association owns this, operators only read it |
| `operators/get_association_service_types.php` | GET | Read-only: operator's own *Bus-approved* association's class catalog (for the vehicle registration picker) |
| `associations/bus_schedules.php` | GET/POST/PUT/DELETE | Association's recurring Bus schedule templates (route+class+boarding/departure time+daily/weekly recurrence) — see Core Flow 12a. PUT does a full field edit if any of route_id/service_type_id/boarding_time/departure_time/recurrence_type is present, else a quick status-only toggle |
| `operators/set_remittance_mode.php` | GET/POST | Operator's own Bus/UV remittance cut — `quota` (0%, settle manually) or `percentage` (own `operator_fee_pct`, auto-deducted) — see Core Flow 12 |
| `operators/set_class_details.php` | POST | Operator upserts their own amenities/details overlay for one Bus class (`{operator_id, service_type_id, details}`) — never writes to `bus_service_types` (see Core Flow 13) |
| `operators/get_uv_queue_status.php` | GET | Read-only: the current `dispatched` driver + `waiting` list for a `(operator_id, route_id)` UV Express queue |
| `operators/advance_uv_queue.php` | POST | Operator-triggered `dispatched→completed` / `waiting→dispatched` advance on the same route-scoped `dispatch_queue` rows a driver can self-trigger via `start_trip.php` |
| `associations/uv_operating_hours.php` | GET/POST/PUT/DELETE | Association's UV Express schedule per route — operating hours + recurrence (daily\|weekly, one row per route, UNIQUE `route_id`) — see Core Flow 8b. POST creates (rejects if the route already has a row); PUT does a full edit (`start_time`/`end_time`/`recurrence_type`/`weekdays`) or a quick status-only toggle, same payload-shape convention as `bus_schedules.php` |
| `associations/jeepney_operating_hours.php` | GET/POST/PUT/DELETE | Association's daily operating hours per Jeepney route — same shape as `uv_operating_hours.php` minus recurrence (flat daily hours only), purely informational, see Core Flow 8g |
| `associations/get_profile.php` | GET | Association Profile/Settings — live `id/name/acronym/email/phone/address/status/created_at` (Core Flow 8c) |
| `associations/update_association.php` | POST | Association Settings → Association Information — `name` always required (Profile's inline edit only ever sends this); optional `acronym`/`contact_email`/`contact_phone`/`address`; `password` required only when email or phone changes (Core Flow 8c) |
| `associations/change_password.php` | POST | Association Settings → Privacy & Security — current-password-gated password change (Core Flow 8c) |
| `associations/update_avatar.php` | POST (multipart) | Profile Picture upload/replace/remove — `{association_id, avatar file}` or `{association_id, remove:1}` (Core Flow 8f) |
| `operators/uv_schedule_activation.php` | GET/POST | Operator's activate/deactivate toggle on an association-published UV schedule — GET merges the schedule list with this operator's own `activation_status`; POST validates the operator's UV Express membership then upserts. See Core Flow 8b |

## Commuter
| File | Method | Purpose |
|------|--------|---------|
| `commuter/nearby_drivers.php` | GET | Jeepney drivers within 5km — also returns each driver's `route_id`/`route_name` (added for Core Flow 8g's map marker tap) |
| `commuter/nearby_commuters.php` | GET | Nearby commuters |
| `commuter/update_commuter_location.php` | POST | Update GPS + looking status |
| `commuter/nearby_drivers_by_destination.php` | GET | Drivers toward destination |
| `commuter/get_profile.php` | GET | A commuter's current `is_verified`/`status`/`category`/`rejected_at`/`name`/`email`/`phone`/`created_at` — powers `UserContext`'s live re-sync (see Core Flow 2 point 7) so an admin verifying/rejecting a commuter mid-session shows up without a re-login. Applies the same rejected-but-resubmittable override as `login_mobile.php`'s commuter branch |
| `commuter/change_password.php` | POST | Settings → Privacy & Security — current-password-gated password change (Core Flow 2b) |
| `commuter/update_contact.php` | POST | Settings → Account Information — current-password-gated Name (First/Middle/Last/Suffix)/Email/Phone update, format + cross-table duplicate checks (Core Flow 2b) |
| `commuter/update_avatar.php` | POST (multipart) | Profile Picture upload/replace/remove — `{commuter_id, avatar file}` or `{commuter_id, remove:1}`, deletes the previous file from disk (Core Flow 8f) |
| `commuter/get_consent_history.php` | GET | Read-only `consent_acceptances` history for this commuter — endpoint still works, no longer surfaced in the current Settings UI (Core Flow 2b) |
| `commuter/deactivate_account.php` | POST | **Dormant** — password-gated reversible deactivation, superseded in the UI by `delete_account.php`; left in place, no current screen calls it (Core Flow 2b) |
| `commuter/delete_account.php` | POST | Settings → Delete Account — password-gated **permanent** soft-delete: anonymizes PII, sets `deleted_at`; wallet/booking/verification history is preserved untouched (Core Flow 2b) |

## Drivers
| File | Method | Purpose |
|------|--------|---------|
| `drivers/drivers.php` | GET/POST/PUT/DELETE | Driver CRUD — POST is the only way driver accounts get created (operator sets credentials directly); PUT default action toggles `status`, `action=update_details` edits name/email/phone/license/password/assigned vehicle (see Core Flow 14) |
| `drivers/update_driver_location.php` | POST | Update GPS/heading/online status |
| `drivers/get_driver_route.php` | GET | Driver's assigned route + waypoints |
| `drivers/get_profile.php` | GET | Driver Settings/Verification — live `id/name/email/phone/license_number/status/operator_id/operator_name/operator_type/association_id/association_name` plus the assigned vehicle's `vehicle_number`/`plate_number` (Core Flow 8e) |
| `drivers/update_profile.php` | POST | Driver Settings → Account Information — `password` always required, optional `name`/`email`/`phone` (driver self-service, distinct from `drivers.php`'s operator-side `update_details`; Core Flow 8e) |
| `drivers/change_password.php` | POST | Driver Settings → Privacy & Security — current-password-gated password change (Core Flow 8e) |
| `drivers/update_avatar.php` | POST (multipart) | Profile Picture upload/replace/remove — `{driver_id, avatar file}` or `{driver_id, remove:1}` (Core Flow 8f) |
| `drivers/driver_summary.php` | GET | Daily trips/earnings — `trip_count` is completed `dispatch_queue` cycles today for **every** vehicle type (the one Dispatch-tab flow shared identically by Jeepney/UV Express/Bus); `total_earnings` is Bus/UV Express only, today's driver `wallet_transactions` credits (`reference_type='booking'`) — Jeepney has no per-ride app earnings by design. Found and fixed twice 2026-08-10, see Core Flow 8e's note |
| `drivers/earnings_history.php` | GET | Driver Profile → Earnings card → "View Earnings History" — per-day breakdown (date/trip_count/total_earnings) of the same booking-credit ledger `driver_summary.php`'s "Today" reads from, last 30 days, newest first, plus a 30-day grand total. Bus/UV Express only in practice (Jeepney never writes this reference_type). Added 2026-08-10 |
| `drivers/get_driver_applications.php` | GET | **Legacy** — old per-driver association status, superseded by the Org Hierarchy endpoints above |

## Queue & Trips
| File | Method | Purpose |
|------|--------|---------|
| `queue/join_queue.php` | POST | Join dispatch queue (waiting) — rejects a Jeepney driver with an overdue platform subscription for the current period (see Core Flow 15) |
| `queue/leave_queue.php` | POST | Exit queue |
| `queue/queue_position.php` | GET | Current queue position |
| `trips/start_trip.php` | POST | waiting → dispatched |
| `trips/end_trip.php` | POST | dispatched → completed; also seeds a capacity-tiered default `driver_quotas` row the first time a Jeepney driver's shift ends |
| `trips/active_ride.php` | GET | Active ride for commuter or driver |
| `trips/end_ride.php` | POST | Complete ride + fare deduction (atomic) |
| `trips/request_dropoff.php` | POST | Commuter requests stop |
| `trips/cancel_dropoff.php` | POST | Cancel stop request |
| `trips/latest_ride.php` | GET | Most recent ride |

## Routes
| File | Method | Purpose |
|------|--------|---------|
| `routes/routes.php` | GET/POST/PUT/DELETE | Route CRUD — legacy, operator-owned rows only; POST rejects every vehicle type now (all routes are association-only — see `save_route.php` and Core Flow 8) |
| `routes/save_route.php` | POST | Save route with JSON waypoints — requires `association_id` for **every** vehicle type now (Jeepney/UV Express/Bus), not just Bus; rejects with a clear message if only `operator_id` is supplied. `vehicle_type` is trusted from the request (the terminal's own type), never derived from the deprecated `operators.operator_type`/`associations.association_type` columns |
| `routes/get_routes.php` | GET | An operator's *usable* routes — legacy operator-owned rows (`operator_id` set, `association_id` NULL) **union** association-owned routes assigned to one of the operator's own drivers via `route_assignments`; response includes `vehicle_type`/`status` per route |
| `routes/get_all_routes.php` | GET | All active routes (public) |
| `routes/get_commuter_routes.php` | GET | Routes for commuter browse |
| `routes/get_association_routes.php` | GET | Routes for association dashboard |
| `routes/route_planner.php` | GET | Journey planner (Nominatim + Haversine) |
| `routes/assign_route.php` | POST | Operator assigns one of their own association's routes (for a driver's own vehicle type) to one of their own drivers — validates the driver belongs to the operator, has a vehicle assigned, and the route's association has an approved `operator_association_applications` row matching that vehicle's type; `action=unassign` clears it. See Core Flow 8 |
| `routes/delete_route.php` | DELETE | Delete route |
| `routes/set_service_fare.php` | GET/POST/DELETE | Per-class fare for a Bus route (writes/reads/removes `fare_matrix` rows); POST/DELETE require `association_id` and validate route+class ownership — association-only, no operator path |

## Bus Trips & UV Slots (scheduled book-ahead)
| File | Method | Purpose |
|------|--------|---------|
| `bus_trips/create_trip.php` | POST | **Legacy/dormant** — the old one-at-a-time "operator commits vehicle+driver, server computes next occurrence" flow. Left in place, fully functional, for any historical/direct caller — no current mobile screen calls it; superseded by system auto-generation (`config/trip_generation.php`) + `assign_trip.php` (see Core Flow 12a's 2026-08-02 rewrite) |
| `bus_trips/assign_trip.php` | POST | Operator assigns a vehicle + its own driver + optional conductor to an already system-generated `pending_assignment` trip — `{operator_id, trip_id, vehicle_id, driver_id, conductor_name?, conductor_phone?}`. Flips the trip to `scheduled` ("Ready/Open for Booking"); rejects if it's no longer `pending_assignment` (race-safety net) — no vehicle/driver scheduling-conflict check (Do Not Break rule #41). See Core Flow 12a |
| `bus_trips/reassign_trip.php` | POST | Operator swaps the vehicle/driver/conductor already assigned to a `scheduled` trip — same payload shape as `assign_trip.php`. Never touches `departure_time`; blocks a vehicle change (not a driver-only change) if the trip already has real bookings, since their `seat_labels` are tied to the current vehicle's seat map. See Core Flow 12a point 3a |
| `bus_trips/update_trip_status.php` | POST | `scheduled→boarding→departed→completed`, or `cancel` (auto-refunds still-reserved passengers). `cancel` from `scheduled` returns the trip to `pending_assignment` (assignment fields reset, trip never deleted, Do Not Break rule #69) instead of the old terminal `cancelled`; `cancel` from `boarding` still terminally cancels |
| `bus_trips/sweep_departed_trips.php` | POST | Idempotent forfeiture sweep for both trips and uv_slots past their QR grace period — purely time-based, not gated on trip/slot status; delegates to the shared `sweepDepartedReservations()` in `config/sweep.php` (also used by `get_reservation_status.php`) so remittance always gets credited from either call site |
| `bus_trips/get_upcoming_trips.php` | GET | Commuter browse (filtered to 24h+ out, vehicle+driver assigned, `status='scheduled'`, and the trip's own schedule active if it has one — see Core Flow 12a point 4). Fare resolution now `LEFT JOIN fare_matrix` + `COALESCE(fm.base_fare, t.fare_override, r.fare)`, mirroring `create_reservation.php`'s Bus branch exactly (Do Not Break rule #68) — previously returned only `COALESCE(t.fare_override, r.fare)`, so a Bus route with a per-class `fare_matrix` price showed the stale flat route fare on Available Trips/Trip Details right up until booking silently charged the correct (different) amount. Also returns `estimated_duration_minutes` (rough Haversine-over-waypoints ÷ flat speed, via `routes/geo_helpers.php` — see Do Not Break rule #71), `null` when the route has no usable waypoints. Optional `?operator_id=` filter (additive, backward-compatible) scopes results to one operator's fleet — used by the rebooking "Select New Trip" flow (Core Flow 9.4) |
| `bus_trips/get_operator_trips.php` / `get_driver_trips.php` / `get_trip_bookings.php` | GET | Operator/driver trip views + manifest. `get_operator_trips.php` returns only this operator's own already-assigned trips (`status != 'pending_assignment'`) — opportunistically sweeps expired pending trips and tops up every active schedule's generated window on every call (no cron in this app), but no longer returns pending trips itself, see `get_pending_trips.php`. `get_trip_bookings.php` (manifest) now also returns `rebook_requested: bool` per row (Core Flow 9a point 8) |
| `bus_trips/get_pending_trips.php` | GET | Date-scoped Pending Assignment view — `{operator_id, date?}` (`date` defaults to today, `YYYY-MM-DD`). Returns only that one calendar date's still-unclaimed `pending_assignment` trips for a schedule the operator is approved to run, sorted by `departure_time`. Runs the same opportunistic expiry-sweep + generation top-up as `get_operator_trips.php`. See Core Flow 12a point 7 |
| `uv_slots/create_slot.php` | POST | **Dormant/legacy** — operator-manual slot creation, superseded by commuter-triggered `resolve_slot.php` (see Core Flow 8b); still works for any historical direct caller but nothing in the mobile UI calls it anymore |
| `uv_slots/resolve_slot.php` | POST | Commuter-triggered: finds or creates the pooled `uv_slots` row for `{route_id, departure_time}` — validates advance window, schedule recurrence/hours, and that at least one operator has activated the schedule; the created row has `operator_id`/`capacity` both `NULL` (route-level, uncapped, pooled across every active operator). See Core Flow 8b |
| `uv_slots/update_slot_status.php` | POST | Same lifecycle as Bus trips; an operator is authorized by exact legacy ownership OR an active schedule activation for that route; a driver is authorized by being currently dispatched for that route (any operator) — see Core Flow 8b |
| `uv_slots/get_available_slots.php` / `get_operator_slots.php` / `get_slot_bookings.php` / `get_slot_passengers.php` | GET | Commuter/operator/driver views — both listing endpoints treat `capacity IS NULL` as unbounded and return `capacity`/`seats_left` as `null` (not `0`) for pooled slots; `get_operator_slots.php` also includes any route the operator has an active schedule activation on, not just slots it strictly owns. `get_available_slots.php` also hides a pooled slot the instant every operator deactivates it (or the association deactivates the schedule itself) — see Core Flow 8b point 7. Also returns `estimated_duration_minutes` (same method as `get_upcoming_trips.php`, Do Not Break rule #71). Optional `?operator_id=` filter on `get_available_slots.php` (additive) — same purpose as `get_upcoming_trips.php`'s, naturally excludes pooled slots when set (Core Flow 9.4). `get_slot_bookings.php` (manifest) also returns `rebook_requested: bool` per row (Core Flow 9a point 8) |
| `routes/geo_helpers.php` | — | Not an endpoint — a shared include (`haversineKm`/`wpLat`/`wpLng`/`polylineKmBetween`/`estMinutes`/`fullRouteKm`) required by `get_upcoming_trips.php`/`get_available_slots.php` for their `estimated_duration_minutes` field. `routes/route_planner.php` keeps its own original local copies of the same functions, not refactored to use this file (Do Not Break rule #71) |
| `routes/get_uv_schedule.php` | GET | Commuter-facing: a UV Express route's schedule (operating hours + recurrence) plus `is_bookable` (whether any operator has actually activated it) — powers the book-ahead date/time picker in `(main)/book.tsx`. See Core Flow 8b |
| `routes/get_jeepney_operating_hours.php` | GET | Commuter/driver-facing: a Jeepney route's published operating hours (`{start_time,end_time}` or `null`) — purely informational, no booking/dispatch effect. Used by `(main)/map.tsx`'s driver marker tap and `(driver)/dashboard.tsx`'s Dispatch tab. See Core Flow 8g |

## Reservations (Bus/UV booking, refund/rebooking, escrow, seats)
| File | Method | Purpose |
|------|--------|---------|
| `reservations/create_reservation.php` | POST | Book a trip/session/slot — enforces advance window (24h+, both Bus and UV Express), discount dedup, mandatory seat pick (Bus), service-type fare override, escrow `held` default |
| `reservations/preview_ticket.php` | GET | Read-only pre-scan lookup (Bus/UV only) — same validation as `board_reservation.php` without mutating; returns discount context (`can_decline`/`can_grant`/full/discounted fare) for the driver scanner's accept/decline/grant prompt (see Core Flow 16), plus (additive) `is_rebooked`/`original_fare_amount`/`rebooking_fee_amount`/`additional_fare_due` when the scanned ticket is a continuation of an earlier rebooked-away booking (Core Flow 9.4) — kept in sync with `board_reservation.php`'s own copy of these fields |
| `reservations/board_reservation.php` | POST | QR scan → boards + releases escrow with the automatic driver/operator/system remittance split (skipped if `payment_status` is already `released` — see Core Flow 12); accepts optional `discount_decision` (`keep`\|`decline`\|`grant`) to override the discount at boarding, charging a surcharge or crediting a cashback accordingly (see Core Flow 16). Also returns the same rebooking-context fields as `preview_ticket.php` when applicable |
| `reservations/request_refund.php` | POST | **Single** commuter-facing cancellation endpoint (rewritten 2026-08-11, simplified 2026-08-12) — computes a two-tier refund percentage (100%/50%, no grace period, see Core Flow 9 point 2) via `config/refund.php`, credits whatever portion is refunded to the wallet, and settles whatever portion isn't via the normal remittance split (Core Flow 9 point 3) |
| `reservations/cancel_late.php` | POST | **Deprecated in place** — frozen at an earlier refund policy (binary 100%/0% cutoff, no 50% tier) for any historical/direct caller; superseded by `request_refund.php`, no longer called by any current screen |
| `reservations/request_rebook.php` | POST | Commuter-only — `{commuter_id, reservation_id}`. Requests a rebook while eligible (`isRebookEligible()` — still `reserved` and not yet departed, or `no_show` within `NO_SHOW_REBOOK_REQUEST_GRACE_HOURS`, see Core Flow 9.4); inserts a `pending` `rebook_requests` row (migration 035/047). Never rebooks anything itself — only surfaces a badge/Approve-Reject on the operator's own manifest. Rejects a duplicate pending request for the same booking |
| `reservations/operator_review_rebook.php` | POST | Operator-only — `{operator_id, reservation_id, action:'approve'\|'reject', rejection_reason?}` (reason required to reject). Grants/denies permission only — no reservation created, no money moved. See Core Flow 9.4 |
| `reservations/complete_rebook.php` | POST | Commuter-only, reachable only once approved — `{commuter_id, reservation_id, new_trip_id?, new_uv_slot_id?, new_seat_label?}` (`reservation_id` = the original booking). Picks the new trip/seat (same operator's fleet only), charges the 10% fee (split system/operator/driver as of 2026-08-12, not credited to the operator outright), settles the old reservation, creates the new one. See Core Flow 9.4 |
| `reservations/operator_rebook.php` | POST | **Deprecated in place** (2026-08-10) — superseded by `operator_review_rebook.php` + `complete_rebook.php`. Left functional for any historical/direct caller; no current screen calls it |
| `reservations/reject_rebook.php` | POST | **Deprecated in place** — superseded by `operator_review_rebook.php`'s `action='reject'`. Left functional; no current screen calls it |
| `reservations/get_reservation_status.php` | GET | Live status + QR expiry + grace countdown for one booking; runs the shared, remittance-paying sweep (`config/sweep.php`) opportunistically — time-based, not gated on the trip/slot's own status |
| `reservations/get_available_seats.php` | GET | Per-trip seat availability (Bus only — UV slots have no fixed vehicle); each seat carries a derived `state` (`available\|booked\|blocked\|driver\|conductor`) driving the commuter UI's 5-color scheme directly |
| `reservations/get_commuter_reservations.php` | GET | Commuter's bookings — `?scope=upcoming` (default, unchanged) active only, soonest-departure first; `?scope=all` every booking regardless of status, newest-created first, capped 100 (Booking History + Recent Activity source, see Core Flow 9a). Both scopes now also return `seat_labels`, `payment_status`, `cancellation_type`, `resolved_at`, plus (for the Booking Details screen, Core Flow 9a point 8) `base_fare_amount`, `passenger_phone`/`passenger_age`, Bus-only `vehicle_plate`/`vehicle_type`/`driver_name`, `estimated_duration_minutes`, `rebook_request_status` (now 5 values, see Core Flow 9.4), `rebook_rejection_reason`, `rebooked_from_reservation_id`/`rebooked_to_reservation_id`, `rebooking_fee_amount`, `rebooking_operator_id` (derived, feeds the "Select New Trip" flow's fleet scoping), and (Core Flow 9a point 9) `vehicle_status` — the trip's/uv_slot's own lifecycle status, feeding the unified commuter-facing status label. Now also runs `sweepDepartedReservations()` opportunistically on every call (Core Flow 11.4) so an expired-but-never-polled booking moves from Upcoming to History without waiting on `get_reservation_status.php` to be the one that catches it |

## Vehicles & Service Types
| File | Method | Purpose |
|------|--------|---------|
| `vehicles/vehicles.php` | GET/POST/PUT/DELETE | Vehicle CRUD — capacity validation is per-type (Jeepney 12–36 even, UV Express 9–18 incl. driver, Bus none — see Core Flow 13); requires `vehicle_number` (all types, added migration 025) alongside `plate_number`; Bus POST requires `service_type_id` owned by the operator's association; GET response includes `assigned_routes` (read-through of the driver's `route_assignments`); `PUT action=update_details` sets `bus_number`/`service_type_id` (Bus) and, if supplied, `vehicle_number` (any type) — capacity is never settable here for Bus, see the Seat Layout Builder endpoints below; **rejects an actual class change (`409`) if the vehicle already has a seat layout** — clear it first (see Core Flow 13, Do Not Break rule #42) |
| `vehicles/seed_seats.php` | POST | "Quick Generate" — auto-fills a Bus vehicle's seat map from `{vehicle_id, capacity, layout_style?}`, reusing a `bus_layout_templates` row per `(operator_id, service_type_id, capacity)` if one exists (silent-reuse-by-key, unchanged); also writes `vehicles.capacity`. Now just a starting point inside the Seat Layout Builder, not the only way to get a layout — see below and Core Flow 13. |
| `vehicles/get_seat_layout.php` | GET | Full per-seat data for a vehicle (label/row/col/type/`is_blocked`/`has_active_booking`) — powers the Seat Layout Builder UI |
| `vehicles/save_seat_layout.php` | POST | Saves a full hand-edited seat layout (`{vehicle_id, seats[]}`) — diffs against stored seats (upsert/delete), recomputes `vehicles.capacity`; rejects (with `locked_seats`) if it would remove a seat with an active booking |
| `vehicles/list_seat_templates.php` | GET | Lists the operator's Seat Layout Library entries (`bus_layout_templates`), optionally filtered by `service_type_id` |
| `vehicles/save_seat_template.php` | POST | Snapshots a vehicle's current seats into a new named Library template (`{vehicle_id, name}`) — purely additive |
| `vehicles/apply_seat_template.php` | POST | Copies a template's seats into a vehicle's own `seats` as an **independent copy** (`{vehicle_id, template_id}`) — same active-booking removal guard as `save_seat_layout.php` |
| `vehicles/delete_seat_template.php` | POST/DELETE | Deletes a Library template — never affects vehicles that already applied it (independent copies) |
| `operators/service_types.php` | GET/POST/DELETE | **Legacy/dormant** — operator-owned class catalog, superseded by `associations/service_types.php`. Nothing in the mobile app calls this anymore. |

## Remittance & Wallet
| File | Method | Purpose |
|------|--------|---------|
| `wallet/wallet.php` | GET/POST | GET: commuter balance + last 20 transactions. POST: **legacy/dormant** direct instant top-up (no payment gateway) — predates the PayMongo integration below, left in place per this codebase's deprecate-in-place convention but no current screen calls it (see Core Flow 15a point 8) |
| `wallet/create_source.php` | POST | Creates a real PayMongo Source (`https://api.paymongo.com/v1/sources`) and returns its `checkout_url` — the one shared entry point for every top-up in the app, commuter or driver, GCash (Demo) or Maya (Demo) alike. Exactly one of `commuter_id`/`driver_id` required; `method` `gcash`\|`paymaya` (Maya is silently sent to PayMongo as `gcash` — the only source type PayMongo's live API actually accepts — while `paymongo_sources.method` still records the commuter's real choice). See Core Flow 15a |
| `wallet/verify_payment.php` | POST | Polls the created Source, charges it once `chargeable`, and credits the correct wallet (`wallets.balance` for a commuter, `drivers.wallet_balance` for a driver) inside one atomic transaction — idempotent via `paymongo_sources.status`. Rejects with 404 if the given `(source_id, owner)` pair has no matching row (ownership gate, not just a dedup check — see Core Flow 15a point 4) |
| `wallet/payment_return.php` | GET | Static HTML redirect target PayMongo sends the browser to after checkout — deep-links back into the app via `transyncmobile://...`, intercepted by `expo-web-browser`'s `openAuthSessionAsync`. Owner/method-agnostic, unchanged by the driver-support work |
| `wallet/operator_wallet.php` | GET | Operator balance + transactions + withdrawal history |
| `wallet/operator_withdraw.php` | POST | Debits `operators.wallet_balance`/`operator_association_wallets` now and creates a `withdrawal_requests` row already `status='paid'`, `resolved_at=NOW()` — completes instantly, no association/admin approval step (rewritten 2026-09-20, Do Not Break rule #76) |
| `drivers/driver_wallet.php` | GET/POST | GET returns balance + last 20 transactions + last 10 `withdrawal_requests`; POST is **legacy/dormant** — the driver's own old direct instant top-up, superseded by `wallet/create_source.php`/`verify_payment.php` (Core Flow 15a), left in place but no current screen calls it |
| `drivers/driver_withdraw.php` | POST | Debits `drivers.wallet_balance` now, creates a `pending` `withdrawal_requests` row (`owner_type='driver'`) — same manual-admin-fulfillment pattern as `operator_withdraw.php` (no admin approval UI yet, matching the operator side); available uniformly to Bus/UV Express/Jeepney drivers, no vehicle-type gating. `association_id` is tagged for record-keeping only (derived live from the driver's current vehicle) — unlike an operator, a driver's `wallet_balance` is a single flat column, never split per-association, since a driver only ever has one vehicle/operator/association at a time |
| `quota/set_quota.php` / `submit_quota.php` / `get_driver_quota.php` / `get_submissions.php` / `review_submission.php` | — | The peso remittance-target pipeline ("Driver Quotas" screen's "Targets" tab, `driver_quotas`/`quota_submissions`) — optional, available for **any** vehicle type now, not Jeepney-only (see Core Flow 12/12b). `end_trip.php` seeds the default amount for Jeepney; confirming a submission (`review_submission.php`, action=confirm) credits `operators.wallet_balance` immediately — no separate manual "cash out" step |
| `quota/get_remittance_targets.php` | GET | `{operator_id, route_id}` or `{operator_id, vehicle_type, association_id?}` (route_id omitted = "Unassigned Drivers" bucket) — drivers resolved via `route_assignments` (or, unassigned, a vehicle-typed driver with no route row), each merged with their `driver_quotas` target + live period progress (same math as `get_driver_quota.php`). Powers the "Driver Quotas" screen's "Targets" tab (Vehicle Type → Route → Driver browse). See Core Flow 12b |
| `quota/remove_quota.php` | POST | `{operator_id, driver_id}` — hard-deletes that driver's `driver_quotas` row only; `quota_submissions` history is untouched (no FK between the two tables). See Core Flow 12b |
| `quota/cashout.php` | GET/POST | **Legacy/dormant** — its POST action is superseded by the auto-credit-on-confirm above; nothing calls it anymore. Left in place, not deleted, since historical `cashed_out_at` data still references it conceptually |
| `quota/tier_defaults.php` | GET/POST | View/retune the 5 Jeepney capacity-tier remittance-target defaults |
| `quota/get_route_quotas.php` / `set_route_quota.php` | GET / POST | The booking-capacity cap pipeline (`driver_route_quotas`, migration 057) — **backend-only as of 2026-09-20, no mobile UI calls either endpoint anymore** (its "Limits" tab was removed, see Core Flow 12b point 6). `{operator_id, route_id}` GET returns drivers on that route with `daily_quota`/`used_today` (Bus only, live `reservations` count); POST upserts `{operator_id, route_id, driver_id, daily_quota}`. Still actively enforced at `create_reservation.php` (Bus) and `board_reservation.php`/`preview_ticket.php` (UV Express) — any pre-existing `daily_quota` row keeps gating bookings even with no UI left to view/change/clear it. Never a peso amount — see Core Flow 12b for how this differs from the remittance target above |

## Jeepney Subscription & System Wallet
| File | Method | Purpose |
|------|--------|---------|
| `drivers/subscription_status.php` | GET | Driver's current rate + last-paid + next-due + `is_overdue`, computed live (no scheduled job) |
| `drivers/pay_subscription.php` | POST | Driver pays the current rate like a bill — debits driver wallet, credits system ledger, inserts `subscription_payments` row |
| `system/subscription_rate.php` | GET/POST | Admin views/sets the single global subscription rate (frequency daily\|weekly\|monthly + amount) |
| `system/system_wallet.php` | GET | Admin: system's live balance (`SUM(credit)-SUM(debit)` over `owner_type='system'`), breakdown by source, last 50 transactions |

## Org Hierarchy legacy (superseded, left in place unused)
| File | Notes |
|------|-------|
| `associations/get_associations.php` | Still current — active associations, e.g. for the operator apply picker |
| `associations/assign_association_driver.php` | Still current — now checks the driver's operator's `association_id` instead of `association_memberships` |
| `associations/leave_association.php`, `associations/kick_member.php` | Legacy — old per-driver association leave/kick |
| `applications/submit_application.php`, `review_application.php`, `pending_applications.php` | Legacy — old driver/vehicle-subject application flow, superseded by the Org Hierarchy table above |

## Reference (read-only, no auth distinction — same data for every role)
| File | Method | Purpose |
|------|--------|---------|
| `reference/ltfrb_fare_guides.php` | GET | Official LTFRB fare guide PDFs — optional `?vehicle_type=` filter. The only endpoint touching `ltfrb_fare_guides`; no POST/PUT/DELETE exists for this table anywhere. See Core Flow 13a |

## System (Admin)
| File | Method | Purpose |
|------|--------|---------|
| `system/overview.php` | GET | Dashboard stats (9 COUNT queries) |
| `system/users.php` | GET/DELETE | User list + delete |
| `system/terminals.php` | GET | Terminal list |
| `system/verify_qr_board.php` | POST | QR scan → create jeepney_ride |

---

# Database Schema

**DB:** `transync` | **Connection:** `transync-backend/config/db.php` (MySQLi)

## Core Tables

### `commuters`
`id, name, first_name/middle_name/last_name/suffix (nullable, added migration 043 — structured personal name, `name` itself unchanged/still populated by concatenation), date_of_birth (nullable, migration 043), email, phone, password, category (default|student|disabled|elderly), discount_id_url, avatar_url (nullable, added migration 049 — self-uploaded Profile Picture, purely cosmetic, see Core Flow 8f), status (active|inactive), deactivated_at (nullable, added migration 045 — reversible self-service deactivation; dormant, superseded in the UI by Delete Account, see Core Flow 2b), deleted_at (nullable, added migration 046 — permanent soft-delete; when set, `name`/`first_name`/`email`/`phone`/etc. have already been scrubbed to placeholders), is_verified (0|1), rejected_at, latitude, longitude, is_looking, created_at`

### `commuter_id_verifications`
Backs the automated OCR/face-match/liveness pipeline (Core Flow 2a) — one row per verification submission (registration, renewal, or category-switch attempt that reached a clean pass or `pending_review`). Category-agnostic table; only the columns relevant to a row's own `category` are populated.

`id, commuter_id (FK→commuters, ON DELETE CASCADE), category (student|disabled|elderly, default student),`
`last_name, first_name, middle_name (nullable),`
`school_name (nullable, Student only), academic_year (nullable, Student only), semester (1st|2nd|midyear, nullable, Student/college only), student_id_number (nullable, Student only), id_type (k12|college, nullable, Student only),`
`id_number (nullable, PWD/Senior only — generic ID number, deliberately separate from student_id_number), birthdate (DATE, nullable, Senior only), issuing_lgu (VARCHAR(150), nullable, PWD/Senior), date_issued (DATE, nullable, Senior only), validity_date (DATE, nullable, PWD only — added migration 042, OCR-cross-checked via `checkPwdValidity()`, source of this row's `expires_at`),`
`id_front_path, id_back_path, enrollment_form_path (nullable, Student only), selfie_center_path, selfie_left_path, selfie_right_path,`
`ocr_raw_text (nullable), ocr_match_result (JSON, nullable), face_match_score (DECIMAL(5,2), nullable), liveness_result (JSON, nullable), provider (VARCHAR(30), 'demo'|'aws_rekognition'),`
`verification_status (verified|failed|pending_review, default verified), verified_at, expires_at (nullable — Student and PWD only, both renewable; Senior rows never expire, so `commuter_switch_category.php`'s "current category expired" gate never passes for a Senior row — only a lapsed Student or lapsed PWD row can), created_at`

No DB-level `UNIQUE` on `student_id_number` or `id_number` — a legitimate renewal/resubmission needs to reinsert the same real number as a new row. Different-commuter duplicate prevention is enforced at the application level instead (`WHERE student_id_number = ? [AND commuter_id != ?]` / `WHERE id_number = ? [AND commuter_id != ?]`). See Core Flow 2a for the full pipeline this table backs, including why `id_number`'s uniqueness namespace is shared between `disabled` and `elderly` rows.

### `operators`
`id, name, first_name/middle_name/last_name/suffix (nullable, added migration 043 — structured personal name, `name` itself unchanged/still populated by concatenation), date_of_birth (nullable, migration 043), business_name (nullable, migration 043), business_address (nullable, migration 043 — dropped from the registration form by migration 044, deprecate-in-place, no longer populated for new rows), entity_type (individual|business, nullable, migration 044 — distinct from the deprecated vehicle-type `operator_type` below), business_phone (nullable, migration 044), email, phone, password, registration_document_url (nullable, added migration 023 — the one required generic ID/business-permit upload at registration, reviewed by the admin; see Core Flow 8), avatar_url (nullable, added migration 049 — self-uploaded Profile Picture, purely cosmetic, see Core Flow 8f), operator_type (Jeepney Op|UV Op|Bus Op, DEPRECATED — nullable, no longer set at registration, never read for gating — see Core Flow 8), association_id (FK→associations, DEPRECATED — same reason, one column can't represent multiple simultaneous vehicle-type memberships), route, cpc_document_url, ltfrb_document_url (both DEPRECATED — superseded by the per-vehicle-type document columns on `operator_association_applications`, see Core Flow 8a), status (active|inactive), is_verified (0|1), rejected_at, wallet_balance, remittance_mode (quota|percentage, default quota), operator_fee_pct (nullable, only meaningful in percentage mode), created_at` — `remittance_mode`/`operator_fee_pct` added migration 020, see Core Flow 12.

### `associations`
`id, name, acronym (nullable, migration 043), rep_first_name/rep_middle_name/rep_last_name/rep_suffix/rep_position (nullable, migration 043 — Authorized Representative's own person record, didn't exist before), rep_government_id_url (nullable, migration 044 — the representative's own required government ID, distinct from `document_url` which proves the org itself), contact_email, official_email (nullable, migration 043 — the org's own contact email, distinct from `contact_email`/the representative's login credential), contact_phone, official_phone (nullable, migration 043), region, address, password, document_url, supporting_document_url (nullable, migration 043 — no longer collected by the form as of migration 044, deprecate-in-place), avatar_url (nullable, added migration 049 — self-uploaded Profile Picture, purely cosmetic, see Core Flow 8f), association_type (Jeepney Op|UV Op|Bus Op, DEPRECATED — an association can have operator members of any/all vehicle types now, see Core Flow 8), status (pending|active|inactive), created_at`

### `consent_acceptances` (added migration 043)
`id, account_type (commuter|operator|association), account_id, terms_version, privacy_version, accepted_at` — polymorphic on `(account_type, account_id)`, same convention as `wallet_transactions.(owner_type, owner_id)`; no FK, since `account_id` resolves against a different table per `account_type`. One row per successful registration. Versioned so a future Terms/Privacy update can require re-acceptance without losing the history of what an account originally agreed to. Read-only via `commuter/get_consent_history.php` (Core Flow 2b) — no endpoint writes to it outside the registration flow itself (`recordConsent()` in `config/consent.php`).

### `drivers`
`id, name, email, phone, password, license_number, avatar_url (nullable, added migration 049 — self-uploaded Profile Picture, purely cosmetic, see Core Flow 8f), operator_id (FK→operators, nullable in the schema but always set in practice — every driver is created by an operator with `operator_id` provided at INSERT), association_id (FK→associations, nullable, legacy/unused — see Org Hierarchy flow), vehicle_id (FK→vehicles, nullable), status (active|inactive), is_online (0|1), is_full (0|1), latitude, longitude, heading, location_updated_at, wallet_balance, created_at`

### `operator_association_applications`
`id, operator_id (FK→operators), association_id (FK→associations), vehicle_type (Jeepney|UV Express|Bus, added migration 022 — the actual source of truth for "which association manages this operator's fleet of this type", see Core Flow 8), status (pending|approved|rejected|removed — `removed` added migration 022 for operator-leave/association-kick as a terminal state with history), rejection_reason, reviewed_at, created_at, doc_lto_cr_url/doc_capacity_spec_url/doc_engine_cert_url/doc_lto_stencil_url/doc_model_proof_url/doc_terminal_route_map_url/doc_garage_proof_url/doc_speed_limiter_url/doc_gps_cert_url/doc_pami_policy_url/doc_fare_matrix_url (all nullable, added migration 022 — only the subset relevant to this row's own `vehicle_type` is populated, see Core Flow 8a)` — per-vehicle-type operator→association applications, no longer fleet-level 1:1 (see Org Hierarchy flow).

### `driver_operator_applications` — **dropped** (migration 017)
Existed briefly to support a driver self-registration + apply-to-operator flow that was built then reverted in favor of operator-direct driver creation. Mentioned here only so the migration history makes sense — do not recreate without re-reading why it was removed.

### `vehicles`
`id, operator_id (FK→operators), type (Jeepney|UV Express|Bus), plate_number, vehicle_number (nullable, added migration 025 — generic fleet-internal identifier collected for all 3 types at registration; distinct from and additive to `bus_number`, which stays Bus-specific/deprecated-in-place-if-unused), bus_number, service_type_id (FK→bus_service_types, nullable, Bus only), capacity (nullable — NULL for a Bus vehicle until a seat layout is built; always set for Jeepney/UV Express at registration), driver_id (FK→drivers, nullable), status, created_at`. "Assigned route(s)" shown in the Vehicle Management UI is a read-through of the vehicle's assigned driver's `route_assignments` (no separate vehicle-to-route mapping) — see `vehicles/vehicles.php`'s GET join.

### `bus_service_types`
`id, operator_id (FK→operators, nullable, legacy/unused — see Core Flow 13), association_id (FK→associations, nullable — the current owner), name, description, created_at` — UNIQUE `(association_id, name)`.

### `bus_layout_templates`
`id, operator_id (FK→operators), service_type_id (FK→bus_service_types), name (nullable, added migration 025), capacity, layout_style (seater|sleeper), created_at`. The old `UNIQUE (operator_id, service_type_id, capacity)` constraint (migration 025 dropped it) has been superseded by a named **Seat Layout Library** model (see Core Flow 13) — an operator can now save multiple named templates per class/capacity and explicitly picks which to apply, instead of one silently auto-reused row per key. `seed_seats.php`'s original silent-reuse-by-key path still works unchanged for rows it creates itself (no `name`).

### `bus_layout_template_seats` (added migration 025)
`id, template_id (FK→bus_layout_templates, ON DELETE CASCADE), seat_label, seat_row, seat_col, seat_type (window|aisle|sleeper|driver|conductor|blank, blank added migration 026)`. A template's actual per-seat data — `vehicles/save_seat_template.php` snapshots a vehicle's current `seats` into a new template row here; `vehicles/apply_seat_template.php` copies these rows into a target vehicle's own `seats` as fresh, independent rows (no FK back to the template), so deleting a template or editing one vehicle's layout never affects any other vehicle that applied it.

### `seats` (formally tracked as of migration 025; pre-existed in the base schema, untracked before)
`id, vehicle_id (FK→vehicles, ON DELETE CASCADE), seat_label, seat_row, seat_col, seat_type (window|aisle|sleeper|driver|conductor|blank — widened migration 025 to add driver/conductor, migration 026 to add blank), is_blocked (added migration 025 — operator-blocked "Reserved/Saved" seat, hidden from commuter booking), is_available (legacy, unused by any code — availability is computed live from `reservations`, not stored)` — UNIQUE `(vehicle_id, seat_label)` (added migration 025; the live table lacked this constraint even though it predates migrations, which had let `seed_seats.php`'s re-seed-by-delete-then-insert pattern mask the gap — any direct upsert without it would silently duplicate rows instead of updating). A `blank` row marks the aisle path itself in a generated layout — never counted toward `vehicles.capacity`, never selectable by a commuter (see Core Flow 13).

### `operator_class_details` (added migration 024)
`id, operator_id (FK→operators), service_type_id (FK→bus_service_types), details (TEXT, nullable), updated_at` — UNIQUE `(operator_id, service_type_id)`. The operator's own amenities/details overlay on an association-owned class (see Core Flow 13) — never a write to `bus_service_types` itself.

### `bus_schedules`
`id, association_id (FK→associations), route_id (FK→routes), service_type_id (FK→bus_service_types), boarding_time (TIME), departure_time (TIME), recurrence_type (daily|weekly), weekdays (VARCHAR, comma-separated 0=Sun..6=Sat, NULL for daily), status (active|inactive), created_at` — association-owned recurring Bus schedule template, added migration 020. See Core Flow 12a. Not a bookable trip by itself — `bus_trips/create_trip.php` creates an actual `trips` row from one plus an operator's own vehicle/driver/date.

### `driver_quotas` (peso remittance target — Core Flow 12b's "Targets" tab)
`id, operator_id (FK→operators, ON DELETE CASCADE), driver_id (FK→drivers, ON DELETE CASCADE), association_id (FK→associations, ON DELETE SET NULL, added migration 031 — snapshotted for display/wallet-split reference only), amount (DECIMAL), frequency (daily|weekly, default daily), created_at, updated_at` — UNIQUE `(driver_id)`: **one target per driver, always** (not per route — a driver's current vehicle-type/route is resolved live for display via `route_assignments`/`vehicles`, never stored here). Upserted by `quota/set_quota.php`, hard-deleted by `quota/remove_quota.php` (never soft-deleted — the thing worth preserving after removal is `quota_submissions` history below, which has no FK to this table at all). Originally created by `transync-backend/sql/quota_tables.sql` (a manually-run script, not a numbered migration), `association_id` added by migration 031.

### `quota_submissions` (a driver's actual remittance payments against their `driver_quotas` target)
`id, driver_id (FK→drivers, ON DELETE CASCADE), association_id (FK→associations, ON DELETE SET NULL, added migration 031), operator_id (FK→operators, ON DELETE CASCADE), amount (DECIMAL), period_type (daily|weekly), status (pending|confirmed|rejected, default pending), note (nullable), submitted_at, reviewed_at (nullable), cashed_out_at (nullable)` — no UNIQUE constraint (a driver submits as many as they like). `submit_quota.php` inserts (debiting the driver's wallet immediately); `review_submission.php` confirms (credits the operator/association wallet, stamps `cashed_out_at`) or rejects (refunds the driver). Carries its own `amount`/`period_type`/`status` directly, independent of whatever `driver_quotas` row exists for that driver at read time — removing a target never touches this table's history (`get_submissions.php`'s `LEFT JOIN driver_quotas` just returns `quota_target: null` for an orphaned row, same as a submission whose driver never had a target).

### `driver_route_quotas` (booking-capacity cap — Core Flow 12b, migration 057, backend-only since 2026-09-20)
`id, operator_id (FK→operators, ON DELETE CASCADE), route_id (FK→routes, ON DELETE CASCADE), driver_id (FK→drivers, ON DELETE CASCADE), daily_quota (INT), created_at, updated_at` — UNIQUE `(route_id, driver_id)`: one cap per driver **per route** (unlike `driver_quotas` above, this one *is* route-scoped, since it exists specifically to gate bookings on that route). A plain integer max-bookings-per-day, never a peso amount — enforced live at `create_reservation.php` (Bus) and `board_reservation.php`/`preview_ticket.php` (UV Express), never at `driver_quotas`/`quota_submissions`. **No mobile UI reads or writes this table anymore** (its "Limits" tab on `(operator)/quota.tsx` was removed 2026-09-20) — the endpoints (`quota/get_route_quotas.php`/`set_route_quota.php`) and the booking-time enforcement are both still fully live, so an existing row keeps working exactly as before, it's just unreachable to view/set/clear from the app now. See Do Not Break rule #30a for why these two tables must never be conflated.

### `subscription_rates`
`id, frequency (daily|weekly|monthly), amount, updated_at` — single global row (POST overwrites rather than versions), admin-tunable via `system/subscription_rate.php`. `daily` added migration 020. See Core Flow 15.

### `subscription_payments`
`id, driver_id (FK→drivers), amount, frequency (daily|weekly|monthly), period_start, period_end, paid_at` — one row per Jeepney driver subscription payment per calendar-aligned period; `drivers/subscription_status.php`/`pay_subscription.php` match against the exact current `period_start` (computed via `config/subscription.php`'s `currentSubscriptionPeriod()`) rather than comparing against `period_end`, since periods are calendar-aligned (midnight/Monday/1st-of-month), not rolling windows from last payment. See Core Flow 15.

### `routes`
`id, name, from_location, to_location, fare, vehicle_type, color, waypoints (JSON array of {lat,lng}), operator_id (FK, nullable), association_id (FK, nullable), status (active|inactive), created_at` — `operator_id`-only rows are legacy (see Core Flow 8.11); current route creation always sets `association_id`, for every vehicle type.

### `route_assignments`
`id, route_id (FK→routes), driver_id (FK→drivers), assigned_at` — no `vehicle_id` column; a vehicle's "assigned route" is always a read-through of its currently-bound driver's assignment. A driver can only have one active row (upserted via `ON DUPLICATE KEY`, see `routes/assign_route.php`).

### `uv_operating_hours`
`id, association_id (FK→associations), route_id (FK→routes, UNIQUE), start_time (TIME), end_time (TIME), recurrence_type (daily|weekly, added migration 028), weekdays (VARCHAR, comma-separated 0=Sun..6=Sat, NULL for daily, added migration 028), status (active|inactive), created_at` — the association's UV Express schedule (route + operating window + recurrence), added migration 027, recurrence added migration 028. See Core Flow 8b.

### `jeepney_operating_hours` (added migration 051)
`id, association_id (FK→associations), route_id (FK→routes, UNIQUE), start_time (TIME), end_time (TIME), status (active|inactive), created_at` — a Jeepney route's daily operating window, structurally the simplest sibling of `uv_operating_hours` (no `recurrence_type`/`weekdays` — flat daily hours only). Purely informational: nothing in the dispatch-queue, route-planner, or fare code paths reads this table. See Core Flow 8g.

### `uv_schedule_activations`
`id, operator_id (FK→operators), uv_operating_hours_id (FK→uv_operating_hours), status (active|inactive), created_at` — `UNIQUE (operator_id, uv_operating_hours_id)`, added migration 028. An operator's activate/deactivate toggle on an association-published UV schedule; multiple operators can independently activate the same schedule. See Core Flow 8b.

### `uv_slots` (see also migration 005/012, predates this schema list's backfill cutoff)
`id, route_id (FK→routes), operator_id (FK→operators, nullable as of migration 028), departure_time (DATETIME), capacity (INT, nullable as of migration 028), seats_booked, status (scheduled|boarding|departed|completed|cancelled), created_at`. `operator_id`/`capacity` are `NULL` for a commuter-triggered pooled slot (route-level, uncapped) — only a legacy operator-created slot (predating migration 028) still carries real values in both. See Core Flow 8b.

### `dispatch_queue`
`id, driver_id (FK→drivers), operator_id (FK→operators), status (waiting|dispatched|completed), joined_at, started_at, ended_at`

### `jeepney_rides`
`id, commuter_id (FK→commuters), driver_id (FK→drivers), status (active|dropoff_requested|completed|cancelled), board_lat, board_lng, board_time, dropoff_lat, dropoff_lng, dropoff_time, distance_km, fare_amount, discount_type, created_at`

### `wallets`
`id, commuter_id (FK→commuters UNIQUE), balance (DECIMAL)`

### `wallet_transactions`
`id, owner_type (commuter|driver), owner_id, type (debit|credit), amount, reference_type (jeepney_ride|topup), reference_id (nullable), balance_after, note, created_at`

### `paymongo_sources` (owner column widened migration 037)
`id, source_id (VARCHAR UNIQUE, PayMongo's src_... id), commuter_id (FK→commuters, nullable), driver_id (FK→drivers, nullable — added migration 037), amount, method (gcash|paymaya), payment_id (nullable, set once charged), status (pending|credited|failed), created_at`.
