66. **`reassign_trip.php` (swap vehicle/driver/conductor on an already-`scheduled` trip) blocks a vehicle change if the trip already has real bookings, but always allows a driver-only change** — a seat_label is tied to a specific vehicle's seat map, so swapping the vehicle under a trip with `reservations.boarding_status IN ('reserved','boarded')` would orphan those commuters' seats against a different layout. The driver/conductor have no seat-map dependency, so they can be swapped freely regardless of existing bookings. Scoped to `status = 'scheduled'` only (not boarding/departed/completed/cancelled) — once boarding starts, the assigned vehicle is already the one passengers are lining up for. The trip's `departure_time` is never editable here either (still owned by the System/schedule, rule #58).
70. **There is no manual Passenger Type/discount selector anywhere on the Passenger Info screen (`PassengerForm.tsx`) — a prior version had one (informational-only) and it was explicitly removed on request.** The Fare Breakdown's Discount/Total are computed purely from `commuterProfile.isVerified && commuterProfile.category` (derived in `book.tsx` from `UserContext`'s live `is_verified`/`category`, Core Flow 2 point 7 — no longer a one-time AsyncStorage snapshot, so this preview now also updates if the commuter gets verified mid-session), with zero UI control over it — no chips, no picker, nothing tappable that resembles choosing a type. Every real discount in this app is derived exactly one way: server-side in `create_reservation.php`, from the commuter's own admin-verified `commuters.category`/`is_verified` (see Core Flow 9.5) — nothing client-supplied has ever been trusted for this, and that's deliberate (an admin-verification-gated discount is the whole point of the verify/resubmit flow, and of the driver discount-override scanner's existence as the "physical ID shown but not pre-verified" pathway, Core Flow 16). Don't reintroduce a selector of any kind here, informational or otherwise — that conversation already happened twice (once resulting in "informational only," then a full removal) and re-adding one needs the same conversation again. Relatedly: this form's Fare Breakdown has a permanent "Booking Fee: ₱0" line — this app charges exactly the resolved fare with nothing added on top (confirmed by reading `create_reservation.php`'s debit logic directly), so don't ever make that line nonzero without also actually changing what the backend charges; a UI total that doesn't match the real charge is the same class of bug Do Not Break rule #68 was written to prevent. Also don't add a "GCash"/"Maya" option to the Payment Method Preview as if it pays for the booking directly — this app's PayMongo integration (`wallet/create_source.php`) only ever tops up the internal wallet balance; every booking is charged from that wallet balance alone, with no parallel per-booking payment gateway.
71. **A Bus/UV trip's "estimated arrival"/"travel time" shown on the Passenger Info screen is a rough Haversine-over-waypoints ÷ flat-speed estimate, computed server-side, not real trip-duration data** — this schema still has no actual duration/arrival-time column anywhere (Do Not Break rule #65 is unchanged), so `get_upcoming_trips.php`/`get_available_slots.php` instead compute `estimated_duration_minutes` from the route's own `waypoints` JSON using the exact same method and per-vehicle-type speed constants (Jeepney 20/UV Express 35/Bus 30 km/h) that `routes/route_planner.php` already uses (Core Flow 6) — extracted into `routes/geo_helpers.php` so both call sites share one implementation (`route_planner.php` itself was left using its own original local copies, not refactored, to avoid any risk to that already-working feature). A route with no/invalid waypoints yields `null`, never `0` — the client must show "—", not a fabricated instant/zero-duration trip. Don't compute or display a specific arrival *clock time* from anything other than `departure_time + estimated_duration_minutes`, and don't present it as more precise than "rough estimate, not live traffic/ETA."
69. **Cancelling a Bus trip before boarding starts must return it to `pending_assignment`, never delete it or leave it terminally `cancelled`** — `bus_trips/update_trip_status.php`'s `cancel` action branches on the trip's status *at the moment of cancellation*: from `scheduled` it resets every assignment-owned field (`operator_id`/`vehicle_id`/`driver_id`/`conductor_name`/`conductor_phone`/`capacity` → `NULL`, `seats_booked` → `0`) and sets `status='pending_assignment'`, reusing the exact same refund logic (every still-`reserved` passenger gets refunded first) either way; from `boarding` it keeps the pre-existing terminal `status='cancelled'` outcome with the trip's assignment left intact. Never skip the refund step before resetting to pending — an unresolved `held` reservation left pointing at a since-unassigned trip (no vehicle, no seat map) would be a real data-integrity break, not just a UX gap. `route_id`/`schedule_id`/`departure_time`/`boarding_time`/`fare_override` are never touched by this reset — only what an operator's own assignment set. Don't extend this branch to UV Express (`uv_slots/update_slot_status.php`'s `cancel` is unrelated and untouched — a UV slot has no per-assignment vehicle/driver ownership to "return" in the first place, see Core Flow 8b) or to `complete`/`depart` (those stay exactly as they were).
68. **Every place that resolves a Bus trip's displayed fare must apply the same `fare_matrix` precedence `create_reservation.php` uses to actually charge it**: `base_fare` (class-specific, association-set via `routes/set_service_fare.php`) beats `trips.fare_override` beats `routes.fare` (the flat legacy column) — `COALESCE(fm.base_fare, t.fare_override, r.fare)`, keyed on `(route_id, vehicle's service_type_id)`. This was a real bug: `get_upcoming_trips.php` only ever computed `COALESCE(t.fare_override, r.fare)`, skipping the `fare_matrix` lookup entirely, so a class-priced Bus route showed the flat route fare on Available Trips/Trip Details/the passenger form while `create_reservation.php` silently charged the correct, different, class-specific amount at booking time. UV Express has no per-class concept and is unaffected (`get_available_slots.php` and `create_reservation.php`'s UV branches both just read `routes.fare` directly, already consistent). The **Choose Route** screen (`RoutePickCard` in `(main)/book.tsx`) deliberately shows no fare at all now — a route's own flat fare is meaningless for a class-priced Bus route before a specific trip/vehicle is picked, so displaying it there was actively misleading; don't reintroduce it.
67. **Superseded 2026-08-06 (see Core Flow 17) — primary action buttons now carry a *visible* text label, not just an `accessibilityLabel`.** The original rule (added across ~20 files as a baseline accessibility pass) was: every icon-only `TouchableOpacity`/`Pressable` must carry an `accessibilityLabel` (e.g. `"Delete route"`, not `"Trash icon"`). That's now the fallback for a small, deliberately-exempted category of embedded input micro-affordances only (password show/hide, search-field clear-X, date/hour/minute/row/column stepper arrows, the admin sidebar hamburger toggle, gallery prev/next arrows) — see Core Flow 17 point 2 for exactly which. Every other icon-only control (Edit/Delete/Close/Add/View/Back/Save/Remove/etc.) must show real, always-visible text next to its icon, per Core Flow 17. When adding a *new* icon-only button: if it's a primary action, give it a visible text label from the start (don't rely on `accessibilityLabel` alone); if it's a genuine embedded micro-affordance matching the exempted category, `accessibilityLabel` alone is still acceptable. A button that already shows visible text doesn't need an additional `accessibilityLabel` (redundant).
42. **A Bus vehicle's class can never change while it has a seat layout**: `vehicles.php`'s `update_details` checks `seats` for that vehicle before accepting a different `service_type_id` and rejects (409) if any exist — a layout is built for a specific class, and letting the class label drift out of sync with the seats/capacity underneath it was an actual reported bug. The only way to change class after a layout exists is to clear it first (`save_seat_layout.php` with `seats: []`, exposed as the builder's "Clear Layout" action). Don't silently allow the class to change without also invalidating the layout, and don't auto-clear it either — the operator must explicitly clear it.
43. **Bus trip capacity always comes live from the vehicle's own seat layout, never a manual override**: `bus_trips/create_trip.php` no longer accepts a `capacity` field at all — it reads `vehicles.capacity` directly and rejects if it's unset. Don't reintroduce a capacity-override field on trip creation; if the operator wants a different capacity, they change the vehicle's seat layout, which then applies to every future trip created against that vehicle automatically.
44. **Bus trip scheduling never asks the operator for a date**: `create_trip.php` computes the next valid occurrence itself from the schedule's own recurrence (see Core Flow 12a) — don't reintroduce a date picker on the Bus "Schedule Trip" flow; that authority belongs entirely to the schedule + server-side computation now, matching the same principle already established for boarding/departure time (Do Not Break rule #34).
45. **A Bus trip's driver is never picked separately from its vehicle at trip-creation time** — it's always derived from the vehicle's own fleet-level assignment (`vehicles.driver_id`, set via `vehicles.php` `action=assign` in the Vehicles tab). This was tried the other way (a standalone driver picker in the Trips tab, letting the operator assign any active driver regardless of vehicle binding) and explicitly reverted — don't reintroduce it without the same conversation. `(operator)/trips.tsx`'s vehicle picker for Bus trip creation must keep requiring both `capacity` (a built seat layout) *and* `driver_id` (an assigned driver) on each candidate vehicle.
46. **Every place that computes a vehicle's bookable seat capacity must exclude the same three seat types**: `driver`, `conductor`, and `blank` (the aisle-path placeholder). `save_seat_layout.php`, `apply_seat_template.php`, and `save_seat_template.php` all filter on the identical `NOT IN ('driver','conductor','blank')` set — if a new seat type is ever added, or a new capacity-computing code path is written, it must exclude the same set or a vehicle's displayed capacity will silently drift from its actual bookable seat count.
47. **The operator's per-class "Add/Edit Details" editor must stay reachable independent of schedule existence** — a class can exist (defined by the association) with no `bus_schedules` row published for it yet, and the operator must still be able to attach amenity details to it. This was built once nested inside schedule selection (only reachable for a class that already had a schedule) and was a real reported bug — don't re-nest it; keep the "Your Classes" section in `(operator)/trips.tsx` populated from the full `get_association_service_types.php` list, shown regardless of which (if any) schedule is selected.
36. **One operator can hold multiple simultaneous vehicle-type memberships — never reintroduce a 1:1 assumption**: `operators.operator_type`/`operators.association_id` and `associations.association_type` are deprecated columns, kept only for historical rows, never written by current code. Every "operator's association" lookup must be scoped by `vehicle_type` via `operator_association_applications`, never assume an operator has exactly one. This was a full architectural change (see Core Flow 8) — re-collapsing it needs the same conversation, not just a quick fix.
37. **`login_mobile.php`'s `findDriver()` must keep deriving the driver's `operator_type`/`association_id`/`association_name` response fields from the driver's own assigned vehicle** (`vehicles.type` → `operator_association_applications` for that type), never from the operator's deprecated fixed `operator_type`/`association_id` columns — `(driver)/dashboard.tsx`'s entire Bus/UV/Jeepney branching (and the Jeepney-only quota card) reads these exact field names and would silently misroute or blank out for any operator registered after this change (whose legacy `operator_type` is null) if this derivation were ever reverted to the old direct column read.
38. **Operator registration must always keep its one required document upload**: `auth/operator_register.php`'s `registration_document` field (stored as `operators.registration_document_url`) was removed once (moved entirely to the association-application step) and then explicitly put back — the admin needs *something* concrete to verify the account against. Don't re-simplify registration to drop this again without the same conversation; it's separate from and doesn't replace the vehicle-type-specific documents on `operator_association_applications`.
39. **The Jeepney platform-quota mechanism's display wording ("Quota") is intentionally decoupled from its schema/API naming ("subscription")**: `subscription_rates`, `subscription_payments`, `drivers/subscription_status.php`, `drivers/pay_subscription.php`, `reference_type='subscription'`, and related type/function names in code (`SubscriptionStatus`, `handlePaySubscription`) were deliberately left unrenamed — only user-visible strings changed. Don't do a partial rename (e.g. renaming the table but not the UI, or vice versa) — keep to this split, and don't confuse this mechanism with the separate, pre-existing driver↔operator quota feature (`driver_quotas`/`quota_submissions`) that now shares the same display word by the user's explicit choice.
48. **Route creation is association-only for every vehicle type, not just Bus** — `routes/save_route.php` requires `association_id` regardless of `vehicle_type`; there is no operator-owned route-creation path anywhere in the current UI (the legacy `operator_id`-only branch only exists to not break rows that predate this). Any endpoint that looks up "the route(s) available to this operator" must resolve ownership through `operator_association_applications` (approved membership matching the route's `association_id` + `vehicle_type`) or through `route_assignments` (a route already assigned to one of the operator's own drivers) — never through `routes.operator_id`. This bit UV Express hard once already (`uv_slots/create_slot.php` and `operators/advance_uv_queue.php` both still checked `operator_id` ownership after routes moved to association-only, silently breaking UV slot scheduling for every operator) — don't reintroduce the same assumption in a new endpoint.
49. **UV operating-hours time comparisons must go through MySQL, never PHP's `date()`/`strtotime()` on a raw string** — `uv_slots/create_slot.php`/`resolve_slot.php` extract a slot's time-of-day via `SELECT TIME(?)` and `uv_sessions/go_online.php` compares against `SELECT TIME(NOW())`, both round-tripped through the DB rather than PHP's `date()` (which runs through `php.ini`'s Europe/Berlin `date.timezone`, mismatched against this server's actual MySQL clock — see the PHP/MySQL clock mismatch landmine under Critical Insights). Don't "simplify" either check to a PHP-side `date("H:i:s", $ts)` — it will silently drift by the timezone offset.
50. **The Bus seat-picking `useEffect` in `(main)/book.tsx` must stay keyed only on the selected trip (`[draft.mode, draft.selected]`), never on wizard `step`** — it used to also fire on `step` changes, and its first line unconditionally called `setSelectedSeat(null)`, wiping the commuter's already-picked seat the instant they advanced from "detail" to "passenger." `handleConfirmBooking`'s `if (draft.mode === "bus" && !selectedSeat) return;` guard then silently no-opped "Confirm Booking" with zero error feedback — a real reported bug. Don't reintroduce `step` (or anything that changes on every wizard advance) to this effect's dependency array.
51. **No-show/QR-expiry sweeping must always go through the shared `sweepDepartedReservations()` in `config/sweep.php`, never a separate inline status-flip `UPDATE`** — a second, simpler copy of this logic once lived directly in `get_reservation_status.php`, and because it didn't call `creditRemittanceSplit()`, it silently left the driver/operator/system unpaid *and* made the row invisible to the real (paying) sweep in `sweep_departed_trips.php` afterward (once `boarding_status` leaves `'reserved'`, no later sweep call can ever match it again). If a new code path needs to react to a stale `'reserved'` booking, call `sweepDepartedReservations($conn)`, don't hand-roll the status flip.
52. **QR/no-show sweeping must be purely time-based (`NOW() > departure_time + grace`), never gated on the trip/slot's own lifecycle status being `'departed'`** — a driver who never taps "Depart" must not leave a long-past-departure booking (and its QR) looking "still valid" in the app forever. `board_reservation.php`'s own scan-time date check was already correctly time-based and would reject such a scan regardless — the bug was only in the *sweep/display* side not reflecting that the booking was actually dead. Don't reintroduce a `t.status = 'departed'`/`s.status = 'departed'` condition to any sweep or grace-countdown query.
53. **A UV Express book-ahead slot's `operator_id`/`capacity` can legitimately be `NULL`** (a route-level pooled slot, Core Flow 8b) — every place that reads or compares these columns must treat `NULL` as "uncapped"/"no single owner," never coerce to `0` (which would read as "zero seats," not "unlimited") or crash on a null-vs-number comparison (`seats_booked < capacity` is `NULL`/false in SQL when `capacity` is `NULL` — always guard with `capacity IS NULL OR ...`). Boarding/lifecycle authorization for such a slot must check dispatch-queue/schedule-activation state, never assume a matching `operator_id` — see `board_reservation.php`'s and `update_slot_status.php`'s authorization checks for the reference pattern.
54. **Never compare `HH:MM` time strings with raw `>=`/`<=`/`<`/`>`** — the accepted input format allows a non-zero-padded hour (`^\d{1,2}:\d{2}$`, e.g. `"5:00"`), and PHP/JS both compare non-numeric strings byte-by-byte, so `"5:00" >= "21:00"` is **true** (`'5' > '2'`) even though 5am is obviously before 9pm. This was a real bug in `associations/uv_operating_hours.php`'s start/end validation (both POST and PUT) and the matching client-side check in `(association)/schedules.tsx` — every place that orders two `HH:MM` strings must convert to minutes-since-midnight first (see `timeToMinutes()` in both files) or otherwise parse them, never compare the raw strings directly.
54a. **`uv_operating_hours.start_time`/`end_time` is allowed to wrap past midnight (`start > end`, e.g. 23:00 → 02:00) — UV Express runs 24 hours, so this is a valid overnight window, not an error.** `uv_operating_hours.php`'s POST/PUT validation only rejects a zero-duration window (`start === end`) now, not `start >= end`. Every place that checks "is this time inside the operating window" must branch on `start <= end` (normal same-day range: `time >= start && time <= end`) vs `start > end` (overnight range: `time >= start || time <= end`, since the valid set wraps around midnight) — never assume the same-day case only. The four places that do this comparison and must stay in sync: `uv_slots/resolve_slot.php`'s bounds check, `(main)/book.tsx`'s `clampUvTime()` (snaps to whichever boundary is closer when the picked time falls in the excluded daytime gap), `(association)/schedules.tsx`'s client-side submit validation, and the backend POST/PUT validation itself (rule #54 above).
55. **A Bus trip's `operator_id`/`vehicle_id`/`driver_id`/`capacity` can legitimately be `NULL`** (a system-generated, not-yet-assigned trip, `status='pending_assignment'` — Core Flow 12a) — every endpoint that reads these must either `LEFT JOIN` (never `INNER JOIN`, which would silently drop pending trips from the very list an operator needs to assign them from — see `get_operator_trips.php`) or, for commuter-facing endpoints, rely on the existing `INNER JOIN`s to correctly exclude them (which they already do, since a pending trip's FKs don't match anything). Never coerce a `NULL` capacity to `0` (reads as "zero seats," not "not yet determined").
56. **Bus trip generation must stay idempotent without relying on a DB `UNIQUE` constraint** — this database has pre-existing duplicate `(schedule_id, departure_time)` rows from before the 2026-08-02 generation rewrite (Core Flow 12a), so `config/trip_generation.php` enforces no-duplicates via a `SELECT`-before-`INSERT` check instead. Don't add a `UNIQUE (schedule_id, departure_time)` index without first reconciling those existing duplicates — it will fail the migration.
57. **Deactivating a `bus_schedules` row must never mutate any `trips` row** — it only stops future generation and removes commuter-visibility (via the `get_upcoming_trips.php` schedule-active join check), for every trip under that schedule regardless of whether it's `pending_assignment` or already `scheduled`/boarded/etc. Reactivating must restore visibility with zero data touched either way. Don't reintroduce an auto-cancel-on-deactivate path — that was considered and explicitly rejected in favor of pure hide/show (see Core Flow 12a point 4).
58. **A Bus trip's date is only ever picked by the System (generation) or already fixed by the time an operator sees it (assignment)** — never reintroduce a date field or next-occurrence preview into the operator-facing Assign Trip flow (`bus_trips/assign_trip.php` / `(operator)/trips.tsx`'s Assign Trip modal); that authority belongs entirely to `config/trip_generation.php`'s own recurrence walk now. This supersedes the identical spirit of the older Do Not Break rule #44 (which described the now-legacy `create_trip.php` flow) without contradicting it — both agree the operator never types a date.
59. **Pending Assignment stays date-scoped — never reload it in bulk again**: `bus_trips/get_operator_trips.php` must keep excluding `status = 'pending_assignment'` entirely; any UI that needs unassigned trips must go through `bus_trips/get_pending_trips.php`'s `{operator_id, date}` contract, one calendar day at a time. This was a deliberate fix for a real scale problem (60–90+ pending rows loading on every single Bus Trips page visit once 30-day generation across multiple schedules is in play) — don't reintroduce a "just return everything" endpoint for pending trips. Expiry (`expirePastPendingTrips()`) is keyed on the whole `DATE(departure_time)`, never the exact time — don't tighten it to compare against exact `departure_time`, or a same-day trip departing later today would incorrectly vanish from Pending Assignment before its own day is even over.
60. **Never extract a local calendar date via `date.toISOString().slice(0,10)` (or any `toISOString()`-based slicing) anywhere on the client** — `toISOString()` always converts to UTC first, which silently lands on the *previous* calendar day in any timezone ahead of UTC (this app's own target timezone, Philippines, is UTC+8). This was a real bug in `(operator)/trips.tsx`'s `addDays()`/`TODAY`: `setDate()`-based local arithmetic followed by a `toISOString()` extraction canceled out almost exactly for a one-day step, so the Pending Assignment "next" button silently computed `addDays(date, 1) === date` and looked broken. The fix (`formatLocalDate()`, reading `getFullYear()`/`getMonth()`/`getDate()` off the `Date` object directly, no UTC conversion at all) is the only safe pattern for "what calendar date is this" anywhere in this codebase — `toISOString()` is fine for timestamps/comparisons, never for extracting a local Y-M-D string. `book.tsx`'s own `const TODAY = new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10)` has the identical latent bug and hasn't been touched — flag it if you're ever in that file for a related reason, but don't fix it as an unrelated drive-by change without the user asking.
61. **`PENDING_WINDOW_DAYS` (mobile) and `BUS_TRIP_GENERATION_WINDOW_DAYS` (`config/policy.php`) must always be changed together, to the same value** — currently **60** (raised from an initial 30). If they drift, the Pending Assignment date picker will either let an operator select a date nothing was ever generated for (picker's cap larger than the server's) or stop short of a date that actually has trips waiting (picker's cap smaller).
62. **Every Bus trip card must keep `tripCard`'s own `marginBottom` and `tripActions`' own `flexWrap: "wrap"`** — removing either reintroduces the original card-overlap bug: without `marginBottom`, a card nested inside a date/status group `View` (which has no gap of its own) renders flush against the next card with zero spacing; without `flexWrap`, 3+ action buttons on a narrow screen overflow past the card's right edge instead of dropping to a second line. Never give `tripCard` (or any Bus trip card) a fixed `height` — it must keep auto-sizing to its content so a wrapped button row grows the card correctly with no extra logic. New action buttons must be added through the shared `TripCard` component (module scope, `(operator)/trips.tsx`), not by re-inlining card JSX elsewhere — three drifting inline copies of this same card is the exact problem `TripCard` was extracted to fix (see Core Flow 12a point 8), and the lifecycle-button-per-status rules (rule below) only stay correct in one place if there's only one place.
63. **A Bus trip's available action buttons are strictly gated by its own `status`, never shown speculatively**: `pending_assignment` → Assign Trip only; `scheduled` ("Ready/Open") → Manifest, Start Boarding, Cancel (no Depart — that only appears once boarding has actually started); `boarding` → Manifest, Depart (no Cancel — cancelling before boarding starts is the Ready/Open action, not this one); `departed` → Manifest, Mark Arrived; `completed`/`cancelled` → Manifest only, read-only. Don't reintroduce a combined condition like `status === "scheduled" || status === "boarding"` for Depart or Cancel — each button belongs to exactly one status now, matching `BUS_STATUS_FILTERS`' own one-status-per-tab split (rule below).
64. **Every Bus status filter tab maps to exactly one real `trips.status` value — never combine two statuses into one tab again**: `matchesBusStatusFilter()` used to fold `departed` into the `boarding` tab; a trip must never appear in more than one tab simultaneously, and `BUS_STATUS_FILTERS` (which now includes a standalone `Departed` tab) plus the exact-match filter function are what guarantee that. The "All" tab is the one exception by design — it deliberately shows every status at once, but as `groupTripsByStatus()`-sectioned groups (each trip still appears in exactly one section), never as a flat mixed list again, and never including individual Pending Assignment cards (a count-only summary banner links to the real, date-scoped Pending Assignment tab instead) — see Do Not Break rule #59 for why bulk-loading pending trips anywhere is off the table.
26. **Seat Layout Library templates are named and explicitly applied, never silently auto-picked** (superseded from an earlier "silent reuse by `(operator_id, service_type_id, capacity)` key" design — that model still exists *only* as `seed_seats.php`'s own internal dedup for its own auto-generated rows, kept for the "Quick Generate" shortcut, but it's no longer the primary way an operator gets a layout). `vehicles/apply_seat_template.php` always creates an **independent copy** in the target vehicle's `seats` — never a live reference back to `bus_layout_templates`/`bus_layout_template_seats`. Editing a vehicle's applied layout, or deleting the template it came from, must never affect the template or any other vehicle that applied it. Don't reintroduce a "just reuse whatever matches" auto-apply path for the Library — the operator always explicitly picks.
27. **Bus/UV remittance is fully automatic — never reintroduce a submission/approval step**: driver, operator, and system all credit instantly in the same transaction via `creditRemittanceSplit()`, at boarding scan, cancellation forfeiture, no-show sweep, or (as of 2026-08-12) a completed rebooking's 10% fee. An earlier design routed the operator's and system's cut through a manual submit-then-admin-review flow (`wallet/submit_remittance.php` + `system/review_remittance.php`) — this was **built and then reverted** because it contradicted the actual requirement (deduction is a percentage of the fare transaction itself, not gated by a separate operator/admin action). Don't rebuild it without the same conversation that reverted it.
28. **Refund tiers are per-vehicle-type, never a single shared schedule**: `REFUND_TIERS` in `config/policy.php` is a map keyed by `vehicle_category` (Bus: 72h threshold; UV Express: 24h threshold), each with exactly two entries — 100% at/above the threshold, 50% below it — `computeRefundOutcome()` (`config/refund.php`) must key off the reservation's own `vehicle_category`. Collapsing this back to one shared schedule breaks the explicit "Bus needs more lead time" requirement. There is no grace-period override anymore (removed 2026-08-12 along with the old 0% tier) — don't reintroduce one without the same conversation that removed it.
32. **The system's remittance cut is a flat 5% that the operator cannot override**: `remittance_rates`'s `default` row is the only source `creditRemittanceSplit()` reads for `system_fee_pct` — there is no more per-operator `operator_override` row for the *system's* cut (that concept existed briefly and was removed). Only the *operator's own* cut (`operators.remittance_mode`/`operator_fee_pct`) is operator-configurable, and it's additive on top of the system's 5%, never a replacement for it.
33. **Subscription suspension gates queue-joining, never login**: `queue/join_queue.php`'s overdue check must never be copied into `login_mobile.php` or any other login path — an overdue driver needs to be able to log in and reach their Wallet tab to pay, or the suspension becomes an unrecoverable dead end. **`(driver)/dashboard.tsx`'s `handleStartQueue` must check `data.success` before showing "queued"** — found and fixed 2026-08-10: it previously ignored the response body entirely and always flipped `queueStatus` to `"queued"`, so an overdue Jeepney driver rejected server-side (no `dispatch_queue` row created) saw the UI claim they'd joined the queue anyway, with the rejection message never shown. Every other queue/trip action handler in this file (`handleTripAction`, `handleSlotAction`) already checked `.success` and `Alert.alert`'d on failure — this was the one that didn't. **`trips/end_trip.php` must check `affected_rows` before reporting success, same reasoning** — found and fixed the same day (root-caused live: this is what actually made "Today's Summary" trip count stay at 0 after End Trip in the reported case): it previously always returned `{"success": true}` even when its `dispatch_queue` UPDATE matched zero rows (driver was never actually dispatched — most often because an earlier `handleStartQueue` call had already silently failed per the bug above, or the driver double-tapped End Trip). `handleEndTrip` now checks the response and `Alert.alert`'s on failure, matching the same pattern. Verified live: a genuine no-op End Trip now correctly reports "No active trip found to end." instead of silently succeeding, and a real join→start→end cycle still correctly reports success and makes `driver_summary.php`'s trip count become 1.
34. **A Bus trip's boarding/departure time always comes from its `bus_schedules` template, never typed directly by the operator**: `bus_trips/create_trip.php` computes `boarding_time`/`departure_time` by concatenating the given `date` with the schedule's stored `HH:MM` fields — don't add a raw time input back to the operator's trip-scheduling form; that authority belongs to the association's schedule (Core Flow 12a).
29. **A settled fare and a later rebooking are two separate transactions, never one reversal**: whenever a reservation's fare has already been settled — either by `sweep_departed_trips.php` marking it `no_show` (immediate full remittance payout), or by `complete_rebook.php` force-settling a still-`reserved` booking as a forfeiture when the commuter proactively rebooks away from it (Core Flow 9.4) — that payout is never undone. The new reservation `complete_rebook.php` creates must be `payment_status='released'` (pre-settled, no re-charge, original fare carried forward) and `board_reservation.php` must skip `creditRemittanceSplit()` for any reservation whose `payment_status` is already `released` when scanned — otherwise the same fare's value pays out twice. This double-payment guard is load-bearing; don't remove the `payment_status === 'released'` check in `board_reservation.php`. Symmetrically, `complete_rebook.php`'s own old-reservation settlement must never re-run `creditRemittanceSplit()` for a booking that's already `no_show` (already paid by the sweep) — only the still-`reserved` branch settles anything. **This is separate from the 10% rebooking fee's own `creditRemittanceSplit()` call** (Core Flow 9 point 4) — the fee is a genuinely new payment (the commuter is paying it for the first time, right now), so splitting it is not a double payment; it's recorded against the *new* reservation's audit columns specifically so it can never be confused with, or overwrite, the *old* reservation's own original-fare settlement above.
30. **Driver-operator remittance target is optional and universal, not Jeepney-gated**: `(operator)/quota.tsx`'s "Targets" tab must stay visible for every operator type. Never hide it behind an `operator_type === 'Jeepney Op'` check — that was tried and explicitly reverted.
30a. **The remittance target (`driver_quotas`, "Targets" tab) and the booking-capacity cap (`driver_route_quotas`, backend-only since 2026-09-20 — no mobile UI calls it anymore) are two separate features that must never be merged or cross-read** (Core Flow 12b) — a remittance target must never gate a driver's ability to accept bookings, and a booking-capacity cap must never be treated as a peso amount. If you're touching one, verify you're editing the right table (`driver_quotas` vs `driver_route_quotas`). Don't reintroduce a mobile UI for `driver_route_quotas` without the same conversation that removed it — it was explicitly asked to be removed, not merely deprioritized.
31. **Jeepney subscription revenue is separate from and must never be mixed into Bus/UV remittance or Jeepney quota**: it's a distinct recurring driver→system payment (`subscription_payments`, `reference_type='subscription'`), unrelated to the operator's cut. Don't route subscription money through `operators.wallet_balance` — it goes straight to the system ledger only.
35. **Discount-override decline/grant is a real wallet debit/credit, not just a flag flip**: `board_reservation.php`'s `discount_decision` handling must reconstruct `full_fare`/`discounted_fare` from `reservations.base_fare_amount` (never from the already-rounded `fare_amount` — that's lossy), actually move money (`discount_surcharge` debit or `discount_cashback` credit), and update `reservations.discount_type`/`fare_amount` to the corrected values *before* `creditRemittanceSplit()` runs, so the driver/operator/system split is computed on the real settled amount. Never allow a `discount_decision` other than `"keep"` on a `payment_status === 'released'` ticket — that would reopen the no-show/rebook double-payment guard (rule 29).
72. **A confirmed-under-60 Senior Citizen age is a hard block, never a soft admin-review flag** — `classifySwitchVerification()` (`config/discount_switch_validation.php`) sets `blocked`, not `rejected`, when `checkSeniorAge()` finds the birthdate on the OCR'd document AND computes age < 60. This is a deliberate exception to this pipeline's otherwise-uniform "inconclusive/confident-but-non-impersonation findings fall through to the admin queue" pattern (Core Flow 2a) — discount-eligibility age is a strict requirement, not a judgment call. Don't soften this back to `rejected`/`uncertain` without the same conversation; an *unconfirmable* birthdate (not found on the document at all) correctly stays `uncertain` and must keep falling through to review — only a *confirmed* under-60 reading is the hard block.
73. **Senior Citizen and PWD ID numbers share one uniqueness namespace** (`commuter_id_verifications.id_number`, no category scoping) — enforced identically by `commuter_switch_category.php` and `commuter_register_senior.php` via `WHERE id_number = ? [AND commuter_id != ?]`. Don't silently scope this to `category='elderly'` (or `'disabled'`) only in one endpoint without updating the other — they must stay in sync, and this was a deliberate product decision (see Core Flow 2a), not an oversight.
74. **A confirmed-expired PWD Validity Date is a hard block, never a soft admin-review flag** — mirrors rule #72's Senior age rule exactly. `classifySwitchVerification()` sets `blocked`, not `rejected`, when `checkPwdValidity()` finds the typed Validity Date on the OCR'd document AND that date is already in the past. An *unconfirmable* Validity Date (not found on the document at all) correctly stays `uncertain` and falls through to admin review — only a *confirmed*-expired reading is the hard block. Relatedly: **PWD's `expires_at` write (`"{validity_date} 23:59:59"`) and every later `expires_at < NOW()` read of it must stay same-day-inclusive** — the validity date itself is still valid, only the calendar day after it is expired. Every `expires_at` read site (`login_mobile.php`'s `pwdVerificationExpired()`, `get_profile.php`'s `disabled`-category block, `commuter_switch_category.php`'s "current category expired" gate) already compares in MySQL (`expires_at < NOW()`), never PHP — keep it that way, per the PHP/MySQL clock mismatch landmine under Critical Insights. Don't merge `pwdVerificationExpired()`/`pwd_verification_expired` into `studentVerificationExpired()`/`student_verification_expired` — that field/flag name is read by `home.tsx`'s Student-specific expired banner and must keep meaning "Student" only; PWD's own expiry check is a deliberately separate, parallel function/field (see Core Flow 2a's "PWD renewability").
75. **A toast/banner rendered in a screen's main view tree is invisible whenever a `<Modal>` on that same screen is open** — `<Modal>` renders into its own separate native root, stacked on top of everything else, so a sibling element positioned in the underlying screen (however high its `zIndex`) never shows through it. Found live in `(association)/fare.tsx` 2026-08-10: every `showToast()` call (save confirmations, validation errors like "Enter a valid fare amount.") rendered into a toast at the screen root, so it was silently invisible any time any of that screen's 3 modals was open — a mistyped or empty fare input produced zero visible feedback, looking exactly like a broken Save button. Fixed by extracting a small `ToastBanner({ toast })` component and rendering it both at the screen root *and* inside each modal's own `<View style={s.overlay}>` (which is `flex:1`, so the toast's existing `position:"absolute", top:100` positions correctly above the bottom-anchored sheet). **Any screen with both a toast/snackbar pattern and one or more modals must render the toast inside each modal's own view tree, not only at the screen root** — this is a real, recurring RN gotcha, not specific to this one screen; check for it before assuming a "the button doesn't work" report is actually about the button.
76. **Operator withdrawals complete instantly, with no association/admin approval step — don't reintroduce one** (`wallet/operator_withdraw.php`, rewritten 2026-09-20 on request). The row is inserted as `status='paid'`, `resolved_at=NOW()` in the same transaction that debits the wallet, never `'pending'`. This is deliberately asymmetric with the driver-side equivalent (`drivers/driver_withdraw.php`), which still inserts `'pending'` and has no admin UI to resolve it either — that gap was never asked to be closed and stays as documented in Known Issues #11; don't "fix" it by copying this same instant-completion change over to the driver flow without the same conversation that asked for the operator one.

---

# Safe Refactor Opportunities

1. **Centralize CORS headers** — Extract repeated `header("Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *")` blocks into a shared `cors.php` include.
2. **Shared fare calculation** — Extract the fare formula from `end_ride.php` into `config/fare.php` — it will need to be reused if fare preview is added.
3. **Geocoding abstraction** — Move Nominatim call in `route_planner.php` to a standalone `geocode.php` utility (also useful for terminal location lookup).
4. **Pagination** — Add `LIMIT/OFFSET` to `wallet.php` transactions and admin user lists.
5. **Delete legacy per-driver association code** — Once confirmed nothing references them: `association_applications`/`association_memberships` tables, `applications/*.php`, `associations/kick_member.php`, `associations/leave_association.php` (see Known Issues).
6. **Wire up the unused session-token system** — Either commit to `requireAuth()` across sensitive endpoints and have the mobile client store/send the Bearer token, or remove the dead `sessions`/`config/auth.php` machinery — right now it's half-built and misleading.


## Notes
1. Do not make any changes until you have 95% confidence in what you need to build. Ask me follow-up questions until you reach that confidence.

2. Use subagents for any exploration or research. If a task needs 3+ files or multi-file analysis, spawn a subagent and return only summarized insights.

## Booking (Bus / UV Express)
- [x] Operator schedules Bus trips ahead of time (route/vehicle/driver/departure); commuters only ever see buses with an actual scheduled trip

- [x] UV Express live "go online" session booking (walk-up, driver-initiated) — **removed**: booking availability is entirely operator/association-controlled (route Active/Not Active toggle + published operating hours); the driver has no availability action of their own and boards commuters only once dispatched (see Core Flow 8b point 8)
- [x] UV Express book-ahead scheduling is association-template + operator-activation, not operator-manual slot creation (see Core Flow 8b) — the association publishes a recurring schedule (route + operating hours + daily/specific-days recurrence), any number of operators independently activate it for their own fleet, and a commuter's own chosen date/time creates (or joins) the actual pooled, uncapped, no-single-owner booking slot; 24h+ advance window unchanged
- [x] Commuter's "Pick Your Own Time" date/time steppers are clamped client-side to the association's actual operating window and recurrence (can't scroll to an out-of-range time/day at all, mirroring `resolve_slot.php`'s own bounds exactly) instead of only rejecting on submit
- [x] A pooled UV slot's commuter-facing visibility (`get_available_slots.php`) tracks operator/association activation live — deactivating stops it from being listed/bookable immediately, no duplicate schedule or manual step needed; a legacy operator-owned slot is exempt (see Core Flow 8b point 7)
- [x] UV slot boarding QR stays inactive until 30 min before departure (anti-line-skipping)
- [x] UV slot rollover, now pooled across every operator active on the route — whichever driver from whichever active operator is currently dispatched for that route can board a slot's passengers, not just one fixed owning operator
- [x] Commuter can re-view a past booking's QR/ticket from "My Upcoming Bookings"
- [x] Minimum advance-booking window (24h+, both Bus and UV Express slots), enforced server-side and reflected in listing endpoints
- [x] Commuter refund — two-tier percentage schedule (rewritten 2026-08-11, simplified 2026-08-12), per vehicle type: Bus 100% at 72h+/50% below, UV Express 100% at 24h+/50% below — no 0% tier, no post-booking grace period, no cancellation is ever refused a refund entirely. Whatever isn't refunded (the other 50%) is forfeited to the driver/operator/system via the normal remittance split, settled by the single `request_refund.php` endpoint (`cancel_late.php` deprecated in place) — no commuter self-service rebooking anymore
- [x] Rebooking — rewritten 2026-08-10 into a 2-step, operator-approved exception (see Core Flow 9.4). A commuter can request a rebook proactively (still `reserved`, before departure, no cutoff) or reactively (`no_show`, within a 24h grace window) — not only after a no-show like the original model. The operator approves/rejects only (`operator_review_rebook.php`); the commuter then picks their own new trip/seat (same vehicle type, same operator's fleet) and pays the 10% (of the original) fee themselves (`complete_rebook.php`, reached via `(main)/select-rebook-trip.tsx`) — the operator no longer picks the new trip on the commuter's behalf. The original fare is never refunded; if the old booking is still `reserved` at completion it's force-settled as a forfeiture (same remittance split as a cancellation), if already `no_show` it's simply linked (no double payment, matching the old model's behavior for its one reachable case). The new ticket carries the original fare forward, is pre-settled (`payment_status='released'`), and is clearly marked as rebooked (`rebooked_from_reservation_id`) — the driver scanner shows a "REBOOKED PASSENGER" breakdown, never re-charging or re-crediting the original fare. The 10% fee itself is split system/operator/driver (as of 2026-08-12), not credited to the operator alone
- [x] Student/disabled/elderly discount capped to once per commuter per trip/slot (no stacking across repeat bookings)
- [x] Payment escrow state machine (`payment_status`: held → released on boarding or pre-settled rebooking, held → refunded on refund, held → forfeited via grace-period sweep or late cancellation — "forfeited" still pays the driver/operator/system, just not the commuter)
- [x] Driver/commuter-facing boarding & departure controls + QR grace period after departure, per vehicle type (Bus 20 min, UV Express 30 min)
- [x] Driver/operator/system remittance (Bus/UV: system flat 5%, operator chooses quota-settlement or their own percentage cut, driver keeps the remainder) — fully automatic, instant, at boarding scan / late cancellation / no-show sweep alike, no submission or approval step (a manual submit-then-admin-review design was built and then reverted — see Do Not Break #27)
- [x] Per-operator Bus/UV remittance mode toggle (`(operator)/quota.tsx` "Your Cut Per Booking" card) — quota (settle with drivers manually, 0% auto-cut) or percentage (operator sets their own rate, auto-deducted alongside the system's flat 5%)
- [x] Bus scheduling is two-tier: association publishes a recurring schedule template (route + class + boarding/departure time + daily/weekly recurrence, `(association)/schedules.tsx`, with full **Edit** support in addition to create/delete/status-toggle); operator commits a vehicle + driver + date against it to create an actual bookable trip (`(operator)/trips.tsx`)
- [x] **Rewritten 2026-08-02 to a three-tier model** (Core Flow 12a) — the System now auto-generates date-specific `pending_assignment` trip instances from every Active schedule for a rolling 60-day window (configurable, `config/trip_generation.php`, no cron — lazy/opportunistic like the rest of this app; raised from an initial 30 on request, see Do Not Break rule #61), eliminating duplicate manual trip creation; the operator's job becomes **assigning** a vehicle + its own driver + optional conductor to an already-generated trip (`bus_trips/assign_trip.php`, "Assign Trip" button per trip card, replacing the old "+ Add" button and its schedule-picker/date-preview flow) instead of creating one from scratch; only fully-assigned, schedule-active, `scheduled`-status, non-cancelled trips are ever commuter-visible. Same-vehicle/same-driver double-booking is still blocked at assignment time with a clear validation message (unchanged logic, now checked against the trip's own fixed date instead of a computed one). The old `create_trip.php` one-at-a-time flow is left in place, dormant, for any historical caller
- [x] Bus schedule recurrence has four UI presets — Every Day / Weekdays / Weekends / Custom — layered over the existing `recurrence_type`/`weekdays` fields, no schema change (`(association)/schedules.tsx`)
- [x] Operators can assign a vehicle/driver to any already-generated trip regardless of how far out it is (days/weeks/a month ahead), not just the day of departure — a direct consequence of trips being pre-generated rather than created just-in-time
- [x] Deactivating a Bus schedule stops future trip generation and hides (never cancels/deletes) every trip already generated under it, including already-assigned ones with no bookings yet; reactivating restores visibility instantly with zero data mutated either way — verified live
- [x] `(operator)/trips.tsx`'s Bus trip list groups by date (soonest first) and orders each date's trips by status (Ready/Open → Boarding → Departed → Completed → Cancelled); status filter chips gained a "Pending Assignment" option
- [x] Pending Assignment is date-scoped (rewritten 2026-08-02) — operators pick a specific date via prev/next arrows or a horizontal scrollable date strip covering the full window (capped at today through the 60-day generation horizon) and see only that date's still-unclaimed generated trips, sorted chronologically, instead of every pending trip across every schedule loading at once (`bus_trips/get_pending_trips.php`, new, replaces the bulk pending-trip inclusion `get_operator_trips.php` had briefly gained)
- [x] Unassigned trips whose calendar date has fully passed are automatically swept to `cancelled` (reusing the existing terminal status, no new one added) and stop appearing in Pending Assignment — operators only ever see today's and future generated trips; future generation from Active schedules continues unaffected
- [x] Bus Trips page UI/UX/layout polish pass (2026-08-02, presentational only — no workflow or business-logic change) — fixed the card-overlap/button-overflow bug (`tripCard` missing `marginBottom`, `tripActions` missing `flexWrap`); extracted a shared `TripCard` component (was three drifting inline copies); action buttons now strictly gated per lifecycle status (Ready/Open no longer shows Depart, Boarding no longer shows Cancel); added a standalone **Departed** status tab (previously folded into "Boarding"); the **All** tab is now a status-grouped operational dashboard (Ready/Open → Boarding → Departed → Completed → Cancelled sections, each date/time-sorted) with a tappable Pending Assignment count banner instead of inline pending cards; unified header/tab spacing and chip heights; route name is now the card's largest text with a clearer info-row hierarchy below it
- [x] Bus trip assignment has no vehicle/driver scheduling-conflict check (2026-08-03) — tried twice: first a same-calendar-date block, then a time-overlap + rest-period check; both were removed on request so the operator has full control over assigning even with overlapping schedules or back-to-back trips (see Do Not Break rule #41)
- [x] Cancelling a scheduled (not-yet-boarding) Bus trip returns it to Pending Assignment instead of deleting it or terminally cancelling it — still-reserved passengers are refunded first, then vehicle/driver/conductor/capacity reset to unassigned so any approved operator can claim it without a duplicate schedule/trip being generated; cancelling once boarding has started still terminally cancels (Do Not Break rule #69)
- [x] Operators can reassign the vehicle/driver/conductor on an already-`scheduled` trip without it reverting to Pending Assignment (`bus_trips/reassign_trip.php`, "Reassign" button on Ready/Open trip cards) — blocks a vehicle change specifically if the trip already has real bookings (seat_labels would orphan against a different seat map), driver/conductor swap always allowed; `departure_time` stays immutable either way (Do Not Break rule #66)
- [x] UV Express operating-hours windows support wrapping past midnight (e.g. 23:00 → 02:00 the next day) — validation across `uv_operating_hours.php` (POST/PUT), `resolve_slot.php`'s bounds check, `book.tsx`'s time-stepper clamping, and `schedules.tsx`'s client-side check all treat `start_time > end_time` as a valid overnight window instead of rejecting it (Do Not Break rule #54a)
- [x] Commuter Upcoming Bookings auto-expires into Booking History without a manual poll — `get_commuter_reservations.php` now runs the shared `sweepDepartedReservations()` opportunistically on every call (both scopes), same function already used by `get_reservation_status.php`/`sweep_departed_trips.php`
- [x] Operator dashboard's "Vehicle Types" card de-duplicates by vehicle_type (approved beats pending beats other) — fixes a duplicate React key when an operator holds several applications for the same type across different associations
- [x] Available Trips (Bus/UV browse cards) and Trip Details cards decluttered — removed additive double-spacing (gap + per-row margin stacking that read as uneven whitespace), fare/seats now share one balanced left/right row on both card types, vehicle/driver info given its own row on the detail card
- [x] Every icon-only button across the app carries an `accessibilityLabel` (~20 files) — buttons that already show a visible text label were left unchanged (Do Not Break rule #67, superseded by the next item)
- [x] Button labeling audit — every primary icon-only action button (Edit/Delete/Close/Add/View/Back/Save/Remove/Center-on-my-location/etc.) across Commuter, Driver, Operator, Association, and Admin now shows a real visible text label next to its icon, not just an `accessibilityLabel` — 53 controls across 20 files (Core Flow 17). A small, explicitly-confirmed category of embedded input micro-affordances (password eye, search clear-X, date/time/row/column steppers, admin sidebar hamburger, gallery prev/next) stayed icon-only as an established UI convention. Along the way, fixed a real icon/behavior mismatch (a "notifications" bell that actually opened Profile, on both Home and Operator Dashboard — now a profile icon + "Profile" label matching its real destination)
- [x] Bus fare display is now consistent with what's actually charged — `get_upcoming_trips.php` gained the same `fare_matrix` per-class lookup `create_reservation.php` already used to charge, so Available Trips/Trip Details/the passenger form no longer show a stale flat route fare for a class-priced Bus route (Do Not Break rule #68); the Choose Route screen no longer shows a fare at all, since a route's flat fare is meaningless before a specific class-bearing trip is picked
- [x] Dual admin+association operator verification gating enforced server-side — an association cannot approve an operator's application until the admin has verified that operator
- [x] Admin System Wallet page (`/system-wallet`) — live system balance + breakdown by source (Bus/UV remittance vs Jeepney quota), read-only reporting, plus a "Jeepney Driver Quota Rate" editor with daily/weekly/monthly frequency
- [x] Jeepney driver platform quota (display wording — schema/API still say "subscription") — separate system revenue stream from the operator's driver-quota cut, admin-tunable recurring rate (daily/weekly/monthly, calendar-aligned periods), driver pays manually like a bill
- [x] Quota non-payment suspends dispatch-queue joining (not login/account) for the current period until paid
- [x] Driver-operator manual remittance target is optional and available for every vehicle type, not Jeepney-only
- [x] Driver Quotas rewritten as a Vehicle Type → Route → Driver remittance-target hierarchy (`(operator)/quota.tsx`'s "Targets" tab, 2026-09-20) — explicitly never a booking cap (a driver who's met/exceeded their target keeps accepting bookings unchanged); vehicle-type chips are always visible, locked (not hidden) when unapproved; an "Unassigned Drivers" bucket covers drivers with a vehicle but no route yet; targets are editable/removable (`quota/get_remittance_targets.php`, `quota/remove_quota.php`, new), with daily/weekly auto-resetting period progress and no schema change (still one target per driver, route shown as live display context only). The pre-existing, separate booking-capacity cap feature (`driver_route_quotas`) briefly got its own "Limits" tab on this screen, then was removed from the mobile UI entirely the same day at the user's request — its backend/enforcement stays fully live, just unreachable from the app now — see Core Flow 12b
- [x] Bus classes (Ordinary/Deluxe/Royal Class) + per-route per-class fares are association-owned (`associations/service_types.php`, `set_service_fare.php`) — operators only assign a class to a vehicle and build its seat layout, never create classes/routes/fares themselves
- [x] Bus seat layout templates — seater or sleeper, chosen once per (operator, class, seat count) and reused automatically for later vehicles matching that combination (`bus_layout_templates`, `seed_seats.php`)
- [x] Vehicle seat-count rules are type-specific: Jeepney 12–36 (even), UV Express 9–18 (any, includes driver's seat), Bus has no upfront capacity (set via seat map generation)
- [x] LTFRB Approved Fare Guide removed from both operator and association fare screens (all three vehicle kinds)
- [ ] LTFRB-mandated fare structures beyond a flat per-class base fare (e.g. distance-based tiers) — `fare_matrix` has `per_km_rate`/`base_km`/`succeeding_rate` columns but nothing reads them yet
- [x] Driver discount-override scanner (Bus/UV Express boarding only) — persistent "Discount Mode" toggle plus an accept/decline prompt on an already-discounted ticket; declining charges a surcharge, granting (physical ID shown, commuter unverified in-system) credits a cashback, both computed from `reservations.base_fare_amount` and settled before the remittance split runs
- [x] Commuter Bus seat picker renders the vehicle's actual saved layout (positioned by row/col) with a 5-color state (Green=Available, Red=Booked, Blue=Selected, Yellow=Reserved/Saved i.e. operator-blocked, Gray=Driver/Conductor), sourced live from the vehicle's `seats` table
- [x] Operator-added amenities/details blurb per Bus class (e.g. "Royal Class — 180-degree fully reclining bunk seats...") — an overlay the operator controls, never a write to the association-owned class itself; shown to the commuter on the trip card/detail
- [x] Operator-facing Bus trip lifecycle controls (Start Boarding / Depart / Mark Arrived) on `(operator)/trips.tsx`, mirroring the driver dashboard's buttons; same-date vehicle/driver double-booking guard at trip creation
- [x] UV Express operator-facing "Route Queue" panel + manual queue-advance control, layered on the existing route-scoped `dispatch_queue` (doesn't replace driver self-service)
- [x] Bus trip scheduling asks only for a schedule + vehicle + driver — the next valid occurrence date is computed server-side (recurrence + 24h advance window + vehicle/driver conflict check), with a live preview before the operator confirms; no manual date picker, no capacity override (capacity always comes from the vehicle's own seat layout)
- [x] Bus vehicle picker in trip assignment shows every active, correctly-classed bus that has both a built seat layout and an assigned driver — the driver always comes from the vehicle's own fleet-level assignment (Vehicles tab), never picked separately at trip-creation time
- [x] Operator's per-class "Add/Edit Details" editor is reachable for every class the operator is approved for, independent of whether a schedule has been published for that class yet
- [x] Seat Layout Builder shows a persistent "unsaved changes" banner whenever a generated or hand-edited draft hasn't been saved to the vehicle yet
- [x] Generated seat layouts include a real, non-bookable "aisle path" placeholder between the seat pairs (standard buses) so the center aisle is visibly represented, not just an unlabeled gap — never counted toward capacity, never selectable by a commuter
- [x] Seat Layout Builder has a "5-Seat End Bench" action to apply the back-row bench pattern on demand to whichever row is currently last, independent of the auto-generator
- [x] Seat Layout Generator — realistic default layouts from a vehicle type + target capacity: 2×2+aisle with an odd-count 5-seat bench row for standard buses, stacked individually-bookable Lower/Upper berths for sleeper buses, Driver/Conductor seats always included and non-bookable; purely a draft the operator reviews/edits before saving, not an automatic commit
- [x] Bus vehicle registration chains straight into the Seat Layout Builder for the new vehicle (generate a default layout or pick from the Library) instead of leaving it to be configured later
- [x] Changing a Bus vehicle's class is blocked while it has a seat layout — must be cleared first, keeping class and layout/capacity always consistent
- [x] Commuter booking sync (My Upcoming Bookings / Recent Activity / Booking History) — a shared `BookingsContext` (see Core Flow 9a) replaces three independently-fetched, driftable copies of a commuter's reservations; a new booking appears instantly (optimistic local update) without a "Book Another Trip" tap or app restart, cancellation drops it from Upcoming instantly, and every screen refetches on tab focus **plus an 18s poll while that screen stays open** (Home, Book, and Booking History all now match this cadence — Core Flow 9a point 10 — so a status change like grace-period expiry or boarding starting shows up without a tab switch). Home's "Recent Activity" (previously a hardcoded, always-empty placeholder) is now wired to a client-derived activity feed (Booking Confirmed / Booking Cancelled / Trip Completed, newest first); a new `booking-history.tsx` screen (reachable from Profile → "My Bookings", previously a dead stub) lists every booking regardless of status, capped at 100 (no pagination anywhere in this app yet). A single unified status vocabulary — Upcoming/Boarding/Completed/Cancelled/Refunded/Rebooked/Expired, derived client-side via `getDisplayStatus()` from `boarding_status`+`cancellation_type`+the trip/slot's own `vehicle_status` (Core Flow 9a point 9) — is shown consistently across Upcoming Bookings, Booking History, and Booking Details
- [x] `reservations.resolved_at` (migration 033) timestamps every place a booking leaves the active/reserved state (refund, late cancel, no-show sweep, trip/slot completion, operator-cancel) — powers the Recent Activity/History ordering above
- [x] Passenger Info screen expanded — fuller Booking Summary (icons beside route/vehicle/seat/terminal/departure/est. arrival/travel time, seat number(s) highlighted in the same blue as the seat map), optional email/emergency-contact fields, a fully automatic discount (no manual Passenger Type selector at all — removed after an interim "informational only" version, see Do Not Break rule #70), a real Fare Breakdown preview (Base Fare/Verified Discount/Booking Fee/Total, mirroring the server's own math), a Payment Method Preview (real wallet balance + top-up link), a Booking Reminder card, a required Terms & Conditions checkbox (gates Confirm Booking, but opening either policy sheet is optional) gating Confirm Booking, a sticky Confirm Booking button, and a pre-submit confirmation dialog (now including the applied verified discount, if any) to prevent accidental bookings
- [x] Terms & Conditions and Cancellation Policy are two separate, independently-opened policy sheets (`PolicySheet`, `PassengerForm.tsx`) reached via two hyperlink-styled blue (`#2563eb`) `Text` links side by side in one row at the bottom of the Passenger Info screen, gray label text around them — Cancellation Policy's copy accurately states the real per-vehicle-type refund cutoff (72h Bus/24h UV Express), that a late-but-before-departure cancellation still goes through but forfeits the fare rather than being blocked outright, that any approved refund lands in the commuter's wallet, that travelling again after cancelling means a fresh standard-fare booking (the 10% fee is only ever for an operator's own no-show rebooking, Core Flow 9.4), and that cancellation ends once boarded/departed
- [x] A trip's Estimated Arrival/Travel Time on the Passenger Info screen is a real (if rough) server-computed estimate — `get_upcoming_trips.php`/`get_available_slots.php` return `estimated_duration_minutes` via a shared Haversine-over-waypoints ÷ flat-speed helper (`routes/geo_helpers.php`, same method+constants as the route planner, Do Not Break rule #71) — never a fabricated number, `null` when a route has no usable waypoints
- [x] Booking Confirmation (success) screen enlarged its total-paid display, added Seat/Vehicle No./Booking ID detail rows, and gained a working "Download Ticket" action (generates a real PDF with an embedded QR image via `expo-print`, opens the native share sheet via `expo-sharing`) alongside the existing Book Another Trip / Back to Home / View My Bookings actions
- [x] Every booking card in "My Upcoming Bookings" and Booking History is tappable, opening a shared Booking Details screen (Booking ID, Route, Vehicle Number/Type, Seat(s), Departure, Est. Arrival, Terminal, Passenger Info, Fare Breakdown, Payment Details, Booking Status, and the commuter's real boarding QR — the same one the driver scans) with Cancel/Request Rebook shown only when eligible (Core Flow 9a point 8)
- [x] Commuter-initiated "Request Rebook" (proactive or reactive, see the rebooking bullet above) — since this app has no notification system, the request only ever surfaces as a badge + Approve/Reject on the operator's own trip/slot manifest, moving to "Select New Trip" on the commuter's own Booking Details screen once approved (`rebook_requests` table, migration 035, widened 047)
- [x] Verified student/senior/PWD discount eligibility re-confirmed correct and left unchanged — still scoped to the exact same trip/slot a commuter already used it on (not a broader per-vehicle-type cap), enforced entirely server-side from the commuter's own admin-verified profile; the Booking Details screen's Fare Breakdown just displays it accurately (₱0 discount when not applicable)

## Operator Features
- [x] Manage drivers (add, edit, delete) — operator sets the driver's login credentials directly and shares them out-of-band
- [x] Edit an existing driver's details (name/email/phone/license/password/assigned vehicle)
- [x] Bus/UV Express driver dashboard shows a time-aware boarding/departure hint (never hard-disables the buttons — manual override always available)
- [x] Vehicle Management module (all 3 types) — vehicle number, plate number, type, seating capacity, status, assigned route(s) (read-through of the driver's assignment), add/edit/deactivate; open 3-way vehicle-type picker, each type locked unless approved for that type; type-specific capacity rules; Bus vehicles pick a class + build a seat layout instead of a raw capacity
- [x] Bus-only Seat Layout Builder — add/move/remove/rename individual seats incl. designated Driver/Conductor seats and an operator-blocked "Reserved/Saved" toggle; a seat with an active booking can't be deleted (only repositioned/retyped)
- [x] Seat Layout Library — save a completed layout as a named, reusable template; applying a template to another vehicle creates an independent copy (editing one never affects the template or other vehicles that applied it)
- [x] View routes and assign one (of the matching vehicle type, from an approved association) to a driver — routes themselves are association-only for all three vehicle types now, operator can no longer create/edit one, only assign a published route to a driver or (Bus) schedule trips against it
- [x] One operator account can hold **multiple simultaneous vehicle-type memberships** — apply for Jeepney at one association and Bus at a different one, independently, each with its own approval status
- [x] Apply to an association per vehicle type, uploading that type's own LTO/LTFRB document checklist (4 docs Jeepney, 4 UV Express, 5 Bus); leave one vehicle-type membership without affecting others
- [x] Account "on hold" per vehicle type (feature-gated) until an association approves that specific type — same UX pattern as the existing admin-verification gate, now scoped per type instead of the whole account
- [x] Registration is generic (name/email/phone/password) but still requires one document — a valid ID or business permit, reviewed by the admin as part of account verification; no vehicle type and no vehicle-type-specific documents collected at signup, those move to the association application
- [x] Activate/deactivate an association-published UV Express schedule for the operator's own fleet (see Core Flow 8b) — no vehicle/driver commitment, replaces the old operator-manual "Schedule UV Slot" flow entirely
- [x] Driver dashboard "Mark Arrived" on a departed Bus trip (mirrors the operator's own button, calls the same `complete` transition) — surfaces the trip as completed back on the operator's Trips tab
- [x] Operator Profile & Settings (`(operator)/profile.tsx`, `settings.tsx`, `operator-verification.tsx`, Core Flow 8d) — Profile's existing multi-association logic (per-association cards, vehicle-type badges, Leave/Switch, Apply) confirmed already correct and left untouched; verification badge is now tappable into a new dedicated detail page, explicitly noting it's separate from per-association approval. Settings covers Account Information (Name/Email/Phone, Operator ID read-only), Notifications (6 inert local preference toggles — no activity-log infra exists yet to gate), Privacy & Security (Change Password — a new capability, this role had none before), and Terms & Policies (`OPERATOR_TERMS`/Privacy Policy viewers). No account-closure action anywhere — none existed to reuse, and none was invented

## Association Features
- [x] View member operators (one row per operator, showing every vehicle type approved *at this association*), each with their drivers nested underneath (transitive and filtered to those vehicle types — drivers are visible through their operator, not independently)
- [x] Approve/reject operator applications (per vehicle type, not fleet-level) — review modal shows `vehicle_type`, whichever of the 11 possible LTO/LTFRB documents are present for that type, **and** the operator's registration-time document — the association sees everything the operator has ever submitted
- [x] Every uploaded document opens in a full-screen in-app viewer (`DocumentViewerModal`, pinch-zoom/pan/double-tap-to-zoom, prev/next navigation across every document the application has, "couldn't load" placeholder for a missing/broken file) instead of kicking the reviewer out to an external browser
- [x] Create routes (the only path to a Bus route — see Operator Features)
- [x] Operator applies to an association per vehicle type, picking any active association (no longer filtered by a matching association type — associations aren't typed anymore)
- [x] An association can have operator members of **all three vehicle types simultaneously** — no more "association only accepts operators of matching type"
- [x] Remove ("kick") a member operator for one specific vehicle type — clears only that type's fleet route assignments, leaving other vehicle-type memberships with the same operator untouched
- [x] Bus associations manage a class catalog (Ordinary/Deluxe/Royal Class) and assign any subset of it, each with its own fare, to any of their routes
- [x] Fare Management always shows all three vehicle-type tabs (Jeepney/UV Express/Bus, chip-switchable) regardless of whether the association has routes of each kind yet — an association manages fares for whichever of the three types it actually operates, one route at a time
- [x] LTFRB Fare Reference — read-only official LTFRB fare guide PDFs, viewable in-app (Core Flow 13a): commuters browse all of them from Profile → "LTFRB Fare Guide"; associations see the ones matching their currently-selected vehicle-kind tab in Fare Management, always visible while configuring fares. Seeded once directly into the database, no upload/edit/delete path anywhere in the app; purely informational, never affects fare computation
- [x] Set/edit/toggle/remove an operating-hours window (start/end of operations) **and recurrence** (daily or specific days) per UV Express route, from a dedicated tab on the Schedules screen — this is the schedule operators later activate (see Core Flow 8b), not just a display window
- [x] Set/edit/toggle/remove a flat daily operating-hours window per Jeepney route, from a third "Jeepney Hours" tab on the Schedules screen — purely a display window (no recurrence, no operator activation, no booking enforcement), shown read-only to commuters (map marker tap) and drivers (Dispatch tab) (Core Flow 8g)
- [x] All schedule-related time fields (Bus boarding/departure, UV/Jeepney operating hours) use a shared 12-hour AM/PM native time picker (`TimeField`) instead of raw `HH:MM` text entry; UV/Jeepney operating hours additionally offer a one-tap "24 Hours" toggle instead of manually picking 12:00 AM/11:59 PM (Core Flow 8g)
- [x] Commuter Map's Route Suggestions cards show a Jeepney route's published Operating Hours (12-hour, overnight-wrap-aware, plus a "Currently not operating" indicator when outside the window) instead of a driver-availability "⚠ No drivers" badge — no driver names/status are shown for Jeepney suggestions anymore; Bus/UV Express suggestions are unaffected (Core Flow 8g)
- [x] Association Profile & Settings (`(association)/profile.tsx`, `settings.tsx`, `association-verification.tsx`, Core Flow 8c) — Profile's status pill now reflects real `status` (was hardcoded "Active"); Settings covers Association Information (Name/Acronym/Email/Contact Number/Address, password required only when Email/Contact Number changes, closing a real pre-existing zero-authentication gap on `update_association.php`), Notifications (8 inert local preference toggles — no activity-log infra exists yet to gate), Privacy & Security (Change Password — new capability, this role had none before), and Terms & Policies (`ASSOCIATION_TERMS`/Privacy Policy viewers). Association Verification is a single status view (Active/Pending/Inactive) — this schema has no separate representative-account-verification concept to distinguish it from. No Delete/Deactivate action anywhere; Applications/Members/Routes/Fares/Schedules remain on their own tabs, untouched
